    `STAMPABLE_STAGES`, so it has no orchestrator-run mechanical check today. Segregation of
    duties is verifiable (per-workstream `host` fields) but not mechanically enforced: an
    operator can still route reviewer and author to the same host without Stagecraft blocking
    the run, except for the adversarial-mode critic, which auto-diverges from the reviewer's
    host when ≥2 hosts are configured (31.3). No code changed; docs only.

### Added

- **`pipeline/context.md` diet: byte budget + auto-compaction, and a per-workstream context-changes delta — phase-32 item 32.5 (`plans/phase-32-performance-parallelism.md`).** Verify-first confirmed `context.md` grows via `devteam:*` marker sections written by `core/gates/validator.js` (red-team-blockers, qa-build-blockers) and `core/driver.js` (run-blockers, right-sizing, deploy-target), that `devteam compact` unconditionally strips every marker section (unchanged by this item), that every dispatched stage's `readFirst` only ever points at the whole file (never inlines it), and — new finding — that no event previously recorded a marker section write/strip, so that was added first as its own commit. New `core/context-log.js` (`logContextSectionEvent`, fire-and-forget) records every marker add/remove to `run-log.jsonl` as a `context-section-change` event. New `pipeline.context_budget_bytes` config (default `8192`) is enforced by new `core/context-budget.js` right after every marker write: over budget, the oldest RESOLVED marker section (in document order; a section is "resolved" only when this module has a rule mapping it to a stage gate whose status is PASS/WARN — currently `right-sizing`→stage-01, `deploy-target`→stage-08, `red-team-blockers`→stage-04c, `qa-build-blockers`→stage-04.qa) is replaced with a one-line digest pointing at its full text, archived append-only under `pipeline/context-archive/<ts>-<section>.md`. New `core/context-delta.js` (`computeContextDelta`) derives, purely from `run-log.jsonl` history, which sections changed since a workstream's *own* previous dispatch (correct across retries — only counts events after that workstream's most recent prior `workstream-started` event); `core/orchestrator.js`'s `plan()` computes it per workstream and threads it onto the descriptor, and a new `renderContextDelta` (`core/adapters/render-helpers.js`) renders a "## Context changes since your last dispatch" section in every host adapter's volatile-tail layer (`core/adapters/markdown-host.js`, `hosts/claude-code/adapter.js`, `hosts/generic/adapter.js`) — omitted on a workstream's first-ever dispatch or when nothing changed. `core/markers.js` gained `parseSections` (promoted out of `core/cli/commands/compact.js`, which now imports it) so both the manual compactor and the new budget auto-compactor walk the same section-parsing logic.
  - Tests: `tests/context-log.test.js` (event shape, fire-and-forget, changeId-scoping, validator/driver call sites), `tests/context-budget.test.js` (under-budget no-op, resolved-section compaction + archive round-trip, unresolved/no-rule sections survive arbitrarily over budget, multi-section compaction order, idempotent re-run, missing-file no-op), `tests/context-delta.test.js` (first-dispatch null, cross-workstream isolation, retry-sequence correctness, changeId scoping, malformed-line tolerance), `tests/render-helpers.test.js` and `tests/orchestrator.test.js` (delta section rendered/omitted end-to-end through `runStage`), `tests/config.test.js` (`context_budget_bytes` default/override/invalid-value fallback).
  - Honest scope note: event logging and budget enforcement are wired only into the writers the verify-first claim named (validator.js, driver.js) — `core/advise.js`'s `devteam:advise` section and `core/cli/commands/restart.js`'s generic strip are not instrumented, so advise-authored growth isn't tracked or auto-compacted (still fully covered by manual `devteam compact`). "Resolved" detection is a static 4-section allowlist keyed to a single gate file each; any other marker section (present or future) is always treated as active and never auto-compacted, so budget pressure from those never self-relieves. "Oldest" is document order, not a true write timestamp (none is recorded per-section), so it's an approximation for sections written via `insert: "prepend"`. `pipeline/context-archive/` was not added to ADR-010's canonical `.gitignore` block (`core/gitignore.js`) — out of scope for this item since it means editing an accepted ADR; a follow-up should add `pipeline/context-archive/` and `pipeline/changes/*/context-archive/` alongside the existing `pipeline/gates/archive/` entry.

### Added

- Added `devteam performance critical-path`, a run-log based report for dispatch wall time, merge time, retry delay, workstream compute, parallel savings, telemetry coverage, and repeated verification-command candidates.
- Persisted `dispatch-started`, `merge-started`, `merge-finished`, and retry `delay_ms` events so critical-path reports do not depend on model-authored claims.

- **Escalation guidance now ignores stale Principal outputs.** `devteam next` no longer treats any prior `PRINCIPAL-RULING:` or `PRINCIPAL-CANNOT-DECIDE:` line in `pipeline/context.md` as proof that the current escalation has been resolved. It checks whether existing Principal outputs match the active escalating gate, lets a newer matching ruling supersede an earlier cannot-decide line, and tells the operator to run `devteam ruling --target-gate ...` before `devteam fix-escalation` when nothing matches.
- **Escalation applicators can now keep documentation-only fixes.** `devteam fix-escalation` now permits README, CHANGELOG, `docs/`, and `changelog.d/` writes when a Principal ruling asks for documentation remediation, and unauthorized applicator writes fail the command instead of being removed while still reporting success.
- **Stage 8 now has a no-config local deploy default.** Projects that omit `deploy.adapter` receive a `local` adapter instead of an escalation: Stage 8 records local/in-place verification, marks `adapter_result.external_deploy: false`, and keeps concrete publish targets opt-in. If Stage 7 records `deploy_requested: false`, the orchestrator now writes the local/no-external-deploy Stage 8 gate and deploy log directly instead of dispatching a deploy agent or escalating for a deployment choice.
- **Write-audit now ignores narrow runtime caches.** Post-hoc `allowedWrites` enforcement no longer fails a stage when Python/test tooling creates interpreter cache files such as `__pycache__/*.pyc`, `.pytest_cache/`, `.mypy_cache/`, or `.ruff_cache/`; real source writes outside `allowedWrites` are still reported as violations.
- **Observability-gate repairs now route back to build workstreams.** Stage 06c blockers with `assigned_to` or file-path hints now clear the owning Stage 4 build gate, re-dispatch build with `--from observability-gate`, merge, and re-run the observability gate instead of repeatedly re-running the audit until convergence escalation.

### Added

- **Stage DAG wave execution (ADR-017, phase-32 item 32.6).** `full`-track runs paid 18 sequential stage slots even where two regions are independent. `core/pipeline/stages.js` gains a curated `dependsOn: string[]` field on exactly the two regions ADR-017 authorizes — `red-team` now depends on `build` instead of its declared-order predecessor `pre-review`, and each of `accessibility-audit` / `observability-gate` / `verification-beyond-tests` / `performance-budget` depends on `qa` — with a header comment on the STAGES table warning that any future region needs its own readFirst-vs-`dependsOn` curation pass, not a mechanical extension. **`red-team`'s `readFirst` drops `pipeline/pre-review.md` and `pipeline/security-review.md`** — a model-visible prompt change, not an incidental side effect of adding `dependsOn`: leaving them in would make `{pre-review ∥ red-team}` an unsafe pairing (see ADR-017's own design-conflict analysis).

  `core/orchestrator.js#_nextImpl`'s loop body is extracted into `evaluateStageInPipeline` (a pure per-stage readiness/action check) with zero behavior change — verified by the full pre-existing test suite before any wave code was added on top. The new `nextWave()` (exported alongside `next()`) is a thin wrapper: it runs the identical sequential scan for the first ready action, then — only when that action is a fresh/resuming dispatch and `autonomy.max_parallel_stages` (new config, default **2**, validated like `max_retries`) allows more — looks ahead for `dependsOn` stages whose named dependencies already hold PASS/WARN, regardless of declared order. A one-member result is byte-identical to `next()`.

  `core/scheduler.js` gains `waveMemberKey` (`(host, stage)` composite) so a caller batching wave members through `mapByHostConcurrency` doesn't let `routing.host_concurrency` — designed to bound workstream fan-out *within* one stage — incorrectly throttle concurrency *across* stages that happen to share a host.

  `core/driver.js`'s main loop now calls `nextWave()` and, for a real 2+ member ready set, dispatches every member concurrently (`dispatchWaveMember`/`dispatchWave`, mirroring the pre-017 run-stage/continue-stage handling exactly, just returning a control signal instead of directly break/continue-ing the loop) through `core/scheduler.js#mapByHostConcurrency` keyed by `waveMemberKey` — the same dispatcher `core/orchestrator.js` already uses for within-stage workstream fan-out, per ADR-017 §2's explicit "no second concurrency implementation" instruction — one member failing halts wave *advancement* (the driver won't form a new wave until every current member reaches a terminal state) without touching an already-passing sibling's gate. One monotonic-per-run `wave_id` is assigned per formed wave and stamped on every member's dispatch events; `state.iterations`/`--max-iterations` count one wave as one iteration. A `opts.next` single-action stub (the pre-existing test DI seam, used by ~120 call sites across the test suite) is automatically wrapped into a size-one wave when no `opts.nextWave` is given, so no existing test needed to change.

  **Fix-up (same day, post-review):** the first cut of `dispatchWave` added `waveMemberKey` but never actually routed dispatch through `mapByHostConcurrency` — it used a bare `Promise.all`, leaving `waveMemberKey` exercised only by its own unit test. Caught in review, not by CI: because a wave's members are always distinct stages by construction, `(host, stage)` keying guarantees no two members ever share a queue within one `dispatchWave` call, so `Promise.all` and `mapByHostConcurrency` are behaviorally identical for both authorized regions — `routing.host_concurrency` was never actually going to throttle cross-stage wave concurrency either way (ADR-017 §2 scopes that cap to *within* one member's own dispatch). This was a spec-literalism/dead-code gap, not a live concurrency bug; the fix wires `dispatchWave` through `mapByHostConcurrency` (host resolved per member via `resolveRoute(config, stage, role)`) so the code now matches the ADR's "no second concurrency implementation" instruction and `waveMemberKey` is exercised by real dispatch, not just its own test. Full suite green before and after, unchanged test counts (proving the no-op).

  `devteam performance critical-path` (`core/performance/critical-path.js`) gains a `waves` array and `wave_realized_savings_ms` (`sum(member durations) − max(member durations)`, grouped by `wave_id`) — additive to the existing sequential dispatch-wall-time accounting, not a correction to it.

  Docs: `docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md` gains a "Stage DAG waves" section and a `wave-formed` entry in the non-halt events list; `docs/FEATURES.md` gains a matching bullet. `plans/phase-32-performance-parallelism.md`, `plans/README.md`, and `plans/prompts/roadmap-2026-prompts.md` updated so no document still shows 32.2/32.6 as open.

  Tests: `tests/dag-wave-execution.test.js` (27 cases) — curated `dependsOn`/readFirst trim; config validation and fallback; `waveMemberKey`; wave formation (`{pre-review, red-team}` and all four QA-sweep stages becoming ready together, `max_parallel_stages` capping and splitting, a size-one wave byte-identical to `next()`, a FAIL not removing a ready sibling); driver integration (concurrent dispatch writing both gates in one iteration, a shared monotonic `wave_id`, a size-one wave carrying no `wave_id` field at all, a FAIL not corrupting the passing sibling's gate, `verify-chain` passing on a waved run); legacy `opts.next` stub compatibility; critical-path realized-savings math and markdown rendering. `tests/compact-qa-fold.test.js`'s four hard-coded pre-29.4 stage snapshots updated to include the new `dependsOn` field — deliberately, since that's new authorized behavior this item adds, not drift the snapshot should catch.

  Honest scope note: only fresh/resuming dispatches (`run-stage`/`continue-stage`) are batched into a real concurrent wave. A ready set containing a `fix-and-retry` (or any other non-dispatch action) always collapses to a single-member wave and runs through the exact pre-017 code path unchanged — concurrent convergence/archiving bookkeeping for multiple *simultaneously failing* wave members was judged materially riskier than this item's wall-clock claim requires (that claim comes from concurrent fresh dispatch, not concurrent retry), and ADR-017 §6 itself only says a wave-aware `next()` "may" return more than one `fix-and-retry` action, not that it must.

### Added

- **`devteam review <path>` — the zero-install external-review entry point (phase-36 item 36.4, `plans/phase-36-external-review-mode.md`).** No `init`, no config, nothing written to `<path>`. Creates (or reuses, via `--workspace <path>`) a review workspace under `~/.stagecraft/reviews/<slug>/` (`core/review-workspace.js`, item 36.3), then dispatches `--track` (default `review-only`) through the normal bounded driver (`core/driver.js#run()`) with `ctx.cwd` = the workspace and `ctx.processCwd` = the subject and `ctx.externalReviewMode = true` — the two-root split 36.3 wired through `runStage()`/`runStageHeadless()` but that, until this item, no caller ever set to `true`. `hosts/acp/adapter.js` reads that flag to flip its permission evaluator (`hosts/acp/permissions.js`, item 36.1) into review mode: any write into the subject is denied, and `execute` becomes a read-only allowlist. `--scope <path>` (repeatable) narrows the review the same way `devteam run --scope` already does (35.1). On completion, runs 35.4's severity-ordered findings collector/renderer against the workspace and prints the report path (`--open` launches it in a browser).

  **Host honesty.** `acp` is the only host whose enforcement is mechanical rather than advisory (`hosts/acp/permissions.js`'s two-root evaluator vs. every other host's prompt-only or post-hoc-audit `allowed_writes` — `core/adapters/render-helpers.js#allowedWritesCaption`). `--host` naming anything else prints a one-line warning that writes to the subject are **not** prevented (enforcement degrades to a post-hoc write audit) and refuses to proceed without `--allow-unenforced-writes` — the house "allow-&lt;compromised-guarantee&gt;" convention (`evidence.js`'s `--allow-unverified`), chosen so the flag itself states what's being given up, not just that the user consents. The warning still prints even when the flag is passed; this command never prints a read-only claim a host cannot keep.

  **`devteam review --list`** enumerates `core/review-workspace.js#reviewsRoot()`, pairing each workspace's `subject.json` (what's being reviewed) with a new `last-run.json` (recorded by this item, not 36.3 — run outcomes, not review-target identity) for subject path / last run date / last status. An empty list renders as "no review workspaces yet," not an error.

  **`--json`** is schema-checked against two new checked-in schemas: `core/review/schemas/review.schema.json` (single-run output: subject identity, workspace path, host/track, `review_mode_enforced`, driver completion state, findings-report summary) and `core/review/schemas/review-list.schema.json` (`--list` output).

  **The test that matters** (`tests/review-command.test.js`): end-to-end against a fixture subject repo, driven by a new scripted ACP agent (`tests/fixtures/acp-stub-review-agent.js`) that completes the *entire* three-stage `review-only` track (security-review, red-team, and all four peer-review areas) in one process invocation by parsing each dispatch's own rendered prompt for its workstream id/role, artifact path, and gate path — `tests/fixtures/acp-stub-agent.js`'s fixed-env-var approach only covers one dispatch per process, which a full `devteam review` run can't offer since the CLI's single invocation drives every stage's dispatch with the same env. Asserts the subject tree (file list + content hash, `.gitignore`/`AGENTS.md` included) is byte-identical before and after, a findings report is written, and `--list` picks up the completed run afterward. Also covers: `--list`'s empty case; the non-acp host warn-and-refuse-without-ack / proceed-with-ack paths (no workspace created on refusal); and both `--json` shapes against their schemas.

  Honest scope note: this item does not change how `Produce <artifact>` / `Write to pipeline/gates/<id>.json` are rendered (`core/adapters/render-helpers.js`) — those instructions are still *relative* paths, which a real (non-scripted) ACP agent would resolve against its own session cwd. In review mode that cwd is the subject (36.1's `processCwd`), not the workspace, so a real agent following the prompt literally would either get its write denied (safe: review-mode's codeRoot check catches it) or would need to independently infer the workspace's absolute path from context this prompt doesn't currently give it. 36.2 solved exactly this ambiguity for *read* targets (framework `readFirst` entries render absolute when roots differ); the same fix for *write* targets (artifact + gate paths) is not part of this item — flagged as an out-of-scope finding, not fixed here. The scripted test stub sidesteps it by being told the workspace's absolute path directly via `ACP_STUB_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, the same way `tests/fixtures/acp-stub-agent.js` already sidesteps path ambiguity for 36.3's own test. Also unaddressed here: `hosts/acp/adapter.js` never calls `core/hooks/approval-derivation.js#deriveForProject` the way `core/adapters/headless.js` does for non-ACP headless hosts, so a *real* ACP-routed peer-review dispatch has no automatic path from a `by-<role>.md` review file to a derived `stage-05.<role>.json` gate — this item's test stub writes that gate directly (a scripted stub, not bound by the "never authored by an agent" contract stage-05's schema describes), which is fine for testing this command's orchestration plumbing but does not exercise or fix that gap.

- **Docker-based headless runner** (closes #282). Adds `hosts/docker/` with a
  non-root Dockerfile, a small entrypoint that delegates to the normal `devteam`
  CLI, lock-state reporting for mounted projects, explicit stale-lock cleanup
  via `STAGECRAFT_RUNNER_CLEAR_STALE_LOCK=1`, and operator docs for build, run,
  resume, UID/GID alignment, secrets, host choices, and resource limits. ADR-014
  locks the trust boundary: the runner is containerized local orchestration, not
  a scheduler, cloud worker, per-stage sandbox, or new host adapter. Offline
  tests cover the packaging surface and entrypoint behavior without requiring
  Docker or model credentials.

### Changed

- **Docs: reposition `full` as the audited path, `loop` as the day-to-day default — phase-29 item 29.5.** `README.md`, `docs/tracks.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`, and `docs/adoption-guide.md` now lead with `loop` for bounded, day-to-day iteration and frame `full` as the **audited** path chosen when stakes or compliance justify the ceremony, rather than the safest-looking default. `README.md`'s Quick start (`--headless` path) now runs `--track loop` first; a new "Which track?" section adds a ceremony-cost decision table pointing at `devteam assess --json` (phase-29.3) for the actual per-track estimate. `docs/tracks.md`'s "Pick by what you're shipping" table gained a ceremony-cost column and was reordered `loop`-first/`full`-last; its decision tree gained an explicit `loop` branch. One sentence in each of `README.md`, `docs/tracks.md`, and `docs/adoption-guide.md` forward-references the Phase 34 roadmap item (exportable, regulator-shaped attestations building on the `full`-track audit trail), each marked as roadmap / not yet built. `docs/user-guide.md`'s "Daily loop" heading (the `next`/`stage` habitual cycle) now disambiguates itself from the `loop` track by name.
  Honest scope note: this is a repositioning pass, not a rewrite — every existing caveat (stoplist behavior, the `pipeline.default_track: full` factory-default note, per-track stage lists, the `nano`/`quick`/`hotfix` guidance in the adoption guide) is preserved verbatim or extended, never deleted. No code changed; `npm run consistency`'s doc-drift checks are the acceptance gate for this item.

### Added

- **Failed gates become replayable eval cases — Phase 33.1.** Every stage FAIL/ESCALATE, and every orchestrator stamp `status_overridden` (the model-lied class — always captured, since it's already a FAIL), now writes a case under `.devteam/evals/cases/<ts>-<stage>-<hash>/`: `case.json` (stage/role/host/track, an orchestrator-computed `prompt_hash`, the gate's C4 reproducibility fields via `core/reproducibility.js#reproducibilityFingerprint`, a sanitized gate snapshot, framework/stamper versions) and `inputs/manifest.json` describing the stage's `readFirst` artifact set. Artifact content is content-addressed (sha256) into `.devteam/evals/blobs/`, deduped across cases; a file that scans positive through `core/hooks/secret-scan.js` (the same path the stage-04c mechanical red-team floor uses) is excluded from the blob store entirely — the manifest records the exclusion and which pattern fired, never the content. `core/verify/stamp.js` finding lint/tests actually failing after a model claimed PASS flips the gate as before; the new capture path just observes whatever status lands.

  Single-role stages are captured in `core/orchestrator.js#runStageHeadless` (alongside the existing `core/corpus.js` fire-and-forget dispatch record — same point, so the post-stamp status is what's captured, never the model's pre-stamp claim). Multi-workstream stages (build, peer-review) are captured in `core/driver.js`'s merge branch, after `stampMerged` — that's the only place the stage's true final status (post per-role stamps *and* the merge-time stage-05 approval re-derivation) is known.

  A resolution-linker pass runs at the end of every `devteam run` (fire-and-forget, same slot as pattern auto-collection/reflector/memory-ingest): for each captured case with no `resolution.json` yet, it looks for a `fix-retry` run-log event on the same stage logged after capture, and — if the stage's current on-disk gate is no longer FAIL/ESCALATE — appends `resolution.json` naming which retry cleared it.

  New config: `evals.capture` (default `true`) opts a project out entirely. New command: `devteam evals gc` removes `.devteam/evals/blobs/` entries no case's `inputs/manifest.json` references anymore. `.devteam/evals/` joins the managed `.gitignore` block (local-only, like `.devteam/corpus/` — no cross-project or cross-run aggregation).

  Tests: `tests/evals-capture.test.js` — a stubbed FAIL dispatch (via `runStageHeadless`) produces a complete case directory; a real `stampStage04a` status_overridden is captured with `capture_reason: "stamp-override"`; a multi-workstream merge FAIL is captured via `core/driver.js#run` with an injected `mergeWorkstreamGates`; a fix-retry run-log event followed by the stage's gate turning PASS links a `resolution.json`; two cases sharing identical artifact content dedup to one blob; `evals.capture: false` captures nothing; a planted secret in a readFirst file is excluded from the blob store; `devteam evals gc` removes only unreferenced blobs.

  Honest scope note: capture is local-only and per-project (mirrors `core/corpus.js`'s scope decision) — there is no bounded-isolation (`pipeline/changes/<id>/`) plumbing for `.devteam/evals/`, matching the run corpus's own precedent. The resolution-linker's "which retry cleared it" is a best-effort correlation by stage id and timestamp ordering against `run-log.jsonl`, not a strict causal proof — a stage that flaps FAIL→PASS→FAIL→PASS within one run links every earlier unresolved case to the same final clearing retry. `devteam evals run` (the replay harness) and `prompt_pack_version` (33.2/33.3) are separate, not-yet-implemented plan items — this item only builds the capture substrate they'll consume.

### Added

- **`devteam evals run` — the eval flywheel replay harness (Phase 33.2).** Replays cases captured by `core/evals/capture.js` (33.1) against the CURRENT framework — today's `core/pipeline/stages.js` stage definitions, role briefs, rules, templates, and `render-helpers.js` prompt layout — to catch framework regressions independent of the model. `devteam evals run [--stub | --headless-host <h>] [--filter <stage|id>] [--budget-usd <n>] [--json]`.

  For each matching case, `core/evals/run.js` rebuilds the descriptor via `core/orchestrator.js#buildDescriptor` against today's stage definitions (never the descriptor at capture time — that's the whole point), materializes a scratch project that overlays the case's captured `inputs/manifest.json` blobs onto the target project's own currently-installed `.devteam/` framework files (or this package's bundled defaults, for a target with no live install — e.g. this repo's own fixture corpus), and renders through the same adapter the case recorded.

  `--stub` (default, free, no model): scores the re-rendered prompt structurally — the always-present scaffold sections (`## Objective`, `## Read first`, `## Allowed writes`, `## Gate to write`, the framework preamble when the stage's `readFirst` starts with `FRAMEWORK_READ_FIRST`) are still there, the rendered bytes are within the stage's committed dispatch-budget ceiling (`docs/reference/prompt-budget.md`'s `parseCommittedBudget`), and how far the prompt hash has drifted from the case's recorded one (always reported, never gated — the replay context legitimately differs from the original run's).

  `--headless-host <h>` dispatches for real via the existing `core/adapters/headless.js#runHeadless` machinery (`DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND` respected, same as every other headless path). A case whose original failure was later RESOLVED (`resolution.json` present) must come back PASS/WARN now — still FAIL/ESCALATE is a regression and exits 1. An unresolved case has no known-good target status, so its current gate is reported but never fails the sweep. Prints a 29.3-style cost preview (`core/ceremony-preview.js`'s `mostRecentModelObserved`/`pricingFor`, byte-sampled from the actual re-rendered prompts) before any dispatch and refuses to run without `--budget-usd`.

  Output: a human table by default, or JSONL (one line per case + a trailing summary line) with `--json`, for CI consumption. New CI job (`.github/workflows/test.yml`) runs `--stub` over a small checked-in fixture corpus (`tests/fixtures/evals/`) on every push/PR — this repo's own framework regression gate.

  Tests: `tests/evals-run.test.js` — stub scoring on the fixture corpus (all-OK, exit 0); `--filter` by stage id; a case whose stage no longer exists in `stages.js` scores `error` (exit 1); `--headless-host` without `--budget-usd` refuses with a printed cost preview (exit 1); a resolved case redispatched to PASS via a scripted `DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND` scores `pass`; the same case scripted back to FAIL (a seeded re-break) scores `regression` (exit 1); an unresolved case still failing scores `still-failing` without failing the sweep.

  Honest scope note: a merge-level case (multi-role stage, `role: null` — see 33.1's capture split between `core/orchestrator.js` and `core/driver.js`) replays against the stage's first currently-configured role only; the case format doesn't identify which per-role workstream actually owned the failure, and this item doesn't add per-role fanout for that class. The committed dispatch-budget ceiling used for the `--stub` budget check (`docs/reference/prompt-budget.md`) includes the role-brief's own bytes, but adapters built on `render-helpers.js` reference the role brief by path rather than inlining it — so the check is a loose sanity ceiling against accidental scaffold bloat (e.g. a runaway learned-context section), not a tight budget for the rendered text itself. GEPA-style offline optimization (33.4) is a separate, not-yet-implemented plan item; `prompt_pack_version` comparison shipped as phase-33 item 33.3 (see `changelog.d/prompt-pack-version.md`).

### Added

- **feat(pipeline):** framework files (`.devteam/rules/*.md`, role briefs, templates) now resolve
  correctly when a review workspace's state root differs from the subject being reviewed
  (`plans/phase-36-external-review-mode.md`, item 36.2). `core/pipeline/stages.js` gains
  `isFrameworkReadFirstPath()`, derived from the existing `FRAMEWORK_READ_FIRST` constant, and
  `core/adapters/render-helpers.js` gains `resolveFrameworkPath(relPath, ctx)`: when
  `ctx.processCwd` (the subject, 36.1's `codeRoot`) differs from `ctx.cwd` (the review workspace,
  `stateRoot`), a framework path renders as an absolute path into `stateRoot` instead of a
  relative path that would resolve into the subject, where it does not exist.
  `core/orchestrator.js#buildDescriptor` applies this to `readFirst` entries;
  `core/adapters/markdown-host.js` (the acp/codex/gemini-cli shared renderer) applies it to the
  role-brief and template pointers. `AGENTS.md` is deliberately left "subject"-rooted — a
  reviewer must read the subject's own `AGENTS.md`, not Stagecraft's init stub.

  Every pre-36.3 caller leaves `ctx.processCwd` unset (36.3 is what will start setting it for a
  real review workspace), so `codeRoot === stateRoot` everywhere in production today and this
  change is a byte-identical no-op until then — covered by
  `tests/external-framework-paths.test.js`'s equal-roots regression, run against both
  `buildDescriptor()`'s output and the fully rendered acp-host prompt.

  Honest scope note: implemented via a small path-based lookup derived from
  `FRAMEWORK_READ_FIRST` rather than annotating every one of `core/pipeline/stages.js`'s ~20
  per-stage `readFirst` arrays with an explicit `{path, root: "framework"}` object — the object
  form (`{path, root, optional}`) is real and honored when present, but marking the two known
  rule files by path keeps this a two-function, zero-`stages.js`-diff change instead of one that
  also has to fix `scripts/prompt-budget.js`'s and `scripts/prompt-optimize.js`'s existing
  strict-shape/`.includes()` readFirst consumers. See the PR description for the full
  deviation rationale.

### Added

- **Docs: external review guide — phase-36 item 36.6 (`plans/phase-36-external-review-mode.md`).** New `docs/external-review.md` documents `devteam review <path>` and `devteam review-pr <number|url>` (36.4/36.5): the two entry points, a per-host enforcement table naming the exact file that backs (or fails to back) each host's read-only claim, the review workspace layout (`core/review-workspace.js`), where a review's evidence lands, and the honest limits — the `acp` `execute` allowlist (`hosts/acp/permissions.js#findReviewExecViolation`) and that `read`-kind calls carry no location check today. Cross-linked from a new `docs/compliance.md` § Reviewing code you don't own; `README.md`'s `--host acp` paragraph now names it as the recommended host for reviewing code you don't own; added to `docs/README.md`'s index.

  Writing the enforcement table surfaced (and now documents) a real gap: in review mode, the non-`acp` post-hoc write-audit (`core/adapters/headless.js`'s `snapshotWritables(ctx.cwd)`) snapshots the review *workspace*, never `ctx.processCwd` (the subject) — it cannot see a subject write at all, and typically no-ops outright since the workspace isn't a git repository. That is weaker than `checkHostHonesty`'s own warning text ("post-hoc audit of what the agent actually wrote") implies for any `--host` other than `acp`.

  Honest scope note: no code changed. The write-audit gap above is documented, not fixed — fixing it (e.g. pointing the audit at `codeRoot` when `ctx.externalReviewMode` is set) is out of scope for a docs item and is left as a follow-up.

### Added

- **Run corpus: one sanitized JSONL record per headless dispatch, plus `devteam corpus stats` — Phase 28 item 28.5.** After every headless dispatch (`devteam run`, `devteam stage --headless`, `devteam replay`, the a11y-fixer), the orchestrator appends one line to `.devteam/corpus/dispatches.jsonl` (project-local, gitignored by the managed block, never uploaded): `{ts, run_id, stage, role, host, model_observed, track, prompt_hash, prompt_bytes, tokens_in, tokens_out, cost_usd, cost_basis, duration_ms, queue_ms, gate_status, blockers, retry_of, framework_version}`. Fields missing for a given dispatch are `null`, never omitted. `model_observed`/tokens/`cost_usd` prefer the gate's `_orchestrator_observed` block over model-asserted top-level fields (same trust-boundary pattern as `core/verify/stamp.js`); `cost_basis` records which one won. `blockers` are sanitized through the same secret-scan path (`core/hooks/secret-scan.js scanContent`) `core/patterns.js` collection uses — a blocker containing secret-shaped text is fully redacted before it ever reaches disk. Writes are fire-and-forget: an unwritable `.devteam/corpus/` directory logs one warning and never fails the run. New `devteam corpus stats [--json]` reports total dispatches, per-stage pass rates, and per-(role, host) dispatch counts — worded to answer the D5/H3 evidence-gate questions in `docs/BACKLOG.md` directly. `.devteam/corpus/` added to the managed gitignore block. `scripts/routing-suggest.js` (D5) now reads the corpus as an additional data source alongside `pipeline/gates/` archives, so dispatch history survives gate archiving/pruning. Docs: `docs/observability.md` "Run corpus" section. Tests: `tests/corpus.test.js` (14 new) — one corpus line per dispatch with correct fields via a stubbed multi-stage headless run, a planted secret in a blocker never reaching disk (both the parsed field and the raw file content), `--skip-completed` no-op dispatches excluded, an unwritable corpus directory logging exactly one warning without throwing or failing a real dispatch, `devteam corpus stats` aggregating a fixture corpus (including a malformed line being skipped) via both the module API and the CLI, and `scripts/routing-suggest.js` merging corpus records into `aggregatePerformance`. Honest scope note: the experimental `--experimental-omnigent-director` dispatch path is not yet recorded to the corpus (it predates per-dispatch usage telemetry entirely); cross-project D5/H3 aggregation remains a manual step — the corpus itself is local-only and never combined across projects automatically.

### Added

- **`loop` track: a 4-slot minimal-ceremony pipeline — phase-29 item 29.1.** New track `loop` (`STAGES_BY_TRACK.loop` in `core/pipeline/stages.js`): `requirements` (stage-01, one-screen brief) → `build` (stage-04, single workstream) → `qa` (stage-06) → `peer-review` (stage-05, single reviewer). Note the order — `qa` runs before `peer-review` on this track, the reverse of every other track; `verifyChain`'s nearest-earlier-gate predecessor rule already walks the track array in declared order rather than by numeric stage ID, so the tamper-evident chain still links correctly (new tests in `tests/chain.test.js` prove this explicitly). Build and peer-review each dispatch one config-overridable workstream (`pipeline.loop_build_role` in `.devteam/config.yml`, default `backend`) instead of the four-role matrix — `loopBuildRole()` in `core/pipeline/stages.js` is the single source of truth both `rolesForStage()` and `requiredApprovalsFor()` read, so the reviewed area always matches what build actually touched. Stage-01 renders a new one-screen template (`templates/loop-brief-template.md`: intent, AC-N list, affected files) on this track only, via a new `trackOverrides` mechanism on the stage definition (parallel to the existing `repairOverride`). `loop` has no design, no red-team, and no sign-off/deploy — `devteam run --track loop` ends at `peer-review`; promoting to a deploy-capable track is a re-run with `--until` on a bigger track or a `custom_stages` config, not a `loop` feature. `loop` is also added to `core/guards/stoplist.js`'s `STOPLIST_TRACKS` — the consequence ceiling applies to the lightest track exactly like the other lighter tracks. Docs: `docs/tracks.md` (new "The `loop` track" section + matrix row), `rules/pipeline-tracks.md`, and every other stage/track-count claim the consistency checker flagged (`docs/concepts.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/README.md`, `docs/comparative-analysis.md`, `docs/presentation-notes.md`, `docs/adr/006-track-inference-under-autonomy.md`, `docs/verification-beyond-tests.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`, `rules/gates-core.md`, `rules/orchestrator.md`, `roles/red-team.md`). Tests: `tests/tracks.test.js` (track shape + role/approval sizing, including the config override and its unrecognized-value fallback), `tests/pipeline-e2e.test.js` (a full stubbed `loop` run completes in exactly 4 dispatches, no merges, with an audit trail identical in shape to the other tracks), `tests/chain.test.js` (the inverted stage-06→stage-05 predecessor link stamps and verifies, and tamper detection still locates the break), `tests/orchestrator.test.js` (the track-based template override reaches the rendered prompt end-to-end via `runStage`, and repair intent still wins over the loop override), `tests/run.test.js` (the stoplist halts a `loop` run exactly like `quick`), `tests/config.test.js` (`loop_build_role` default and override), `tests/contract.test.js` (every `trackOverrides` template exists on disk). Honest scope note: track *selection* — `devteam assess` inferring `loop` from a change description — is out of scope for this item (phase-29 item 29.2); `loop` is opt-in via `--track loop` or `pipeline.default_track: loop` only.

### Added

- **Severity-ordered findings report with mitigations — Phase 35.4.** Today, "put an existing codebase through reviews and get one list of things to fix" means hand-assembling it from `pipeline/security-review.md`, `red-team-report.md`, `code-review/by-*.md`, `pipeline/gates/stage-06d.json`, and `docs/audit/*.md` separately. `devteam report --findings [--out <file>] [--json]` collects one ranked table instead: severity, `file:line`, what's wrong, suggested mitigation, rough effort, and provenance.
  - **Sources** (`core/report/collect-findings.js`): security-review (stage-04b `BLOCKER:` lines, escalated to `critical` on veto), red-team including the 31.2 mechanical floor (stage-04c `must_address_before_peer_review[]` / `noted_for_followup[]`, cross-enriched with `red-team-report.md` prose for mitigation/effort/file:line), peer-review + critic (stage-05 blockers, stage-05.critic.json unresolved challenges), the 31.4 mutation smoke gate (stage-06), verification-beyond-tests (stage-06d `blocking_findings[]` / `non_blocking_findings[]`), and `docs/audit/*.md` once `docs/audit/status.json` shows the audit workflow has run.
  - **Provenance is the important column**, reusing the existing `_orchestrator_stamped`/`_orchestrator_observed` distinction rather than inventing a new one: a finding is `orchestrator-observed` only when it traces to code the orchestrator itself ran — a mechanical red-team floor check (`source: "mechanical"`), an executed mutation gate, a stage-06d method the orchestrator actually confirmed with real evidence (not downgraded to `attempted_but_blocked:*`), or a peer-review approval mismatch the approval-derivation hook derived from the review file. Everything else — a reviewer's or auditor's judgment, even when well-evidenced in prose — is `model-asserted`.
  - A generic markdown finding-block parser (`core/report/parse-markdown-findings.js`) reads the "heading + bullet fields" shape shared, with drift, by `red-team-report.md` (`### RT-1 — <title>`) and `docs/audit/*.md` (`#### Finding S1: <title>`) — used only for best-effort enrichment (mitigation/effort/file:line), never as the source of truth for counts or provenance.
  - Self-contained HTML (`core/report/render-findings-html.js`, same offline-file discipline as `devteam report`) plus `--json`, schema-checked against `core/report/schemas/findings-report.schema.json`. The no-findings case renders an honest empty state, not a broken table.

  Honest scope note: file:line extraction is regex-based best-effort text parsing (`path/to/file.ext:NN` token matching), not a structured field on every source — security-review and peer-review blockers, in particular, only have a file:line when the reviewer happened to write one inline. Critic-challenge provenance is `model-asserted` for the claim content even when the hook mechanically forced its disposition to `unresolved` for missing evidence — this module doesn't split provenance within a single finding.

### Added

- **One front door for 103 doc files, and a check that keeps it that way — Phase 37.5.** `docs/START-HERE.md` names the five documents a new user actually needs (`EXAMPLE.md`, `docs/adoption-guide.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`, `docs/tracks.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`); `README.md` and `docs/README.md` both point to it ahead of the existing exhaustive four-reader-path map, which is unchanged and still there for everyone else.

  `docs/historical/` and `docs/reference/` each gained a `README.md` index (the former lists the one archived doc that already lived there plus the archive convention from `CONTRIBUTING.md`; the latter lists all five generated/hand-maintained reference docs and which is which). `docs/audit/README.md` — already existing — is now linked from `docs/README.md` too.

  `scripts/consistency.js`'s existing `docs-index` check (added under an earlier phase, D2) wholesale-excluded `historical/`, `reference/`, and `audit/` from the "every doc must be reachable from `docs/README.md`" requirement. Rather than add a second, overlapping check, this narrows that exclusion list down to a single deliberate exception — `audit-archive/`, a dated-snapshot pipeline-output archive indexed by its own `HISTORY.md` rather than a `README.md`, and not part of the hand-authored-doc sprawl this check exists to catch. `historical/`, `reference/`, and `audit/` now rely on the same directory-index-coverage rule `adr/` and `runbooks/` already used — no new mechanism, just closing three exemptions that had outlived their reason. `npm run consistency` went from 376 to 397 checks passed with the new indexes in place; zero files were deleted or moved.

  Tests: `tests/consistency-meta.test.js` gained a positive test that `historical/`, `reference/`, and `audit/` are covered once each has a linked `README.md`, and a negative test that a `historical/` doc without one is still (correctly) flagged as an orphan. The pre-existing "excluded dir is not required" test encoded the old wholesale `historical/` exemption; renamed and repointed it at `audit-archive/`, the one exemption this item keeps, since that's the behavior it actually verifies now.

  Honest scope note: no documentation was moved. The only file that ever lived under `docs/historical/` (`GAP-ANALYSIS.md`) was already there from an earlier phase; this item indexed it rather than relocating anything else, because nothing else in `docs/` was found to be genuinely superseded — see the PR description for the full list the generalized check would have flagged before these fixes (all of it indexed, none of it deleted). `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s Archive policy section claims `docs/historical/` "already uses" a datestamp-prefix naming convention "for the self-audit archive" — that's actually true of `docs/audit-archive/`'s dated subdirectories, not `docs/historical/`'s one undated file; left as-is and flagged for a human, since fixing it means picking between renaming the file (and its stub redirect) or correcting the sentence.

- **gemini-cli retired from a first-party host to a plugin package (phase-34 item 34.4, completes 28.6).** `hosts/gemini-cli/` moved to `packages/host-gemini-cli/`, a `@devteam/host-gemini-cli` package resolved through the existing A4 pluggable-adapter mechanism (`core/router.js`) rather than the first-party `hosts/` enumeration. First-party surface is now claude-code, codex, antigravity, openai-compat, acp, omnigent, and generic. `devteam init --host gemini-cli` now fails with an exact install instruction (`npm install @devteam/host-gemini-cli`) when the plugin isn't present, and succeeds identically to before once it is. `devteam doctor`'s existing gemini-cli deprecation warning (phase-28 item 28.6) now fires independent of whether the plugin is installed, and reports a plugin-specific "not available" message instead of the generic `no adapter at hosts/<name>/` text. The adapter's own behavior is unchanged — same capabilities, same contract — only its location and packaging moved; its README documents the honest scope note that this package isn't published to any registry, and that its relative `require()`s into Stagecraft's core only resolve inside a Stagecraft checkout (tracked as `docs/BACKLOG.md` item A10).
  - **Bugfix surfaced along the way:** `core/router.js`'s `listExternalHosts()` used `Dirent.isDirectory()`, which reflects a directory entry's own type — false for a symlink, even one pointing at a directory. npm workspaces and `npm link` install local packages as symlinks under `node_modules/<scope>/`, so any first-party-maintained plugin installed that way (not just gemini-cli) was invisible to `listHosts()`/`loadAdapter()`. Fixed with an explicit `fs.statSync` follow when the entry is a symlink; regression test in `tests/router.test.js`.
  - Tests: `packages/host-gemini-cli/tests/adapter.test.js` (adapter's own contract, moved from `tests/adapter-contract.test.js` and friends), `tests/gemini-cli-plugin.test.js` (init error path + plugin-installed round-trip via a symlinked fixture), plus updates across `tests/adapter-contract.test.js`, `tests/audit.test.js`, `tests/capabilities.test.js`, `tests/doctor.test.js`, `tests/fanout.test.js`, `tests/goal-loop.test.js`, `tests/orchestrator.test.js`, `tests/prompt-layout.test.js`, `tests/router.test.js`, and `tests/write-audit.test.js` to reflect gemini-cli no longer being a first-party, always-loadable host in this dev checkout. Root `package.json`'s test script now also globs `packages/*/tests/*.test.js`.
  - Honest scope note: this package is shaped to be publishable but is not actually published anywhere as part of this change (see `packages/host-gemini-cli/README.md` and `docs/BACKLOG.md` item A10).

### Changed

- **`devteam --help` collapses from 343 lines to one screen, grouped by what you're trying to do — Phase 37.4.** The default listing doubled as the full flag reference, which meant a new user's first screen was 343 lines long. `core/cli/commands/help.js` now defaults to a ~27-line grouped view (`Start here`, `Daily`, `Review`, `Verify`, `Fix & retry`, `Learn`, `Audit`, `Setup & tools` — one line per group, commands separated by `·`) built from every entry in `core/cli/command-list.js`. The full listing — same content, nothing lost — moves behind `devteam --help --all` / `devteam help --all`, and still backs the generated `docs/reference/cli.md` (unchanged apart from documenting the new `--all` flag itself). `bin/devteam`'s `-h`/`--help`/no-argument branch now runs the shared flag parser against `help`'s own flag schema instead of ignoring everything after the flag, so `--all` reaches `help.run()`. Unknown-command messages (`devteam help <typo>`, `devteam <typo>`) now show the grouped view instead of the full one, for the same one-screen reason.

  No command renames or removals — presentation only. Grouping decisions belong in one place: `GROUPS` in `help.js`, exported for tests.

  Tests: `tests/help-cmd.test.js` gained — default help ≤ 45 lines; `help --all` and `--help --all` both print the full reference (`Commands:`/`Quickstart:`); a coverage test that every command in `command-list.js` appears in `GROUPS` exactly once, so a future command can't be added without being grouped; unknown-command fallback shows the grouped view. Updated three pre-existing tests that encoded the old "default help is the full listing" behavior: `help-cmd.test.js`'s no-argument and unknown-command assertions now check for the grouped view (their old assertions moved to the new `--all` test); `cli.test.js`'s per-command coverage regex loosened from start-of-line matching (which assumed one command per line) to a word-boundary match, since commands now share a line within a group; `evidence-status.test.js`'s help-listing assertion split into a default-help check (`evidence` — the bare command name) and an `--all` check (`evidence status` — the subcommand detail that moved).

  Honest scope note: `docs/reference/cli.md` is *not* byte-unchanged as the plan item's acceptance criterion literally states — adding the `--all` flag itself to `help`'s schema (needed so `parseFlags` accepts it and `generateHelp`/`docs:generate` stay the single source of truth for flag docs) adds one documented flag row to the generated reference's `help` section. The alternative — special-casing `--all` outside the flag schema — would have made `devteam help --all` and `devteam --help --all` diverge in how they parse the flag, which seemed worse than a four-line doc diff.

### Fixed

- `devteam help`'s top-level command listing was missing `corpus` and `evals` entirely — both are real, working, `--help`-documented commands (registered in `core/cli/command-list.js`, phase 37.1), just never added to this hand-written summary list. Added one-line entries for each, matching the surrounding style. `tests/cli.test.js` gained a regression test asserting every command in `core/cli/command-list.js` appears in the top-level listing, so a future command can't silently go missing from it again.

  Honest scope note: this listing is still hand-maintained prose, not generated from the flag specs like `docs/reference/cli.md` (which already listed both commands correctly) or the per-command `--help` output (phase 37.1) — unifying it with the generated doc is a larger change than this fix and out of scope here.

### Added

- **Agent Client Protocol (ACP) host adapter — phase-34 item 34.1 (`docs/user-guide.md` "Using ACP", `core/adapters/host-adapter.md`).** `hosts/acp/` speaks [Agent Client Protocol](https://agentclientprotocol.com) (Zed, JetBrains, Google, GitHub, 25+ agents) as a *client* over stdio, so any ACP-compatible coding agent becomes a Stagecraft host with zero adapter code written per agent — verified against the published schema (`zed-industries/agent-client-protocol` `schema/v1/schema.json`, protocol version 1, pinned in `hosts/acp/capabilities.json`'s `acpProtocolVersion`) rather than prose docs alone, since the docs site omits transport framing (newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio; stdout carries only protocol messages, stderr is agent logging). `hosts/acp/jsonrpc.js` is a from-scratch ~130-line bidirectional JSON-RPC client (no new npm dependency — same house style as the existing `claude-stream-json`/`codex-exec-json` line-JSON extractors) since ACP requires both directions: Stagecraft sends `initialize`/`session/new`/`session/prompt`, and the agent sends `session/request_permission` back plus `session/update` progress notifications. Dispatch = initialize → session/new → one `session/prompt` turn with the rendered stage prompt → stream `session/update` into `pipeline/logs/<workstreamId>.log` → best-effort `session/close` → resolve once the turn ends (`stopReason`) and the gate file is checked (the existing hooks:false poll fallback in `core/orchestrator.js` covers the rest).
  - **Enforcement mapping (`hosts/acp/permissions.js`).** Every ACP tool call is a `session/request_permission` round-trip *before* it executes, carrying `kind`/`locations`/`rawInput` — real structure to decide on, unlike claude-code's own tool-call-time enforcement (static per-project permission globs configured once at install, `hosts/claude-code/adapter.js` `renderSettingsLocal`). The adapter checks the *live* per-dispatch `descriptor.allowedWrites` (via `core/guards/write-audit.js`'s `isAllowed`) for edit/delete/move tool calls, and a claude-code-parity dangerous-command stoplist (`rm -rf`, `git push --force`/`-f` — mirroring `renderSettingsLocal`'s `permissions.deny`, not `core/guards/stoplist.js`'s topic-based auth/crypto/PII patterns, which gate track selection for a whole run and would false-positive on ordinary full-track work touching e.g. `auth.js`) against the tool call's title/rawInput. `capabilities.json` declares `allowed_writes`/`stoplist: "tool-call-time"` — **the first non-claude-code host with call-time enforcement**, closing part of the enforcement-parity gap the landscape review flagged. Deny/allow always selects a real offered `PermissionOption` (`reject_once`/`allow_once` preferred over the `_always` variants, since Stagecraft re-decides every call from the current descriptor); silence never defaults to permissive — an unmatched deny cancels the turn rather than falling through to allow.
  - **Routing (`core/config.js`, `core/router.js`, `core/orchestrator.js`).** `routing.roles.<role>`/`routing.stages.<stage>` gain a third string shape, `"acp:<agent-command>"` — there's no single default ACP binary the way there is for claude/codex/gemini, so the launch command travels with the route itself. `normalizeRouteValue` parses it into `{host: "acp", agentCommand}`; the object form (`{host: "acp", agentCommand: "..."}`) works too. `resolveRoute`/`resolveAdapter` surface `agentCommand` alongside `hostName`/`model` (same "rides on the descriptor" pattern 32.3 established for `model`) — added *only* when non-empty, so every pre-34.1 route shape stays byte-identical (verified by re-running the existing `deepEqual`-based back-compat tests unchanged). `hosts/acp/adapter.js`'s `resolveAgentCommand` resolution order: `DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND` → `descriptor.agentCommand` → `.devteam/config.yml` `hosts.acp.command` → `capabilities.headlessCommand` (a runnable reference default, `npx -y @agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp` — the official ACP bridge for Claude Code; any other agent needs an explicit command).
  - **Wording fix (`core/adapters/render-helpers.js`).** `allowedWritesCaption`'s tool-call-time wording hardcoded "hooks" — accurate for claude-code but wrong for ACP, which enforces via permission requests, not hooks. Added an optional `mechanism` parameter (default `"hooks"`, byte-identical for every existing caller) that `core/adapters/markdown-host.js` now threads from a new optional `capabilities.enforcementMechanismLabel` field; `hosts/acp/capabilities.json` sets it to `"permission requests"`.
  - Tests: `tests/host-acp.test.js` (end-to-end stage completion via a scripted stub agent, an out-of-allowedWrites edit denied at call time via a real `reject_once` option, the `rm -rf` stoplist denied the same way, a malformed non-JSON protocol line terminating the session with a reported `protocolError` instead of hanging, an agent that never responds bounded by `ctx.timeoutMs`, plus unit coverage of `permissions.js`), `tests/fixtures/acp-stub-agent.js` (the scripted agent — speaks the wire protocol directly, independent of `hosts/acp/jsonrpc.js`, so a framing bug there can't hide behind a shared implementation), `tests/config.test.js` and `tests/router.test.js` (the `acp:<command>` routing form + back-compat), `tests/adapter-contract.test.js` (added to `PROMPT_ONLY_HOSTS`, `HOSTS_WITH_RENDER`, `SHARING_HOSTS`, and the 6.1 tool-budget-resolution host list — the shared gate-footer equivalence check passes unmodified, confirming the enforcement-level wording fix above didn't leak into the footer boundary).
  - Honest scope note: `telemetry: "estimated"` — ACP has no standardized usage/cost field, so this adapter falls back to the same promptBytes/4 estimate every non-native host uses. `subagents`/`hooks`/`goalLoop`/`worktrees` are all `false` — Stagecraft doesn't wire into any ACP-side equivalents here. Enforcement only covers tool calls the agent honestly reports via `kind`/`locations`/`rawInput`; an `execute`-kind tool call that writes files via shell redirection isn't checked against `allowedWrites` (same gap claude-code's own Bash permission model has — Bash isn't glob-restricted to allowed directories either, only the dangerous-command deny-list applies). No post-hoc write-audit backstop is layered on top, matching the precedent every other `"tool-call-time"`-declaring host (claude-code) already sets. ACP server mode (Stagecraft driven *by* another tool) is explicitly out of scope, per the phase-34 plan.

- **Antigravity CLI host adapter (phase-28 item 28.6) — Gemini CLI continuity.** Gemini CLI stopped serving free/Pro/Ultra requests 2026-06-18; Google's supported successor is Antigravity CLI (binary: `agy`). `hosts/antigravity/` is a thin shell over `makeMarkdownHostAdapter`, structurally identical to `hosts/codex/` and `hosts/gemini-cli/`: plain-markdown role prompts at `.agents/prompts/roles/`, skills at `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, no hooks/slash commands/native-subagent dispatch, post-hoc git-diff write audit, headless via `agy --print --dangerously-skip-permissions` (prompt piped to stdin). Every capability was verified against the actual installed binary rather than public docs alone — `agy --help`, an authenticated live `agy --print` dispatch, and the CLI's own embedded customization-root documentation (`strings` on the binary) — because several third-party "cheat sheet" sites surfaced by web search disagreed with the real binary (e.g. claiming plugins stage under `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/` when the actual global customization root is `~/.gemini/config/`). `devteam doctor` now warns whenever routing resolves any role or stage to `gemini-cli`, pointing at `devteam init --host antigravity`; `gemini-cli` itself is untouched and stays installed-and-functional (full retirement is a separate later item). Updated `docs/user-guide.md`'s multi-host section, README's host lists, and `tests/adapter-contract.test.js`'s shared-host arrays (gate-footer equivalence, PATCH MODE rendering, tool-budget rendering) to include the new host. *Honest scope note:* `telemetry: "estimated"` — no native usage/cost parsing was implemented for `agy`'s own JSON output in this item (that's tracked separately under item 28.3, which already shipped `estimated` for gemini-cli/generic/omnigent); `subagents`/`hooks`/`goalLoop` are declared `false` even though `agy` has some native equivalents (an `--agent` flag, plugin `hooks.json`) because Stagecraft doesn't wire into them here, matching the existing codex/gemini-cli stance of only declaring capabilities Stagecraft itself dispatches through.

- **Orchestrator-observed token telemetry for codex; honest `estimated` fallback elsewhere (phase-28 item 28.3).** The codex headless command now requests `--json`; `core/adapters/codex-exec-json.js` parses the resulting JSONL for the `turn.completed` event's `usage.input_tokens` / `usage.cached_input_tokens` / `usage.output_tokens` (codex reports neither a model id nor a dollar cost in this stream, so `model_observed`/`cost_usd` stay `null` rather than guessed), tees only the readable `agent_message` text into the transcript log, and `core/orchestrator.js`'s shared `patchGateForObservedUsage` writes it onto the workstream gate under `_orchestrator_observed` with `source: "codex:exec-json"`. Every host's `capabilities.json` now declares `telemetry: "native"` or `telemetry: "estimated"` (enforced by `tests/adapter-contract.test.js`; claude-code and openai-compat are `native` per items 28.1/28.2, codex per this item, gemini-cli/generic/omnigent are `estimated`). For `estimated` hosts, a new `patchGateForEstimatedUsage` records `_orchestrator_observed: { tokens_estimated: true, tokens_in_estimate, source: "orchestrator:prompt-bytes-estimate", at }` — a `promptBytes / 4` heuristic, distinctly named from `tokens_in` and always paired with the `tokens_estimated` flag so it can never be silently mixed with an observed value. *Honest scope note:* gemini CLI's `-o json`/`-o stream-json` do carry token stats in principle (confirmed by reading the installed CLI's bundled source), but its backend has returned 404s since 2026-06-18 and the host is being retired in favor of Antigravity (item 28.6) — a native parser was not built for a host on its way out; it falls back to the estimate. `--budget-usd`/`devteam status` preferring observed cost over self-reported (item 28.4) remains follow-up work.

### Added

- **Inline the framework set and role brief into the cacheable prompt prefix (phase-37 item 37.2).** Layers 1-2 of every stage prompt — `AGENTS.md`, `.devteam/rules/pipeline.md`, `.devteam/rules/gates-core.md`, and the role brief — now carry those files' actual content, in the stable order phase 32.1 established, instead of just naming their paths. The verify-first measurement in `plans/phase-37-interface-and-token-efficiency.md` §37.2 found the pointer-only prefix shared across two same-role dispatches was 213 B (12.7% of the prompt; the plan's own prior measurement cited 268 B/15.2%) — the ~22 KB framework block was paid at full price every dispatch via 4+ sequential tool-call reads, with almost nothing cacheable. Inlining moved the shared prefix to 18,932 B (92.8%) in the same measurement, and eliminates those reads entirely. New config `prompts.inline_framework` (default `true`) gates this; `false` reproduces the pre-37.2 path-pointer rendering byte-for-byte, for a host with no prefix caching and a small context window. `openai-compat`'s existing `cache_control` breakpoints (32.1) land after the now much larger inlined block unchanged. `core/evals/run.js`'s structural scoring now recognizes both the inlined and pointer framework headings.

  Honest scope note: `docs/reference/prompt-budget.md`'s per-dispatch byte totals are unchanged by this item — that doc already counted the full framework + role-brief bytes as consumed per dispatch regardless of whether they arrived inline or via a tool-call read, so there is nothing to re-baseline there; the growth this item causes is in the *literal* rendered-prompt size (measured above), not in that file's metric, and `npm run consistency`'s prompt-budget advisory does not fire. Rewriting the role briefs to be smaller (tempting now that their size is cacheable) is explicitly out of scope for this phase.

### Added

- Added p50/p95 duration and retry-adjusted completion metrics to performance and routing suggestions, plus capacity guidance for remote runners, prompt caching, and persistent-session boundaries.
- Added bounded changed-file manifests with path/status/byte/digest facts so stage prompts can slim repeated context while preserving on-demand reads.
- Added per-dispatch prompt byte and manifest-count telemetry to run logs, status state, and evidence aggregates without storing prompt text.

### Added

- **Memory retrieval into stage prompts + auto-ingest at run end — phase-30 item 30.4 (`docs/memory.md`).** When `.devteam/memory/` already exists and `memory.inject` isn't `false` (both true by default once a project has run `devteam memory ingest` at least once), every **headless** stage dispatch (`devteam run`, `devteam stage --headless`) now queries the store for the top `memory.inject_top_k` (default 3) chunks similar to the stage's feature/brief text, filters by `memory.inject_similarity_floor` (default `0`), and renders a bounded (≤1,200 bytes) `## Prior Project Knowledge` section with kind + source attribution per entry — same greedy accumulate-skip-oversized budget discipline as `Known Project Patterns` (`core/patterns.js` `selectForDescriptor()`). Stage 2 (design) descriptors additionally query the org-shared store for `kind: adr`, making `devteam architecture lookup` automatic instead of a role-brief-only suggestion (`roles/principal.md` updated; the manual command remains for deeper/broader queries). `core/driver.js` also auto-ingests once at a clean `pipeline-complete`, gated on the same `.devteam/memory/`-exists condition and reusing `memory.inject` as the single on/off switch for both sides of the loop — fire-and-forget, an ingest failure (including the optional `@huggingface/transformers` dependency being absent) is logged (`memory-ingest-failed`) and never fails the run. New config: `memory.inject` (default `true`), `memory.inject_top_k` (default `3`), `memory.inject_similarity_floor` (default `0`). New module `core/memory/inject.js` (`priorKnowledgeForStage()`) and `renderPriorKnowledge()` (`core/adapters/render-helpers.js`, wired into all seven host adapters). *Honest scope note:* `buildDescriptor()`/`runStage()` (`core/orchestrator.js`) are and remain synchronous — 37 direct `runStage()` calls and 25+ `buildDescriptor()` calls across the test suite, plus three preview-only CLI commands (`devteam stage` without `--headless`, `devteam reproduce`, `devteam replay`), assume a plain synchronous return, and embedding is inherently async. Retrieval is therefore pre-fetched in `runStageHeadless()` (the one dispatch path that was already async) and threaded through as a plain `opts.priorKnowledge`/`descriptor.priorKnowledge` field; the interactive (non-headless) preview path does not query memory. Tests: `tests/memory-injection.test.js` — gating (blank query text, no store, `memory.inject: false`), similarity-floor filtering + kind/source attribution, deterministic budget truncation favoring highest similarity, stage-02-only org query, one-warning embedder-absent degradation, `renderPriorKnowledge()` rendering, `buildDescriptor()`/`runStage()` threading (byte-identical prompts when absent), an end-to-end `runStageHeadless()` wiring test with a hand-seeded store record (deterministic similarity via an identical query/stored embedding), a stage-02 org-ADR end-to-end test, and driver auto-ingest (store-present/absent, `memory.inject: false`, and the fire-and-forget failure contract). `tests/config.test.js` covers the new config defaults/overrides. No pre-existing tests required changes: gating auto-ingest on `.devteam/memory/` already existing means the many driver tests built on `makeTargetProject()`'s bare fixture (no memory store) see zero new run-log events.

### Added

- **Per-role/per-stage model-tier routing + escalate-on-retry — phase-32 item 32.3 (`docs/user-guide.md` "Pinning a model in routing config", `docs/cost.md`).** `routing.roles.<role>` and `routing.stages.<stage>` now accept either the pre-32.3 bare host-name string (unchanged, still the only form `devteam init` writes) or `{host, model}` to also pin a model for that role/stage. `core/config.js`'s new `resolveRoute(config, stage, role)` implements the unchanged stage→role→critic-diversity→default_host precedence but returns `{hostName, model}`; `resolveHost` stays a byte-identical wrapper around `.hostName` for every pre-32.3 caller. `core/router.js`'s `resolveAdapter` surfaces the resolved model alongside the adapter. Verify-first inventory found the routed model reaches every headless host by an identical `--model <value>` CLI flag (`claude`, `codex`, `gemini`, `agy`) — `core/adapters/headless.js`'s shared `runHeadless` appends it generically when `descriptor.model` is set, so claude-code/codex/gemini-cli/antigravity needed no adapter-specific code. `hosts/openai-compat/invoke.js` and `hosts/omnigent/adapter.js` (both non-`runHeadless` paths) were extended so the routing-pinned model takes precedence over their existing `hosts.openai-compat.models`/`hosts.omnigent.model` config, preserving that config as the fallback. The model actually requested is stamped onto the dispatch's gate as `model_requested` (orchestrator-set, fire-and-forget, mirroring the `_orchestrator_observed`/`_orchestrator_stamped` provenance pattern — distinct from the model-asserted `model` field and from `_orchestrator_observed.model_observed`) and threaded into `.devteam/corpus/dispatches.jsonl`. New `routing.tiers` (per-host ordered cheap→frontier model ladder) + `routing.escalate_on_retry` (default `false`) bump a pinned model exactly one tier on a fix-and-retry re-dispatch (`core/config.js`'s `escalateModel`, applied in `core/orchestrator.js` when the driver signals `opts.isRetry`), recorded as `_orchestrator_escalated: {from_model, to_model, reason, at}`. The "frontier plans, cheap executes" preset is documented as an opt-in config block in `docs/user-guide.md` + `docs/cost.md` — not a changed default. `scripts/performance.js` gains an additive `aggregatePerformanceByModel` (groups `(role, host, model)` instead of `(role, host)`, leaving the existing aggregation untouched) feeding a new "per-tier cost delta" section in `npm run routing:suggest`'s report.
  - Tests: `tests/config.test.js` (`resolveRoute` back-compat + object-form + critic-diversity-with-object-routes + `escalateModel`), `tests/router.test.js` (`resolveAdapter` surfaces `model`), `tests/headless.test.js` (`--model` reaches the spawned command; absent when unpinned), `tests/openai-compat-adapter.test.js` and `tests/omnigent-adapter.test.js` (routing pin overrides host-native config; falls back when unset), `tests/orchestrator.test.js` (`model_requested` stamped on the gate; escalate-on-retry bumps/doesn't bump per config; three fire-and-forget cases for the new patch function), `tests/run.test.js` (driver passes `isRetry: true` only on a fix-and-retry re-dispatch), `tests/corpus.test.js` (`model_requested` round-trips into the corpus record), `tests/performance.test.js` and `tests/routing-suggest.test.js` (`aggregatePerformanceByModel` / `buildTierDeltas` / `renderTierDeltas`).
  - Honest scope note: escalation is a single one-tier bump per fix-and-retry dispatch, not cumulative across repeated retries of the same stage — a second retry re-escalates from the route's pinned model, not from wherever the first retry landed. Escalation isn't yet folded into pre-dispatch `--budget-usd` enforcement (same gap as D7/28.4's cost-preference work). Fanout entries (`routing.review_fanout`) don't carry a routing-resolved model in this pass — only the primary stage/role resolution path does.

### Added

- **Opt-in mutation smoke gate for stage-06 — Phase 31.4.** `core/verify/mutation.js`, wired into `core/verify/stamp.js#stampStage06`. Disabled by default (`pipeline.verify.mutation.enabled: false`); when enabled, the orchestrator detects a supported mutation-testing runner already present in the target project — Stryker via a `@stryker-mutator/core` package.json devDependency (JS/TS), mutmut via the binary already on PATH/venv (Python), never installed by devteam — and runs it against the changed-file set only (`pipeline/changed-files.txt`, the same source `redteam-floor.js` uses), intersected with an optional `pipeline.verify.mutation.paths` scope. The run is time-boxed via `pipeline.verify.mutation.timeout_ms` (default 5 min), reusing `runCommand`'s existing `core/process-kill.js` machinery — a hung run is killed and recorded as a skip, never a false pass. The parsed score, runner, and scope are stamped onto the gate as `mutation_score` / `mutation_runner` / `mutation_scope`. Below `pipeline.verify.mutation.threshold` (default `0.7`) is advisory: a gate warning plus a `noted_for_followup` entry (`severity: "high"`) so it surfaces in `devteam advise` classification (routes to `PEER_REVIEW_RISK`) rather than being silently absorbed; it only becomes a blocking FAIL when `pipeline.verify.mutation.threshold_hard: true`. An absent runner, an empty scope, a timeout, or unparseable tool output are all recorded as an honest skip. `docs/verification-beyond-tests.md` now documents this mechanical gate distinctly from stage-06d's model-driven, full-track-only mutation pass, which it does not replace.

  Tests: `tests/verify-stamp-mutation.test.js` — disabled-by-default leaves stage-06 untouched; no-runner and empty-scope cases record honest skips; a below-threshold fixture WARNs and its `noted_for_followup` item classifies `PEER_REVIEW_RISK` via `core/advise.js#classifyItem`; an at/above-threshold score does not warn; `threshold_hard: true` flips a model-PASS gate to FAIL; a hung mutation command is killed cleanly at `timeout_ms` and recorded as a skip; unit coverage for runner detection, path-scope intersection, and Stryker output parsing.

  Honest scope note: mutation-tool output parsing is regex-based against each tool's documented clear-text summary format (Stryker's `N/M mutants killed (X%)` line, per `skills/verification-beyond-tests/SKILL.md`'s worked example; mutmut's emoji progress line) rather than a structured report file — unparseable output is always an honest skip, never a fabricated score, but a future tool version that changes its summary format will silently downgrade to skips until the regex is updated. `pipeline.verify.mutation.command` is an added override beyond the plan item's literal config shape (`{enabled, threshold, threshold_hard, timeout_ms, paths}`), mirroring the existing `dependency_audit_command` override from 31.2 — without it there is no way to exercise the run/parse/threshold pipeline in tests without a real Stryker/mutmut install, which devteam must never do. mutmut's real-world output format is inferred from documented CLI behavior, not verified against a live install; the Stryker-format branch is exercised end-to-end via the same override, but neither runner's auto-detection path (real `npx`/`mutmut` invocation) is exercised in CI, the same limitation `runSemgrepFloor`'s tests already accept for a binary devteam cannot assume is installed.

### Added

- Add experimental `devteam stage --headless --experimental-omnigent-director` mode for all-Omnigent multi-workstream stages, preserving normal child gate validation and merge behavior.

### Changed

- Add `hosts.omnigent` launch profile config for harness/model/server/session selection while preserving `DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND` override precedence.

### Changed

- Add opt-in Omnigent policy-file generation for allowed writes, shell/network requirements, and role tool budgets.

### Changed

- Use Omnigent's current `--prompt` transport by default, with stdin and opt-in `--prompt-file` compatibility modes.

- **Omnigent runtime adapter** (closes #291). Adds a first-party `omnigent` host adapter that
  installs Stagecraft role prompts, skills, rules, templates, and a default
  Omnigent agent bundle under `.omnigent/stagecraft/agent/`, then invokes Omnigent's
  one-shot `omnigent run ... --no-session --prompt <prompt>` path for headless
  workstreams. The design and Phase 24 plan document the intended evolution:
  configurable harness/model launch profiles (#292), hardened prompt transport
  (#293), Omnigent-policy bridging (#294), session evidence (#295), and a later
  director-mode experiment (#296). *Honest scope note:* this first slice treats Omnigent as a
  conservative host runtime with Stagecraft post-hoc write auditing; Omnigent-managed
  Codex runtime-home cache writes under `.codex-tmp/omnigent-codex-home-*` are ignored, but it does
  not yet claim Omnigent-native tool-call-time enforcement, sub-agent
  consolidation, or remote-session evidence.

### Changed

- Record adapter-private Omnigent session evidence sidecars with session/conversation IDs and policy verdict counts.

### Fixed

- Exempt OpenAI-compatible adapter orchestrator state and transcript files from
  post-hoc write audit in bounded isolation, matching the in-place behavior.

- **Orchestrator-observed token/cost telemetry for openai-compat (phase-28 item 28.2).** `hosts/openai-compat/invoke.js` now sums `usage.prompt_tokens` / `usage.completion_tokens` / `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` across every turn of the tool loop and computes `cost_usd` via `core/pricing.js` `computeCostUsd` (unknown model → `cost_usd: null`, never a guess). `core/orchestrator.js`'s shared `patchGateForObservedUsage` (introduced for claude-code in item 28.1) writes the result onto the workstream gate under `_orchestrator_observed`, now generalized to record a per-adapter `source` (`"claude-code:stream-json"` / `"openai-compat:usage"`) and an optional `cached_tokens` field — distinct from and never overwriting the model's self-reported `tokens_in`/`cost_usd`/`model` fields. A response with no `usage` object degrades to `telemetry: "unavailable"` and no gate mutation, same fire-and-forget contract as 28.1. *Honest scope note:* codex and gemini-cli/antigravity (item 28.3) and `--budget-usd`/`devteam status` preferring observed cost over self-reported (item 28.4) remain follow-up work; openai-compat's `cost_usd` is a token × pricing-table estimate (no cache discount applied), not a provider-billed figure like claude-code's.

- **Project-local pattern learning begins.** Added `devteam patterns collect/list/review/promote/retire/stats`, local `.devteam/patterns/` storage, sanitized observation collection from gates and archived retry failures, explicit promotion with secret-shaped text rejection, retired-pattern suppression, and a bounded `Known Project Patterns` prompt section for promoted guidance. *Honest scope note:* this first slice is advisory and project-local; live retry hooks, positive first-pass reinforcement, import/export, noise reporting, and deterministic-gate graduation recommendations remain follow-up work.

- **Patterns collect automatically at run end.** `devteam run` now calls pattern collection itself, fire-and-forget, on a clean `pipeline-complete` and on any halt that left a gate on disk — closing the loop that previously required a manual `devteam patterns collect` after every run. Collection also now consults `retired.json`: a candidate matching a retired pattern's identity is dropped and counted as `suppressed` in the collection summary instead of silently re-entering the candidate pool. `devteam patterns collect` is unchanged for manual and backfill use. *Honest scope note:* `devteam patterns list`/`review`/`promote` still recompute candidates independently from raw observations and do not consult `retired.json` or `pending-review.json` — retired-key suppression only guards the auto/manual collection path, not those commands directly (tracked as an out-of-scope finding).

### Added

- **Wire the pattern-learning outcome-feedback counters and add a demotion flow — phase-30 item 30.2 (`docs/pattern-learning.md`).** `stats.injected` and `stats.recurrence_after_injection` on a promoted pattern were initialized at promotion and never incremented anywhere; this closes that loop. `core/patterns.js` gains `recordInjection()`, called at real DISPATCH time only — `core/orchestrator.js`'s `runStageHeadless` (both the normal per-workstream path and the omnigent-director path) and `core/cli/commands/stage.js`'s non-headless "print the prompt for a human to paste into a host" path — never at a preview/render-only call to `runStage()` (`devteam reproduce`, `devteam replay --dry-run`), since no model ever sees those renderings.
