Each real dispatch increments every injected pattern's `stats.injected` once and appends a `pattern-injected` run-log event. `collect()` reads those events back and increments `recurrence_after_injection` when a blocker maps to a pattern id that was injected into that same stage's dispatch this run; detection is keyed by gate file identity (a new `.devteam/patterns/recurrence-checked.json`), not observation fingerprint, because a recurring blocker shares its fingerprint with the observation that got the pattern promoted in the first place. `devteam patterns review` now flags a promoted pattern as a demotion candidate once `recurrence_after_injection` reaches `patterns.demotion_recurrence_threshold` in `.devteam/config.yml` (default `3`) — flagging only, never automatic. `devteam patterns demote <id>` is the new explicit operator action that moves a promoted pattern back to candidate status into `.devteam/patterns/demoted.json`, recording an audit line (`demoted_by`, `demoted_at`, `reason`, `counters_at_demotion`) that survives a later `devteam patterns promote <id>` re-promotion. Docs: `docs/pattern-learning.md` (new "Outcome-Feedback Counters" section, updated Storage/Open Decisions), `docs/reference/cli.md` (regenerated). Tests: `tests/patterns.test.js` — a real headless dispatch increments `stats.injected` exactly once while a preview-only `runStage()` call never does; a seeded three-attempt recurrence scenario flags the pattern in `devteam patterns review` and stays idempotent on a second `collect()`; demote → promote round-trips preserve `demotion_history` and re-enable selection; counters persist across `collect()`/`promote()` cycles without one pattern's write clobbering a sibling's stats. Honest scope note: `stats.noise_reports` remains unwired — no command produces a noise report yet, so review can only ever flag on recurrence, not operator-reported noise. Auto-demotion and auto-retirement remain explicitly out of scope, consistent with the design doc's "promotion is explicit" stance.

### Added

- **`devteam patterns export --skill` — SKILL.md export of promoted patterns, phase-30 item 30.5 (`docs/pattern-learning.md`).** Serializes every currently-promoted pattern (`prompt_text`, `tier`, `domain`, and a short evidence-based rationale — never raw observations) into a `SKILL.md` file following the [Agent Skills open standard](https://agentskills.io/specification) (spec fetched 2026-07-31: `name` 1-64 chars, lowercase alnum/hyphen, no leading/trailing/consecutive hyphen, and must match the parent directory name; `description` 1-1024 chars), so the same learned knowledge loads natively in Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and Gemini/Antigravity outside a devteam run. Because `name` must match its directory, `[--out <dir>]` is treated as a *parent* directory rather than the skill directory itself — the skill always lands at `<out>/learned-patterns/SKILL.md` (`.devteam/learned-patterns/SKILL.md` by default) — so the constraint holds unconditionally instead of only for the default path. The generated file opens with `<!-- Generated by devteam patterns export; regenerate to update; do not hand-edit. -->` and groups patterns into one Markdown section per `domain`; re-exporting with an unchanged `promoted.json` produces a byte-identical file (no timestamps embedded). The rendered output is re-scanned with the same secret-scan path `devteam patterns promote` already uses (`core/hooks/secret-scan.js`'s `scanContent`) before anything is written — defense in depth against a hand-edited `promoted.json`, since `prompt_text` is otherwise only scanned once, at promotion time. `devteam init` now mentions the export path in its summary output — never auto-installs it — whenever promoted patterns already exist and at least one host being initialized declares a `skillsDir` in its adapter capabilities (read per-adapter, not hardcoded, so a new host adapter picks this up automatically). Docs: `docs/pattern-learning.md` (new "SKILL.md export (30.5)" subsection under Privacy and Export, plus a history update), `docs/reference/cli.md` (regenerated). Tests: `tests/patterns.test.js` — export produces spec-conformant frontmatter (name/description constraints, name-matches-directory-name) and a domain-grouped body; two exports over an unchanged `promoted.json` are byte-identical; `--out` always resolves to `<out>/learned-patterns/`; a `promoted.json` hand-edited to contain a secret-shaped `prompt_text` is rejected and no file is written; the CLI subcommand round-trips end-to-end and errors with exit code 2 when `--skill` is omitted. `tests/init.test.js` — the export tip appears only when promoted patterns exist *and* the initialized host has a skills directory, confirmed both ways plus for `generic` (no `skillsDir`) with patterns present. Honest scope note: only the Agent Skills format is supported (`--skill` is currently mandatory, not a choice among formats); `devteam init` never copies the exported directory into a host's skills directory itself — that copy step stays manual, consistent with the item's "no auto-install without the existing init overwrite rules."

### Added

- **Stage-05 (peer-review) quorum re-derivation — Phase 31.5.** Approval state was only kept honest by the claude-code PostToolUse hook (`core/hooks/approval-derivation.js`); on every other host — and any claude-code save that happens outside the hook, which is why `devteam derive-approvals` exists — a workstream gate could claim `PASS` off a status that was never actually derived from `pipeline/code-review/by-*.md`. `core/verify/stamp.js#stampStage05Merged` closes that gap: after stage-05 merges, the orchestrator re-derives approval state itself by calling the hook's own `parseReviewFile()` directly (never reimplementing the `REVIEW:`/`CHANGES REQUESTED` grammar) over every `by-*.md` file, on every host, unconditionally. A panel-mode workstream gate (`backend`/`frontend`/`platform`/`qa`) claiming `PASS` whose review file says `CHANGES REQUESTED` — or has no parseable verdict for that area at all — flips the merged stage-05 gate to `FAIL`, with a `{field: "approval_state", workstream, gate_said, file_said}` record on `_orchestrator_stamped.fields` and a matching blocker. `stage-05` joins `STAMPABLE_MERGE_STAGES` alongside `stage-04`, wired through the existing merge-time stamping call site in `core/driver.js` (no new call sites needed).

  Tests: `tests/verify-stamp.test.js` — a seeded mismatch (gate says `APPROVED`, review file says `CHANGES REQUESTED`) flips the merged gate to FAIL with the field-level record; agreeing states leave the merge untouched (no fields recorded, no status override); a workstream claiming `PASS` with no matching review-file section at all is recorded as its own `NO_PARSEABLE_VERDICT` mismatch class; adversarial mode's `reviewer`/`critic` workstreams are left untouched. Also updated `tests/verify-stamp.test.js`'s pre-existing "stampMerged rejects a stage with no merged stamping defined" test, which used `stage-05` as its not-yet-stampable example — swapped to `stage-06`, which still has no merged stamper.

  Honest scope note: scoped to panel mode's per-area workstreams only. Adversarial mode's (31.3) `reviewer`/`critic` workstreams aren't area-named — `by-reviewer.md` rolls every area into one combined gate and `by-critic.md` uses a different (challenge) grammar entirely — so re-deriving them against a per-area map isn't a valid comparison; they're left to their own existing verification path (evidence-required challenge disposition). This re-derivation also doesn't recompute *how many* distinct reviewers approved an area (the `required_approvals` count) — only whether any review file for a `PASS`-claiming area contradicts or is silent on that claim. Re-verifying quorum counts independently would be a natural follow-up but is out of scope for this item.

### Fixed

- **`devteam help <command>` and `devteam <command> --help` now print real, command-scoped help — Phase 37.1.** Previously `devteam help <anything>` ignored its argument and printed the identical 343-line full command list regardless of what was passed. Both forms now generate help from that command's own flag spec (`core/cli/flags.js#generateHelp`) — a synopsis/usage line, then each flag with its `(type)` and description — with no hand-written per-command prose to drift out of sync. `devteam help <command>` delegates to the target command's own `--help` handling (`cmd.run([], { help: true })`), so the two forms are guaranteed byte-identical: it's the same code path, not two implementations kept in sync by hand. `devteam help` with no argument is unchanged. An unknown command name (either form) now prints the full command list plus a "did you mean" suggestion from a small Levenshtein-distance helper (`core/cli/nearest-match.js`, no new dependency). The `bin/devteam` → command-module map moved to `core/cli/command-list.js` so `help.js` and `bin/devteam` resolve command names from one shared list instead of two.

  Also fixed: `devteam hosts --help` and `devteam stages --help` previously exited 2 ("Unknown flag: --help") since neither command declared a `help` flag; both now support it like every other command.

  Tests: `tests/help-cmd.test.js` — `help run` and `run --help` produce byte-identical output under 60 lines covering all 21 of `run`'s flags with their types; output differs between two different commands (the regression this item fixes); an unknown command name suggests a near match via both entry points; `help` with no argument is unchanged; and a meta-test asserting every flag in every one of the 44 command modules has a non-empty description (all already did — no spec fixes were needed). `tests/flags.test.js` gained a unit test for `generateHelp`'s new `(type)` tag.

  Honest scope note: the "synopsis line" called for in the plan item is realized as the existing generated `Usage: devteam <command> [options]` line rather than a new hand-authored one-line blurb per command — adding a separate synopsis field to all 44 modules would have created a second place (beyond the flag spec) to keep in sync, working against the single-source-of-truth goal this item is for.

### Added

- **Per-role orchestrator stamping for stage-04 (Build) — Phase 31.1.** Orchestrator-stamped verification previously stopped at `plan.workstreams.length === 1`, so build's four role workstreams (backend/frontend/platform/qa) and their merged gate were never independently verified — only model self-report. Two new stamp scopes in `core/verify/stamp.js`: `stampStage04Workstream` runs a lint check scoped to each role's `allowedWrites` directories (paths appended as extra args to the configured lint command — best-effort; tools that ignore extra positional args just lint the whole project, but `scoped_paths` records what was requested) and stamps `lint_passed`/`tests_passed` onto that role's own gate as it completes (wired into `core/orchestrator.js`'s per-workstream dispatch loop); `stampStage04Merged` runs the full test suite exactly once, after `mergeWorkstreamGates` writes the merged `stage-04.json` (wired into `core/driver.js`'s merge handler), and overrides a model-claimed `tests_passed` that disagrees with the observed suite result. `mergeWorkstreamGates` now rolls any per-role `tests_passed` self-report into the merged gate so the merged-level check has a claim to compare against. Verification receipts (already content-addressed by command+workspace+config+env) mean the per-role test checks and the merged check collapse to one real subprocess execution rather than one per workstream — proven via receipt-hit counting, not new dedup logic. Tests: `tests/verify-stamp.test.js` (lint scoping, status-flip + model_said audit trail, skip messaging, merged override, receipt-collapse across 4 roles + merge, dispatch rejection paths), `tests/orchestrator.test.js` (`tests_passed` roll-up aggregation, end-to-end `runStageHeadless` wiring including `stamp: false`), `tests/run.test.js` (driver wires the merged stamp in after merge and it wins over the model's claim). Existing single-workstream stamping (stage-03b/04a/06) is untouched — same code path, same behavior.

  Honest scope note: lint scoping is best-effort (path args aren't guaranteed to be honored by every lint tool/config); the standalone `devteam merge` CLI command does not yet trigger the merged-level stamp (only the `devteam run` driver loop does); `devteam verify stage-04` does not yet support on-demand multi-workstream re-stamping. All are natural follow-ups, not required for this item's acceptance criteria.

### Changed

- **Docs: phase 35 marked complete; track-list and stage-06d drift fixed.** `plans/phase-35-existing-codebase-mode.md`, `plans/README.md`, and `plans/landscape-review-2026-07.md` still described `review-only`/`devteam review-pr` (35.1/35.2) as not-started and phase 35 overall as proposed, despite all five items being merged (#373, #375, #376, #377, #378) — reconciled against `git log`. `docs/concepts.md`/`docs/FEATURES.md`'s "Nine tracks" was stale after the `refactor` track landed (now "Ten tracks"); the stage-06d ("verification beyond tests") description still described pre-35.3 model-self-report-only behavior — added a note on `core/verify/stamp.js#stampStage06d`'s orchestrator-verified re-run/downgrade. `docs/FEATURES.md` gained a `devteam review-pr` write-up next to the existing outbound "GitHub PR integration" section, checked against `core/cli/commands/review-pr.js`'s actual `--post`/`--yes`/partial-vs-FAIL behavior before writing.
  - **Also fixes three pre-existing gaps** flagged as out-of-scope findings in #378: `core/gates/schemas/gate.schema.json`'s `track` enum was missing `"loop"`; `templates/README.md`'s "eleven templates" table hadn't been updated in several phases (now lists all 21, including `characterization-template.md`, and fixes a stale destination path); `rules/orchestrator.md`'s inline track-list comment was missing `loop`/`review-pr`/`refactor`.

  Honest scope note: no behavior changed — `gate.schema.json`'s `track` enum isn't ajv-validated at runtime in this repo (structural/meta-tested only, per `tests/schemas.test.js`), so adding `"loop"` is a documentation-accuracy fix, not a behavior change. No new tests were needed for a docs-only pass; existing `npm run consistency` checks are the acceptance gate.

### Fixed

- **Read-only reporting commands no longer invent a pipeline outside a Stagecraft project (phase-37 item 37.3, `plans/phase-37-interface-and-token-efficiency.md`).** `devteam next`/`summary`/`status`/`log`/`validate` had no initialisation check, so running any of them in a directory that was never `devteam init`-ed silently fell through to `core/config.js`'s defaults and reported a fabricated stage-01 zero-state (`▶️ run-stage — requirements (stage-01)`) as though a real pipeline were waiting there. Added one shared guard, `core/cli/project-guard.js#requireProjectContext`, wired into exactly those five commands: it detects a missing `.devteam/config.yml`, exits non-zero, and prints what's missing plus the fix (`devteam init --host <name>`); `--json` callers get a structured `{error, command, cwd, message, fix}` object instead of a silent zero-state. Commands that legitimately run without a project (`init`, `review`, `review-pr`, `hosts`, `stages`, `help`, `doctor`) are explicitly exempted and asserted so by a meta-test in `tests/project-guard.test.js`, which also checks that the guarded list is actually wired and the unguarded list isn't accidentally guarded — the split can't drift silently as commands are added.

  Honest scope note: the plan's verify-first claim pointed at `core/cli/commands/review-pr.js` as the precedent for this refusal shape, but `review-pr.js` doesn't refuse at all — it silently falls back to a review workspace when `.devteam/config.yml` is absent (phase-36 item 36.5), which is correct behavior for that command, not a refusal. The actual existing refusal-shape precedent is `core/cli/commands/stage.js`'s warn-before-printing check; this fix reuses its wording (adapted from a warn-and-continue into an exit(1) refusal, since these five commands have nothing honest left to report without a project).

### Added

- **GEPA-style offline prompt optimizer — Phase 33.4.** `scripts/prompt-optimize.js` (out-of-band, like `scripts/budget.js` — deliberately not a `devteam` command): given a `--target roles/<role>.md` or `rules/<name>.md` and a mandatory `--budget-usd`, samples the captured eval cases (`.devteam/evals/cases/`, phase-33.1) that exercise the target's stages, runs a bounded (default 4) reflective loop — one frontier-model diagnosis call per iteration proposing a revised file body — and scores each candidate against a real, unmodified `core/evals/run.js` structural baseline, `scripts/prompt-budget.js`'s byte-ceiling accounting recomputed with the candidate's size, and a budget-capped real-model dispatch subset (the candidate patched into a scratch project, never the real repo file). Candidates are kept as a Pareto frontier (pass-rate vs. token size, `arXiv:2507.19457` §3.2) rather than a single greedy winner. Output: a unified diff for exactly the target file (generated by diffing original vs. winning-candidate content directly — never a model-produced diff) plus an evidence table, printed to stdout and written under `.devteam/evals/optimize/`. **Never writes the target file itself** — the diff is for human review like any PR. A candidate whose structured response proposes a different `target_path` than requested is rejected outright (the diff-scope guard).

  Tests: `tests/prompt-optimize.test.js` — end-to-end against the checked-in fixture corpus with a scripted headless host produces a diff touching only the target file plus an evidence table; refuses to run without (or with a non-positive) `--budget-usd`, before any dispatch; a candidate proposing a different `target_path` is rejected with no diff produced; the diagnosis model is invoked exactly `--iterations` times, never more; Pareto-frontier dominance pruning.

  Honest scope note: every host adapter renders a role brief or rule file as a path pointer, never inlined content — confirmed by reading `core/adapters/markdown-host.js` and `hosts/claude-code/adapter.js` (a VERIFY-FIRST check against this item's own claim). A candidate's content therefore never changes the rendered dispatch prompt text, only what a real host CLI reads from disk when it acts on the pointer. "Structurally valid" is checked via the real `core/evals/run.js` stub path (content-invariant, a sanity floor); "within prompt budget" is checked via `scripts/prompt-budget.js`'s own byte accounting with the candidate's size substituted in (this DOES vary per candidate). Locating a role brief's installed copy inside a scratch project for the real-model subset is done generically, by byte-matching installed `.md` files against the original content — untested against any host beyond `claude-code` (verbatim rule-file installs are uniform across every host and don't need this). `--model` selects a dispatch HOST adapter (the `evals run --headless-host` convention), not a raw model id — Stagecraft always resolves an actual model indirectly via host + `routing.tiers`, so this follows that precedent rather than inventing a new flag. Cost tracking reuses `core/evals/run.js#costPreview`'s posture exactly: an input-token estimate priced off the most recently observed model for `(role, host)` in the run corpus — an estimate, never a bill, and `null` (untracked) when no prior dispatch exists to price against, same as a brand-new project's first `evals run --headless-host` sweep.

### Added

- **`prompt_pack_version` — a content-hash version for the prompt surface, recorded everywhere, comparable (Phase 33.3).** New `core/prompt-pack.js#computePromptPackVersion`: a sha256 (truncated to 12 hex chars) over every file under `roles/` + `rules/` + `templates/` (stable sorted ordering), so the version changes if and only if a role brief, rule file, or template changes. It's orchestrator-computed, never model-asserted — recorded on the gate two ways, mirroring `dispatched_tool_budget`'s dual-path convention: `core/orchestrator.js#patchGateWithPromptPackVersion` stamps it onto every workstream gate (and the merged stage gate) for headless runs; `core/gates/validator.js#autoInjectMetadata` injects it for user-driven runs. The same value is also recorded on every run-corpus row (`core/corpus.js`, phase-28 item 28.5) and every eval case (`core/evals/capture.js`, phase-33 item 33.1), read straight off the gate.

  New `devteam evals compare --pack <A> --pack <B> [--min-n <n>] [--json]` (`core/evals/compare.js`): per-stage pass-rate deltas between two `prompt_pack_version` values from the run corpus, with dispatch counts, so "did the new principal brief help?" is a query instead of a feeling. Honesty rule: a stage with fewer than `--min-n` dispatches (default 5, the same floor D5 uses in `core/corpus.js#computeStats`) on either pack is reported `refused` and never assigned a delta.

  Docs: `docs/reproducibility.md` new "Prompt-pack version (C4's consumer)" section; `docs/observability.md` corpus record example updated; `docs/reference/cli.md` regenerated.

  Tests: `tests/prompt-pack.test.js` — version changes iff any file's content or path under roles/rules/templates changes, unaffected by files outside those three directories, stable across repeated calls. Propagation, added to the existing suites for each stamping/read site: `tests/orchestrator.test.js` (headless workstream + merged-gate stamping, idempotent — a pre-existing value is never overwritten — and the fire-and-forget contract), `tests/gate-validator.test.js` (the validator's auto-injection for user-driven gates), `tests/corpus.test.js` (a corpus row via `recordDispatch`, `null` when the gate has none), `tests/evals-capture.test.js` (a captured eval case). `tests/evals-compare.test.js` — per-stage deltas on a fixture corpus, the minimum-n refusal (default and custom `--min-n`), stages that only appear for one pack, an empty corpus, and the CLI's `--pack`/`--json` wiring including the "exactly two `--pack` values" guard.

  Honest scope note: `prompt_pack_version` hashes this framework installation's own bundled `roles/`/`rules/`/`templates/` directories, not a target project's installed `.devteam/{rules,templates}/` copy — those can diverge if a project hand-edits its installed rules (`core/adapters/base-install.js`'s `installRules`/`installTemplates` skip existing files by default), and role briefs are rendered into per-host formats with no single installed `roles/` directory to hash consistently across hosts (see `base-install.js`'s header comment). The version therefore answers "did the framework's own prompt surface change" reliably; it does not detect a project's local customization drifting from what it was installed with.

### Added

- Add `devteam prototype build <id>` as an explicit host-run step for
  prototype packets. The command runs the packet's `build-prompt.md` through a
  headless host, records the build in `prototype.json`, and keeps output in
  `pipeline/prototypes/<id>/workspace/` by default.
- Add `--apply-to-project` for the deliberate escape hatch where prototype code
  should touch the project root. Prototype builds remain pre-SDLC learning
  records, not gate evidence.

- **Prototype mode workflow.** Add `devteam prototype start|note|promote`
  for fast pre-SDLC learning loops under `pipeline/prototypes/<id>/`,
  including feedback capture, promotion handoff into normal Stagecraft tracks,
  documentation across the operator guides, and openai-compat coverage for
  allowed prototype packet writes. *Honest scope note:* prototype packets are
  committable learning records, not gates, sign-off evidence, or deploy
  evidence.

- Add `devteam prototype`, a pre-SDLC workflow for fast prototype packets, feedback capture, and explicit promotion into normal Stagecraft tracks.

### Added

- **Mechanical red-team floor for stage-04c — Phase 31.2.** Red-team's adversarial pass stayed 100% model self-report: a gate claiming `findings_count: 0` was believed, in the one stage whose job is distrust. `core/verify/redteam-floor.js` adds four orchestrator-run checks post-dispatch: (a) `npm audit --json` (or `pipeline.verify.dependency_audit_command` override; explicit `null` disables) — a network-unavailable audit records `skipped: "offline"`, never a pass; (b) the existing `core/hooks/secret-scan.js` reused as a library over the changed-file set (`pipeline/changed-files.txt`, falling back to `git diff --name-only HEAD`); (c) semgrep, only when the project already has a semgrep config file **and** the binary is already on PATH — devteam never installs either; (d) a lockfile delta (new/removed top-level `package-lock.json` entries) against the previous stage-04c attempt, read from `core/gates/archive.js`'s per-attempt archives, so a first attempt records a baseline snapshot rather than a false empty diff. `core/verify/stamp.js#stampStage04c` merges these into the gate: `findings_count := max(model_reported, mechanical)`, `severity_breakdown` is raised per-band the same way, and any mechanical `high`/`critical` finding is appended to `must_address_before_peer_review` with `source: "mechanical"` — which pushes a blocker onto the gate, so `finalizeStamp`'s existing PASS/WARN→FAIL override fires exactly like every other stamped stage. Rather than re-implementing the `pipeline/context.md` blockers-injection, `core/gates/validator.js#injectRedTeamBlockers` is now exported and called directly from `stampStage04c` so a mechanically-forced FAIL surfaces to the next build re-run the same way a model-authored FAIL always has. `stage-04c` joins `STAMPABLE_STAGES` (`devteam verify stage-04c` now works) using the existing single-workstream dispatch path in `core/orchestrator.js` — no orchestrator wiring changes were needed there.

  Tests: `tests/verify-stamp-04c.test.js` — a seeded critical dependency vulnerability flips a model-PASS gate to FAIL; an offline dependency audit records `skipped: "offline"` and does not affect gate status; explicit-disable and non-Node-project skips are recorded with distinct reasons; missing semgrep config skips honestly; model `findings_count: 0` + 2 mechanical (medium-severity) secret-scan findings merges to `2` without forcing FAIL (severity floor, not count floor, drives the forced-blocker path); a model-reported count already above the mechanical count is never lowered; missing/first-attempt lockfile-diff cases; every tool's stamp record carries `{ran, skipped, reason, findings}`.

  Honest scope note: **[verify-first] finding** — the plan item's claim that a "polyglot equivalent" of `npm audit` already exists via the Phase 19/PR #264 suite-detection does not hold; that work (`core/verify/runner.js`) discovers *test* commands (Node/pytest/go test) only, with no dependency-audit/SCA analog for non-Node ecosystems (no pip-audit/govulncheck wiring). The dependency-audit check therefore only runs for Node projects (`package.json` present) or via the `dependency_audit_command` override; other project types record an explicit skip rather than guessing at unvetted tooling. The lockfile-delta check only understands `package-lock.json` (npm lockfileVersion 1–3), matching the same Node-first scope. Semgrep is scoped to the changed-file set (not a full-repo scan) to keep the mechanical floor time-boxed. The lockfile-delta's "previous attempt" lookup reuses `core/gates/archive.js`, which only archives a stage-04c attempt when it was `FAIL` — a `PASS` attempt is never archived, so a diff run after an unbroken PASS streak has no prior snapshot to compare against and records a fresh baseline rather than a true delta. A dedicated snapshot store (independent of FAIL-only archiving) would close this gap but is out of scope for this item.

### Added

- **Opt-in Reflector pass — phase-30 item 30.3 (`docs/pattern-learning.md`).** `learning.reflector: true` in `.devteam/config.yml` (default `false`) makes the driver dispatch one extra headless call after a *clean* `pipeline-complete` — never on a halt — routed like any role (`routing.roles.reflector`, falling back to `routing.default_host`) so it can go to a cheaper model than the build agents. `core/learning/reflector.js` builds the dispatch prompt from the new `roles/reflector.md` brief plus embedded JSON (this run's gate summaries, `run-log.jsonl` events with heartbeats stripped, and the current promoted-pattern set — the model gets no live tool access to re-read them) and spawns the resolved host's headless command directly (same one-off-dispatch shape as `core/a11y-fixer.js`, not a stage dispatch — there is no gate footer telling the model to write a gate, because this isn't one). Output must be a single JSON object matching the new `candidates-delta` schema (`core/gates/schemas/learning/candidates-delta.schema.json`, deliberately filed under a `learning/` subdirectory so it isn't picked up by the stage↔schema 1:1 sweeps in `contract.test.js`/`scripts/consistency.js`), hand-validated by `core/learning/validate-candidates-delta.js` (no ajv dependency, matching this repo's existing `core/gates/validator.js` convention). Valid `new_candidates` — including the new `tier: "positive"`, which gate-derived collection structurally cannot produce — land in the same observations store via the new `patterns.ingestReflectorCandidates()`, tagged `source: "reflector"` and fingerprint-deduped exactly like gate-derived observations, so a reflector proposal can reinforce an existing candidate's `pattern_key` instead of always minting a new one. `counter_adjustments`/`dedup_merges` are advisory-only — logged in full as a `reflector-proposal` run-log event for an operator to act on through the existing `devteam patterns demote`/`promote` flow, never auto-applied. Malformed output (invalid JSON, or JSON failing schema validation) is discarded *in full* — one `reflector-output-malformed` event, nothing partially ingested. Any dispatch failure (no adapter, no `headlessCommand`, non-zero exit, timeout) is the same fire-and-forget contract as pattern auto-collection: one `reflector-dispatch-failed` event, never fails the run. Role brief registered in `core/roles.js` (`ROLE_TOOLS.reflector`, read-only: `Read, Glob, Grep`) and `hosts/claude-code/adapter.js` (`ROLE_FRONTMATTER.reflector`, `model: haiku`). Docs: `docs/pattern-learning.md` (new "Reflector (ACE-lite)" section, citing Zhang et al. arXiv:2510.04618, plus an Open Decisions/history update). Tests: `tests/reflector.test.js` — `validateCandidatesDelta` unit coverage; an end-to-end scripted-headless-output run lands a `source: "reflector"` candidate and logs the proposal event; invalid-JSON and schema-invalid outputs are each discarded whole with exactly one malformed-output event and zero observations written; disabled-by-default runs never invoke the reflector and emit no `reflector-*` events; a throwing `runReflector` is caught and logged without failing the run (fire-and-forget contract); `roles/reflector.md` stays under a single-page byte budget. Honest scope note: `counter_adjustments` and `dedup_merges` are recorded for audit but have no dedicated review-surface UI yet (no CLI command reads them back out) — they're visible today only via `run-log.jsonl`; auto-promotion remains explicitly out of scope, per the design doc's "the Curator is the existing human promote flow."

### Fixed

- **Non-`acp` hosts' post-hoc write-audit was blind to the subject in review mode — phase-36 item 36.6 follow-up (`plans/phase-36-external-review-mode.md`).** In review mode (`ctx.externalReviewMode`), the existing post-hoc write-audit for `codex`/`antigravity`/`omnigent`/`openai-compat` snapshotted `ctx.cwd` (the review workspace) before and after a dispatch, never `ctx.processCwd` (the subject under review) — so it structurally could not see a write into the subject, and typically no-op'd entirely since the workspace isn't a git repository. `core/adapters/headless.js` and `hosts/omnigent/adapter.js` now run a second, independent snapshot/audit pass against `ctx.processCwd` when review mode is active and the subject differs from the workspace, treating any new path there as a violation unconditionally (`subject:<path>`, mirroring `hosts/acp/permissions.js`'s own rule that `codeRoot` is unconditionally read-only) — flipping the gate to `FAIL` with a named blocker. This is detection, not prevention: it cannot stop the write, only report it after the fact, and it inherits the write-audit's existing blind spots (no-ops on a non-git subject; can't see a re-edit of a file already dirty before the review started). Guarded to never double-audit when `ctx.processCwd === ctx.cwd`, and to stay a no-op for prototype mode (`ctx.processCwd` set without `ctx.externalReviewMode`).

  Five new tests (`tests/write-audit.test.js`, `tests/omnigent-adapter.test.js`) cover: detection when the workspace isn't a git repo but the subject is; no detection without `externalReviewMode` (prototype-mode parity); and no double-audit when `codeRoot === stateRoot`. Confirmed to fail without the fix.

  `docs/external-review.md`'s per-host enforcement table, `README.md`'s `--host acp` note, and this plan's 36.6 entry are updated to describe the new three-tier picture precisely: `acp` prevents, `codex`/`antigravity`/`omnigent`/`openai-compat` now detect, `claude-code` still gets neither (a known, separately-tracked gap — `--state-dir` for claude-code, phase-36's own Out-of-scope list).

  Honest scope note: `claude-code`'s hook-discovery gap in review mode is unchanged by this fix — it is out of scope for this phase by the plan's own design, not an oversight here.

### Added

- **`review-only` track + artifact-tolerant readFirst — phase-35 item 35.1 (`plans/phase-35-existing-codebase-mode.md`).** Reviews code that already exists — a brownfield repo, an inherited module — instead of assuming the intent→code direction every other track takes. `devteam run --track review-only [--scope <path>]` dispatches `security-review` → `red-team` → `peer-review` only: no requirements, design, build, sign-off, or deploy. `--scope <path>` (repeatable) narrows the review to a subtree; it renders as a "Scope: ..." prompt line and lands on the gate (`scope: [...]`) for audit, both only when passed (every other track's prompts/gates are unaffected).

  **Soft readFirst.** `core/pipeline/stages.js`'s pipeline-artifact `readFirst` entries for `security-review`, `red-team`, `peer-review` (including the 31.3 adversarial override), and `verification-beyond-tests` are now `{path, optional: true}` — the same form 31.3/G3 already established for `by-reviewer.md`/`production-feedback.md`, extended rather than duplicated into a parallel array. `core/orchestrator.js#buildDescriptor` now backs `optional: true` with a real filesystem check (`existsForReadFirst`, glob-aware for `pipeline/pr-*.md`) instead of the pre-35 behavior of always rendering the entry annotated `"(if present)"`: an absent optional path is omitted from the `## Read first` section entirely, a present one renders plainly (indistinguishable from a required entry). No `cwd` in scope (a preview/eval-replay path with nothing to check against) fails open — assume present, matching every pre-35 caller. This closes the "review stages instructed to read files that don't exist, degrading instead of failing" gap the phase exists to close.

  `security-review`'s `conditionalOn: {stage: "stage-04a", ...}` already treats a missing prerequisite gate as "run it anyway" (existing `core/orchestrator.js` behavior, not new here) — since `review-only` never dispatches `stage-04a`, security-review runs unconditionally on this track, same as red-team and peer-review.

  Tests: `tests/review-only-track.test.js` — optional-entry omission/inclusion (plain path, glob, no-cwd fallback); full-track byte-identical regression for all four touched stages (rendered prompt identical to a hand-authored plain-string-readFirst copy when every artifact is present); `devteam run --track review-only` walks to `pipeline-complete` on a fixture with **no `pipeline/` directory at all**; no dispatched `## Read first` section mentions a nonexistent artifact; `verify-chain` passes on the 3-stage track; `--scope` reaching the prompt and the gate, and its absence changing nothing on every pre-35 track. `tests/compact-qa-fold.test.js`'s `verification-beyond-tests` snapshot and `tests/tracks.test.js`'s "build is in every track" assertion updated for the new readFirst shape and the one track that legitimately never builds anything.

  Honest scope note: item 2's plan text called for gate-schema conditionals making "AC-referencing fields null-permitted when track: review-only" (the 29.4 stage-06x precedent). **Verify-first check found this premise false**: none of `security-review`/`red-team`/`peer-review`'s gate schemas (`core/gates/schemas/stage-04b|04c|05.schema.json`) have any required field that references acceptance criteria — the AC↔Scenario↔test contract lives entirely in stage-01/03b, neither of which this track includes. No schema conditional was added; there is nothing to relax. `core/gates/schemas/gate.schema.json`'s `track` enum gained `"review-only"` (it was already missing `"loop"` — pre-existing drift, not fixed here, out of scope). Peer-review sizing on `review-only` falls through to `PEER_REVIEW_SIZING.full` (the 4-area matrix, 2 approvals) rather than a scoped single-reviewer variant — unlike `nano`/`loop` there's no single-workstream build to size the review to, and an arbitrary existing subtree can span every area.

### Added

- **`devteam review-pr <number|url>` works with no checkout and no initialised project — phase-36 item 36.5 (`plans/phase-36-external-review-mode.md`).** Running from an already-initialised project (`.devteam/config.yml` present in `--cwd`) is byte-identical to before. Otherwise, `review-pr` now materializes the PR into a 36.3 review workspace (`core/review-workspace.js`) instead of the invoking directory: `gh pr view`/`gh pr diff` are fetched once, then written into `<workspace>/pipeline/review-input/` and dispatched exactly as the in-place path does, with `pipeline/gates/stage-05*.json` and `pipeline/code-review/by-*.md` landing under the workspace. The workspace is derived from the PR's own URL (`core/review-workspace.js#slugForIdentity`/`resolveWorkspacePathForIdentity`, new — `slugForSubject`'s `path.resolve()` would otherwise resolve a non-path identity against the invoking cwd, making the slug vary by invocation directory instead of by PR), so repeat reviews of the same PR reuse the same workspace regardless of where the command is run from. `--workspace <path>` overrides the derived path, mirroring `devteam review`.

  **No clone, ever, in this path.** The diff *is* the subject — `core/review-workspace.js#createReviewWorkspace()`/`writeSubjectManifest()` now accept `subjectPath: null` (required as an explicit key, not merely omitted, so a caller can't silently forget it) plus directly-supplied `remote`/`commitSha`/`pr` fields instead of shelling `git` against a path that doesn't exist; `subject.json` records `subject_path: null` and a `pr: {number, url, title}` block in that case. Dispatch sets `ctx.externalReviewMode: true` and a new `ctx.noCodeRoot: true` (threaded through `core/orchestrator.js#runStage()` exactly like 36.3's `processCwd`/`externalReviewMode` — unset on every other caller, so byte-identical there).

  **Fixed a real gap this surfaced in `hosts/acp/adapter.js`.** `hosts/acp/permissions.js#findWriteViolation` already special-cased a falsy `codeRoot` for this exact scenario (comment naming 36.5 was already in the file from an earlier item), but `adapter.js`'s own `codeRoot: processCwd` — where `processCwd = ctx.processCwd || ctx.cwd` — never actually produced a falsy `codeRoot`: with `ctx.processCwd` unset, `codeRoot` silently became `ctx.cwd` itself (i.e. `codeRoot === stateRoot`), which `findWriteViolation` reads as "every write target is inside the subject" and denies outright. `ctx.noCodeRoot` is the fix: `codeRoot` is `null` only when explicitly opted into, leaving the existing fail-closed default (deny when `processCwd` is merely omitted, protecting against a review-mode dispatch that forgot to set up a real code/state split) completely intact for every other caller.

  **Publishing safety is untouched.** `--post` stays opt-in, the confirmation still throws (not a silent no-op) and actually stops the command, non-interactive still requires `--yes`, and a partial/incomplete dispatch still refuses to post even with `--yes` — re-verified with a parallel copy of every 35.2 publishing-safety test run against the new no-project-directory path, plus the original in-place suite re-run unmodified.

  Tests: `tests/host-acp.test.js` — an end-to-end `adapter.invoke()` case proving a write under `ctx.cwd` matching `allowedWrites` is allowed with `ctx.noCodeRoot: true` and no `ctx.processCwd` (fails without the `adapter.js` fix), plus a sibling case proving the same paths are still denied when `ctx.noCodeRoot` is merely omitted (guards against re-loosening the pre-existing fail-closed test). `tests/review-workspace.test.js` — `slugForIdentity`/`resolveWorkspacePathForIdentity` stability and cwd-independence, `createReviewWorkspace`/`writeSubjectManifest` with `subjectPath: null`, the required-key throw, and `runStage()` threading `ctx.noCodeRoot`. `tests/review-pr.test.js` — a scripted `gh` stub drives `devteam review-pr <url> --json` from a plain tmpdir with no `.devteam/` and `STAGECRAFT_REVIEWS_DIR` pinned to a fresh tmpdir: asserts the workspace path reported in `--json`, that nothing lands under the invoking cwd, that `subject.json` records the PR's identity, that a repeat run reuses the same workspace, and re-runs the non-interactive-refusal / `--post --yes` / partial-review-never-posts cases from 35.2 against this path. The full original `tests/review-pr.test.js` suite (in-place mode) passes unmodified.

  Honest scope note: no `--host`/adversarial-mode support in workspace mode — the workspace's generated `.devteam/config.yml` pins `routing.default_host: claude-code` and sets no `review.mode`, so a bare-PR review from an uninitialised directory always runs single-reviewer panel mode; adversarial mode and a different host still require an initialised project (`--cwd` pointed at one) until a future item exposes equivalent flags, mirroring `devteam review`'s `--host`. A bare PR *number* (as opposed to a full URL) still requires being run inside an actual checkout of the repo — `gh` infers the target repo from the invoking directory's git remote, which a URL doesn't need; this is a pre-existing `gh` behavior, not something this item's code decides, and isn't newly broken by it. Also found, and left as documentation only: `core/cli/commands/review-pr.js`'s pre-36.5 precondition check (`.devteam/config.yml` absent) never actually refused — it printed a cosmetic stderr warning and then dispatched into the invoking `cwd` regardless (no downstream guard existed either, confirmed by reading `core/config.js#loadConfig`'s silent-defaults fallback and `core/orchestrator.js#runStageHeadless`). The in-place code path is left exactly as it was; the new workspace routing described above is this item's actual fix for that gap, not a hardening of the old warning.

### Added

- **`devteam review-pr <number|url>` — phase-35 item 35.2 (`plans/phase-35-existing-codebase-mode.md`).** Review an inbound GitHub PR with the existing reviewer/critic machinery instead of hand-assembling a brief. Materializes the PR via `gh pr view`/`gh pr diff` into `pipeline/review-input/` (`pr.md` — title/body as the stated intent; `diff.patch` — the unified diff; `changed-files.md` — the changed-file list), then dispatches `peer-review` (stage-05) against it on a new `review-pr` track: a single "reviewer" workstream in panel mode (`PEER_REVIEW_SIZING["review-pr"]`, sized like `nano` rather than `review-only`'s four-area matrix — a PR is already one bounded, diffed unit of change), reviewer-then-critic when `review.mode: adversarial` (31.3) — that override is config-driven exactly as it is everywhere else, no new flag needed. Output is a normal stage-05 gate (`pipeline/gates/stage-05.json`, or the merged adversarial gate) plus `pipeline/code-review/by-*.md`, dispatched through the unmodified `runStage`/`runStageHeadless`/`mergeWorkstreamGates` path — 35.1's soft-readFirst already made `peer-review` tolerant of a repo with no `pipeline/brief.md`/`design-spec.md`, and three new optional `readFirst` entries (`pipeline/review-input/{pr.md,diff.patch,changed-files.md}`) were added to both the panel and adversarial-override shapes so the materialized PR is only ever mentioned in the prompt when it actually exists.

  `gh` auth/error handling is reused verbatim from `scripts/pr-publish.js` (the only existing place that shells to `gh`) — `gh`/`ensureGh` are now exported from there rather than duplicating a second pattern.

  **Publishing is opt-in and gated.** Default is local-only: findings land on disk, nothing is sent anywhere. `--post` publishes the combined `by-reviewer.md`(`+by-critic.md`) content as a `gh pr review --comment` PR review comment: it prints the exact payload (to stderr, so `--post --json` still emits one clean JSON object on stdout), then requires interactive confirmation — auto-confirmed with `--yes`, required in a non-interactive context (no TTY) since there's no one to prompt. Refuses outright, before ever building a payload, when the dispatch was partial (a workstream timed out, exited non-zero, wrote no gate, or the adversarial merge failed) — a FAIL/ESCALATE gate that *completed* is still postable (that's the point — post the findings), only an *incomplete* dispatch is refused.

  Tests (`tests/review-pr.test.js`): a scripted `gh` stub + a `DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND` writer script (mirrors `tests/replay.test.js`'s pattern) drive a full `devteam review-pr` CLI invocation end to end — materialization content, panel-mode single-workstream gate, a bare-number vs. full-URL positional argument, adversarial mode's reviewer+critic merge, `--post` refusing (and posting nothing) in a non-interactive context without `--yes`, `--post --yes` actually calling `gh pr review` with the expected body, missing `gh` giving an actionable error instead of a stack trace, and a partial dispatch (`DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND=true`, no gate written) refusing `--post` even with `--yes`.

  Docs: `docs/tracks.md` gained a `## The review-pr track` section and its generated stage matrix regenerated (`scripts/generate-tracks-matrix.js` now marks review-pr's peer-review `✓ˢ`, same annotation as nano's single-reviewer scoping); `docs/concepts.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`, `rules/pipeline-tracks.md`, `rules/gates-core.md` updated from eight to nine tracks; `docs/reference/cli.md` regenerated (`npm run docs:generate`) to include the new command.

  Honest scope note: `core/gates/schemas/gate.schema.json`'s `track` enum gained `"review-pr"` — it was already missing `"loop"` (pre-existing drift flagged, not fixed, by the 35.1 changelog; still not fixed here). `rules/pipeline-tracks.md`'s per-track approvals table predates `review-only` and still doesn't list either `review-only` or `review-pr` — flagged inline with a pointer to `docs/tracks.md` rather than expanded, to avoid widening this item's diff into an unrelated table rewrite. `review-pr`'s single "reviewer" workstream reuses the exact role name the 31.3 adversarial override already treats specially (`by-reviewer.md` → `applyAdversarialReviewerFile`) purely as a filename-matching side effect of `core/adapters/headless.js`'s post-dispatch approval-derivation fallback — harmless (the model's own direct gate write to `pipeline/gates/stage-05.json`, per every stage's "## Gate to write" contract, is what the orchestrator actually reads) but it does mean a redundant `stage-05.reviewer.json` gets written alongside the real gate on panel-mode runs, exactly as nano's `backend`-named single workstream already leaves behind an unused `stage-05.backend.json` today. Not fixed here — pre-existing characteristic of the single-workstream shape, not something 35.2 introduces.

### Added

- **Review workspace + dispatch plumbing — phase-36 item 36.3 (`plans/phase-36-external-review-mode.md`).** `core/review-workspace.js` creates `~/.stagecraft/reviews/<slug>/` (`--workspace <path>` overrides), where `<slug>` is the subject's basename slugified plus a short stable hash of its absolute path (collision-safe across two checkouts sharing a basename; stable across repeat reviews of the same path). The workspace mirrors the parts of `devteam init` a review needs — `pipeline/gates/`, a minimal `.devteam/config.yml` pinning `routing.default_host`/`pipeline.default_track`, and the given host adapter's own `install()` for role prompts/rules/templates/skills (ACP's `.acp/stagecraft/{roles,skills}` per `hosts/acp/capabilities.json`) — reusing the existing adapter contract rather than hand-rolling a second install path. `.devteam/patterns`, `corpus`, and `evals` are deliberately not pre-created: every consumer already `mkdirSync(..., {recursive:true})`s on first write, same as a freshly-`init`'d project.

  `createReviewWorkspace()` also writes `subject.json`, recording the subject's absolute path, its `git remote get-url origin` (null if none/not a git repo), and the commit SHA at workspace-creation time — so a future attestation pass can name **what was reviewed**, not what was produced (34.2's `resolveSubjectCommits()` reads `ctx.cwd`'s own git history, which is empty for a review workspace; wiring that read is left to a later item, not this one — see honest scope note).

  **Orchestrator plumbing.** `core/orchestrator.js#runStage()` now threads `opts.processCwd`/`opts.externalReviewMode` onto `ctx`, the same passthrough `opts.scope` already gets (phase-35 item 35.1); `core/driver.js#run()`'s single `_runStageHeadless()` call site threads them the same way. Both default to `null`/`false` when absent, so every existing caller is byte-identical. This is the wiring `hosts/acp/adapter.js` and `core/adapters/render-helpers.js#resolveFrameworkPath` (36.1/36.2) were already waiting on — `ctx.externalReviewMode` was previously set by no orchestrator path at all.

  New env var `STAGECRAFT_REVIEWS_DIR` (default `~/.stagecraft/reviews/`), same precedent as `STAGECRAFT_ORG_MEMORY_DIR` (`core/memory/index.js`) — see `docs/reference/environment-variables.md`.

  **The test that matters** (`tests/review-workspace.test.js`): a real dispatch through `hosts/acp`'s adapter (driven by the existing `tests/fixtures/acp-stub-agent.js` scripted agent — no network, no real model) against a `createReviewWorkspace()`-built workspace and a genuinely separate fixture subject repo, with `ctx.processCwd` = subject and `ctx.cwd` = workspace. The subject tree (file list + content hash, `.gitignore`/`AGENTS.md` included) is snapshotted before and after and asserted completely unchanged — both immediately after the real dispatch and again after the rest of the `review-only` track completes. Also covers: gates/logs landing under the workspace (not the subject); `subject.json`'s path/remote/SHA fields, including the no-git-repo fallback; `runStage()`/`driver.run()` opts passthrough (present and byte-identical-when-absent); and `devteam verify-chain --cwd <workspace>` passing against the resulting chain.

  Honest scope note: this item wires the workspace and the ctx split; it does not yet flip `ctx.externalReviewMode` on for any real CLI command (that's item 36.4's `devteam review <path>`) or wire `subject.json` into `core/evidence/attestation.js`'s subject resolution (flagged above, left for a later item since 36.3's own acceptance criteria doesn't require it). `driver.js` currently has exactly one `_runStageHeadless()` call site, so the passthrough only needed one edit; if a future item adds a second dispatch path, thread the same two fields there.

### Added

- Added per-workstream start/finish progress for `devteam run`, including host, outcome, elapsed time, and gate/log pointers.
- Added `active_workstreams` and `last_workstream` to run state and `devteam status --verbose`.
- Expanded `devteam run --watch` with active workstreams, the last settled workstream, and transcript/gate pointers.

### Added

- Added deterministic run right-sizing: active workstream candidates for Stage 01 confirmation, auditable applicability skips, expected workstream counts, and `pipeline.right_sizing: false` opt-out. (The high-confidence-only auto-track selection this originally shipped with is superseded before release by `assess-by-default.md` below — it now runs at any confidence and is audited via `pipeline/track.json`.)

### Added

- Emit a `devteam run` plan preview, and durable `run-plan` event, with effective track, stage counts, configured skips, conditional stages, and base workstream count before dispatch.

### Added

- Added per-host workstream concurrency limits, queue-wait telemetry, critical-path queue reporting, and retry reason/backoff fields for transient no-gate dispatch retries.

### Added

- **docs(analysis):** ACP read-scope evidence review (`plans/acp-read-scope.md`), item 36.0. Ran the real `@agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp` agent (0.64.2, real model, real cost) against a session cwd with an unrelated absolute-path target outside it, plain and symlinked, plus a denial control. Finding: the agent's own filesystem access is not sandboxed to the ACP session cwd — reads succeed unsandboxed either way, gated by one `session/request_permission` round-trip that `hosts/acp/permissions.js`'s `WRITE_KINDS` (`edit`/`delete`/`move` only) already auto-allows for `read`/`execute`-kind calls today. Recommendation for 36.2: render framework paths as absolute paths into `stateRoot`, no permission-layer change required.

  Honest scope note: report-only spike, no production code changed. Tool choice (Read vs. shell `cat`) was observed to be non-deterministic across turns for the same prompt — both succeeded identically once permission was granted, so this doesn't change the recommendation, but it means a single run isn't a guarantee of which tool a given dispatch will use.

### Added

- **Orchestrator-verified evidence for stage-06d (verification-beyond-tests) — Phase 35.3.** `STAMPABLE_STAGES` was `{stage-03b, stage-04a, stage-04c, stage-06}` — stage-06d, the stage whose entire value is verification depth, was 100% model self-report: a gate claiming `methods_attempted: ["property"]` was believed with no orchestrator check. `core/verify/stamp.js#stampStage06d` closes that gap: for every method the model claims with a bare tag (`"property"`, `"mutation"`, `"formal"` — not an already-honest `attempted_but_blocked:*`), the orchestrator tries to produce real executable evidence before the claim is accepted.
  - **Property-based** (`core/verify/property.js`): detects fast-check (JS/TS, package.json dependency), hypothesis (Python, requirements.txt/pyproject.toml), or proptest (Rust, Cargo.toml) — never installs any of them. Runs the property tests found under `pipeline.verify.property.paths` (default `src/tests/property/`) and parses the runner's own summary for an executed-property count and pass/fail. fast-check runs via Node's built-in test runner (`node --test --test-reporter=tap`); a genuine counterexample (`Property failed after N tests`) FAILs the gate on orchestrator evidence, and a claim backed by zero executed properties downgrades to `attempted_but_blocked:property`.
  - **Mutation**: reuses the phase-31.4 runner (`core/verify/mutation.js#runMutationGate`) directly — no second implementation. Honors the model's own pre-declared `threshold` (the schema calls this "audit-grade: prevents goal-post moving"); a re-run score below it FAILs the gate, unlike stage-06's smoke gate where below-threshold is advisory by default.
  - **Formal** (`core/verify/formal.js`): presence-and-exit-code only. TLA+/Alloy/Lean/Coq output is too varied to parse reliably, so the orchestrator stamps `{tool, ran, exit_code}` and nothing more — a non-zero exit raises a warning for human triage, never an automatic FAIL. No manifest auto-detection (no single signal the way fast-check has one); the project declares its check via `pipeline.verify.formal.command`.

  `stage-06d` joins `STAMPABLE_STAGES` (`devteam verify stage-06d` now works) using the existing single-workstream dispatch path in `core/orchestrator.js` — no orchestrator wiring changes were needed there. A method the model never claimed (a legitimate `methods_skipped` entry, or an already-honest `attempted_but_blocked:*`) is left untouched; the orchestrator verifies claims, it doesn't invent new ones. `.devteam/config.yml` gains commented `pipeline.verify.property` / `pipeline.verify.formal` documentation alongside the existing `mutation` block.

  Tests: `tests/verify-stamp-06d.test.js` — a fixture with a real fast-check-shaped counterexample FAILs on orchestrator evidence while the method claim stays confirmed (it did run); a model claiming `property` with zero executed properties downgrades to `attempted_but_blocked:property` with the model's original claim preserved under `_orchestrator_stamped.runs.property.model_claim`; absent toolchain (no fast-check/hypothesis/proptest signal) records the same downgrade; mutation claims below/above the model's declared threshold FAIL/PASS respectively using a `command`-override fixture (mirroring `tests/verify-stamp-mutation.test.js`); formal claims downgrade when unconfigured, stamp `{tool, ran, exit_code}` on exit 0 without touching the model's other formal fields, and warn-without-failing on a non-zero exit; a legitimately skipped method and an already-honest `attempted_but_blocked:*` claim are left untouched; unit tests for `detectRunner`/`parseOutput`/`buildCommand`/`walkFiles` (property.js) and `resolveFormalConfig` (formal.js) against canned tool output. No pre-existing `tests/verification-beyond-tests.test.js` test required changes — that suite covers the model-facing schema/skill/template/prompt surface, which this change doesn't touch.

  Honest scope note: only the fast-check/Node-built-in-test-runner path is integration-tested end-to-end (via a `command`-override fixture, following `tests/verify-stamp-mutation.test.js`'s existing pattern — this repo carries no real fast-check dependency, matching the "never install" doctrine). hypothesis (pytest-style summary) and proptest (`cargo test`-style summary) detection and output-parsing are unit-tested against canned strings only, since this repo has no Python or Rust toolchain to run them against for real. Formal-method exit codes are intentionally never interpreted as pass/fail by the orchestrator — a non-zero exit could mean a genuine counterexample or a tool/config error, and the plan explicitly asks for presence-and-exit-code evidence only; this is a deliberate, narrower guarantee than mutation's, not an oversight.

### Added

- **`refactor` track — behavior-preservation for structural changes (phase-35 item 35.5, `plans/phase-35-existing-codebase-mode.md`).** `devteam run --track refactor` dispatches the same 3-stage shape as `nano` (`build` → `peer-review` → `qa`, single-reviewer sizing) but with a different bar: no new acceptance criteria, the existing test suite must pass **unchanged**, and a surviving mutant is treated as evidence the refactor didn't preserve behavior.
  - **Build (stage-04)** gets a `trackOverrides.refactor` on `core/pipeline/stages.js`: the objective and template (`templates/characterization-template.md`) swap to a CHARACTERIZATION brief — document current behavior (inputs/outputs, edge cases, error handling, existing test coverage) *before* touching structure. Gate shape is unchanged from `nano`.
  - **QA (stage-06)** gets a matching `trackOverrides.refactor`: `all_acceptance_criteria_met` and `criterion_to_test_mapping_is_one_to_one` are seeded `null` (no brief.md exists on this track, same as `nano` — `core/verify/stamp.js#checkAcceptanceCriteria` already treats a missing brief as not-applicable and leaves the field untouched, so `null` is the honest initial value rather than `false`). `core/gates/schemas/stage-06.schema.json` widens both fields' type to `["boolean", "null"]`.
  - **The 31.4 mutation smoke gate defaults to *enabled*** on the `refactor` track only (`core/verify/mutation.js#defaultMutationEnabledFor`) — every other track keeps its pre-35.5 opt-in-off default. An explicit `pipeline.verify.mutation.enabled` in config always wins over the track default, in either direction. `core/verify/stamp.js#stampStage06` now threads `gate.track` into `runMutationGate`.
  - `PEER_REVIEW_SIZING.refactor` mirrors `nano`'s (single reviewer, 1 approval) — without this entry the track would silently fall back to the full 4-area matrix. `core/gates/schemas/gate.schema.json`'s `track` enum gained `"refactor"`.

  Tests: `tests/refactor-track.test.js` — track/stage-list/sizing contract identical to `nano` except the two documented differences; `build`/`qa` descriptors diverge from `nano` only on the objective/template/gate fields called out above; `resolveMutationConfig` defaults on for `refactor` and off for every other track (explicit config always wins); `stampStage06` integration proving the mutation gate actually runs by default on `refactor` and stays skipped on `nano` with the same unconfigured verify block; a behavior-changing edit (existing test suite failing) FAILs stage-06 on `refactor` via the ordinary test-command blocker — no new mechanism needed, it's the same check every other track already has; `--track refactor` walks `build → peer-review → qa` to `pipeline-complete` on a fixture with a complete, correctly-null audit trail.

  Honest scope note: the plan's "AC-mapping gate fields null-permitted as in 35.1" language describes a JSON-Schema `if/then` conditional keyed on `track`; this codebase's schemas are not ajv-validated at runtime (`tests/schemas.test.js`'s own header notes this — "avoids adding ajv as a dep"), so the schema change here is a type-widening (`["boolean", "null"]`), not an enforced per-track conditional — the actual per-track behavior lives in the `trackOverrides.refactor` gate skeleton and in `checkAcceptanceCriteria`'s pre-existing not-applicable handling. "The existing suite must pass unchanged" is enforced exactly as far as every other track already enforces "tests must pass" (the stage-06 test-command blocker) — this item does not add a mechanical check that test *files themselves* were left untouched during the refactor; that would be a further-reaching, unrequested mechanism.

### Added

- Reuse successful orchestrator-run verification commands through content-addressed receipts keyed by command, suite, purpose, workspace bytes, verify config, material env/toolchain data, and Stagecraft verifier version; failed or uncertain receipts rerun pessimistically.

### Added

- Run orchestrator-discovered verification suites with bounded concurrency, deterministic result ordering, optional resource-group exclusivity, and capped stdout/stderr capture.

## [0.9.0] — 2026-06-28

### Added

- Authenticate stage-gate chains with optional HMAC-SHA256 signatures from `DEVTEAM_SIGNING_SECRET`, with `--require-signed` and `pipeline.require_signed_gates` anti-downgrade enforcement.

### Added

- **`devteam report` — post-run HTML pipeline report.** New command that collects all pipeline data and renders a self-contained `pipeline/report.html`, opened automatically in the default browser. The report is a three-tab SPA (no external dependencies, works offline): **Summary** (status badge, progress bar, header stats, brief problem statement, clickable AC and scenario count chips), **Pipeline** (per-stage timing, dispatch counts, stage document links, workstream breakdown, blocker log), and **Documents** (all pipeline artifacts embedded inline — brief, spec, design-spec, build-plan, code reviews, ADRs, test report, accessibility/observability reports — with a sidebar navigator). Status classification reads terminal events correctly: `COMPLETED` (complete event), `HALTED` with halt-type subtitle (ceiling-halt, convergence-halt), or `INCOMPLETE` (no terminal event, waiting for manual advance). Heartbeats written after a halt no longer mask the real state. Header shows wall-clock time, total model compute time (sum of all `dispatch-observation` durations from `run-log.jsonl`), retry and stall counts, and cost. Per-stage rows show compute time and a "(N dispatches)" annotation for retried stages. Clicking a stage pill in the progress bar jumps to that row in the Pipeline tab. Stage document links (small blue pills per row) and clickable stat chips navigate directly to the relevant embedded document. `--json` emits raw `ReportData` for scripting. Documented in `help.js`, `docs/reference/cli.md`, `README.md`, and `docs/user-guide.md`.

- **OpenAI-compatible host adapter.** Adds a first-class `openai-compat` host for OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions providers, including HTTP-native tool calls, per-role model configuration, installed host prompts and skills, write-audit integration, and direct command execution for stages that require command-line verification. The command executor now parses model-provided commands into argv, rejects shell syntax and non-allowlisted executables, and keeps CI prompt-echo smoke tests explicit with `DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_TEE=1`. *Honest scope note:* command execution is not an OS sandbox; allowlisted commands still run with the invoking process's project permissions.

- **Three `active_roles` filtering bugs fixed — infinite dispatch loops on non-build and multi-role stages.** When a stage-01 gate carries `active_roles: ["backend", "platform", "qa"]` (no frontend), three interacting defects caused dispatch loops:
  1. **`inferActiveRoles` zeroed out non-build stages** (`computeDispatchPlan`): for stages like design (`roles: ["principal"]`) the intersection with build-workstream `active_roles` was empty — an empty array is truthy, so `roles` was set to `[]`, the stage dispatched zero workstreams, wrote no gate, and looped until `max-iterations`. Fix: return `null` (no filter) when the intersection is empty.
  2. **`_nextImpl` used `stageDef.roles` (unfiltered) for multi-role completion check**: after all active workstream gates were written, `stage-04.frontend.json` was absent (frontend was suppressed), so `remaining=[frontend]` was always non-empty and `next()` always returned `continue-stage` — all three active workstreams were skipped every iteration. Fix: compute `effectiveRoles` via `inferActiveRoles` before the gate-existence loop.
  3. **`summary()` showed suppressed workstreams as pending**: same root cause as (2) in the display path — `devteam plan` showed `frontend: pending` for projects with no frontend workstream. Fixed alongside (2).
  Four regression tests added to `tests/workstream-suppression.test.js` (three for `next()`, one for non-build stage dispatch).

### Fixed

- **Targeted-fix false-positive halt when blocker `file` includes a line-number suffix** (`src/backend/server.ts:5`): `blockerFiles()` now strips the `:\d+` suffix before hashing, so a genuine source-file change is correctly detected and the pipeline continues instead of escalating with `convergence-exhausted`. Closes #269.

### Fixed

- **Structural-input halt when patchItems push headless prompt over Claude Code's 4000-char goal-condition limit**: `runHeadless()` now detects prompts that exceed the limit and re-renders without `patchItems` before spawning the agent. A warning is emitted to stderr. The agent reads `pipeline/context.md` for blocker guidance instead of the inline patch block — the auto-fix mechanism already writes blockers there before dispatch, so no information is lost. Closes #277.

### Fixed

- **Spec drift false positive when AC line carries an inline backtick annotation** (e.g. `` **AC-10** `[deploy-deferred]` — … ``): `AC_LINE_RE` in `core/spec/verify.js` now optionally consumes a `` `[tag]` `` token between the AC identifier and the separator, so the drift checker extracts the correct AC count and stage-03b no longer halts with `orphan_scenarios=1`. Closes #274.

- **`spec drift: orphan_scenarios=N` false positive when brief uses numbered-list AC format fixed.** `AC_LINE_RE` in `core/spec/verify.js` accepted dash/star/plus bullet markers but not numbered-list markers (`1. **AC-N**`). When the PM agent writes ACs as a numbered list (e.g. `1. **AC-1 — HTTP endpoint exists**: ...`), no ACs were extracted from the brief; every scenario's `@AC-N` tag then referenced an ID absent from the empty brief, orphaning all scenarios and halting stage-03b with `spec drift: orphan_criteria=[none], orphan_scenarios=N`. Fix: extend the optional bullet-prefix alternation to also match `\d+\.\s+`.

### Fixed

- **Targeted-fix ghost-workstream cycle**: when a post-build stage (accessibility-audit, peer-review, QA) triggers a `fix-and-retry` for a file whose `file_ownership` points to a workstream that stage-04 never dispatched (e.g. `frontend` in a backend-only project), the driver now rejects the inferred workstream and falls back to a regular build dispatch instead of silently cycling until fix-retry budgets are exhausted. Closes #271.

- **Durable dispatch evidence (Phase 17).** Autonomous runs now retain one privacy-bounded `dispatch-observation` per non-skipped workstream, allowing `devteam evidence status` and consented bundles to measure D5 routing history without treating current or archived gate snapshots as durable evidence. Legacy projects remain readable and explicitly unready for the durable-history condition.

### Added

- Add explicit, privacy-bounded `devteam evidence accept-resolution --yes` records and
  cross-project H3 readiness for human-accepted derivable retry resolutions.

### Added

- Discover and aggregate Node, pytest, and Go test suites during orchestrator-stamped
  pre-review, QA, and repair verification while preserving explicit command overrides.

- **Rolling autonomous-run watch mode.** `devteam run --watch` renders current stage,
  dispatch elapsed time, log growth, heartbeat age, and observed stall status on an
  interactive terminal, with cursor cleanup and ANSI-free redirected-output fallback.
  This is display-only; ADR-007 Tier 2 process termination remains evidence-gated.
