   if the file is absent — one scaffold scenario per AC in `brief.md`.
2. Runs `verify()` (pure Node.js, same function `devteam spec verify` uses) to detect
   drift between `brief.md` acceptance criteria and `spec.feature` scenarios.
3. Stamps all seven spec-related gate fields with model-said vs orchestrator-observed:

| Field | Stamped value |
|-------|---------------|
| `criteria_count` | AC count from `brief.md` |
| `scenarios_count` | scenario count from `spec.feature` |
| `criteria_to_scenario_mapping` | per-criterion scenario names |
| `all_criteria_mapped` | true iff no orphan criteria or duplicates |
| `orphan_scenarios` | scenario names with no matching AC |
| `orphan_criteria` | AC IDs with no matching scenario |
| `drift` | true iff any orphan or duplicate found |

If `drift` is true or `all_criteria_mapped` is false, a blocker is appended and
the gate status flips to FAIL — same `finalizeStamp` pattern as stage-04a/06.

`"stage-03b"` added to `STAMPABLE_STAGES`.

#### `roles/pm.md` — stage-03b procedure rewritten

The pm's stage-03b procedure now:
- Reads `brief.md` for acceptance criteria (source of truth).
- Reads or writes `spec.feature` directly via the Write tool — one scenario per AC,
  each tagged `@AC-N`, with concrete Given/When/Then steps.
- Writes `pipeline/gates/stage-03b.json` with its self-assessment.
- No shell commands appear anywhere in the procedure.

A note clarifies that `devteam spec generate` and `devteam spec verify` are pipeline
shell commands the orchestrator runs on the pm's behalf after dispatch.

Rejected alternative — grant pm Bash: verification belongs to the orchestrator, not
to an agent self-certifying what the orchestrator should verify.

#### `scripts/consistency.js` — new check class `role-budget-brief`

`checkRoleBriefToolBudgetCompatibility()` scans `roles/*.md`. For each role whose
`toolBudgetFor()` result does not include Bash, any line matching
`` Run `devteam <subcommand>` `` is flagged as a violation. The check distinguishes
imperative instructions ("Run `devteam …`") from informational references
(`` `devteam …` `` without the leading "Run"), so descriptions of what the pipeline
does are not penalised. The real `roles/` tree produces zero violations after the
`pm.md` rewrite.

### Tests added

**`tests/verify-stamp.test.js`** — 6 new tests for `stampStage03b`:
- Happy path: all 7 gate fields stamped correctly, `drift=false`, status PASS.
- Drift detected: status flips to FAIL, `model_said` recorded, blocker appended.
- Scaffold generated when `spec.feature` is absent.
- Graceful skip when `brief.md` is absent (track without requirements stage).
- `model_said` vs orchestrator captured on count mismatch.
- `stamp()` dispatch round-trip for `"stage-03b"`.

**`tests/consistency-meta.test.js`** — 4 new tests for `role-budget-brief`:
- `"Run \`devteam spec generate\`"` in a no-Bash role → violation detected (exit 1).
- Same command in a role with Bash (e.g. qa) → no violation (exit 0).
- Informational reference `` `devteam spec generate` `` without "Run" → no violation.
- Real `roles/` regression guard → 0 violations after `pm.md` rewrite (exit 0).

- **Fix (6.4): de-overfit fix recipes — provenance-based blocker routing replaces regex heuristics.**
  Three overfit sources removed:

  1. **`_wsFromText` regex demoted to last-resort fallback.** Recipe routing now reads workstream attribution from blocker fields (`assigned_to`, `workstream`) directly — provenance first, regex only when no structured attribution exists (emits a WARN). A new `_wsFromProvenance()` helper encodes this priority.

  2. **`mergeWorkstreamGates` preserves workstream on object blockers.** The merge previously flattened all workstream gate blockers into a single array without recording their source. Object blockers now carry `workstream: role` when merged, enabling provenance-based routing downstream.

  3. **stage-06b recipe: backend hardcoding and demo-project comment removed.** The old recipe unconditionally routed A11Y blockers to the backend workstream (based on a demo-project CSS filename). The updated recipe routes to whichever workstream `assigned_to`/`workstream` fields identify; falls back to regex then to all build roles when blockers carry no structured attribution.

  4. **Hardcoded `["backend","frontend","platform","qa"]` fallback arrays → `_buildRoles()`.** Two last-resort fallbacks in stage-04c and stage-06d that listed build roles explicitly now derive them from the build stage definition (`getStage("build").roles`), so they stay correct if the build stage's role list changes.

  **Tests added:** frontend-owned A11Y blocker (assigned_to: frontend) routes to frontend, not backend; multi-workstream A11Y blockers clear all attributed gates; soc2 scenario (string blockers) uses general build dispatch; recipe-hygiene meta-test ensures no recipe source quotes a filename that exists only under `examples/`.

  Honest scope note: string blockers without `assigned_to` still fall back to regex then to clearing all build workstream gates — these are safe but may over-clear. Adding `assigned_to` to A11Y blockers in the gate schema is the long-term fix.

- **fix(7.1):** Git-aware consistency enumeration and meta-test isolation. `scripts/consistency.js` now uses `git ls-files -z` when `.git` is present so untracked files never block a blocking check — they emit an advisory instead. Added `--only <check-class>` flag so meta-tests run only their own check class, eliminating five-test fan-out from a single stray file. Added `PROMPT_BUDGET_FILE` env override (mirrors `CONSISTENCY_BASELINE_FILE`) so prompt-budget meta-tests write to a tempdir instead of rewriting the real file in place. The stage-05.md "known exceedance" test is now fixture-based via `--root` + `--only file-size-ceiling`, so trimming that file in Phase 8 won't break the suite. CI workflow permanently creates `docs/SCRATCH-ci-probe.md` before `npm test` as a regression guard; this step was red on the pre-fix main and is green after. (plans/phase-7-test-harness.md §7.1)

## fix/ci-signal (Phase 7.2) — CI signal quality

### 7.2.1 — Tighten onboarding smoke assertion
- The CI onboarding smoke step now captures `devteam stage` stdout+stderr and
  asserts the requirements-role header (`Stage stage-01`) appears in the
  output. A crash inside `devteam stage` now fails the step instead of
  silently succeeding behind `|| true`.
- Pinned `devteam next` action check to `run-stage|continue-stage` (the two
  actions reachable from a headless run that wrote no gate), replacing the
  previous six-action whitelist that included `pipeline-complete` and
  `resolve-escalation` (neither reachable from a fresh `init`).

### 7.2.2 — Coverage signal surfacing
- Coverage step now emits a summary block to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` — numbers
  are visible from the PR checks list without opening the raw log.
- New `upload-artifact` step saves `coverage-report.txt` for 14 days per node
  version (non-blocking, `if-no-files-found: ignore`).
- Moved the recorded baseline from a YAML comment into
  `.github/coverage-baseline.json` so updating it produces a visible diff.

### 7.2.3 — a11y-fixer success-path tests
- Added three tests covering `fixA11yBlockers`'s dispatch → re-validation path
  (the reason the module exists), which was untested at 69.7% line coverage:
  - PASS gate: returns `{ status: "PASS", exitCode: 0 }`.
  - FAIL gate with remaining blockers: returns `{ status: "FAIL", exitCode: 1,
    remainingBlockers: [...] }`.
  - No gate written by re-run: returns `{ status: "MISSING", exitCode: 1 }`.

### 7.2.4 — Fix preflight git-hygiene dead code
- Fixed `core/preflight.js`: added `-c` (`--cached`) flag to `git ls-files
  --ignored --exclude-standard`. Without it the command exits 128 on git ≥
  2.27, making the blocker path permanently unreachable on modern git (macOS
  Apple Git ≥ 2.28, Ubuntu default git ≥ 2.27).
- Replaced the two documenting tests (which recorded the broken behavior) with
  three behavioral tests: clean repo passes, committed-then-ignored file fires
  a blocker with a `git rm --cached` suggestion.

- **Fix: `--allow-stage` now accepts comma-separated values.** Previously, `--allow-stage sign-off,deploy` was treated as a single literal stage name and failed to grant the consequence ceiling for either stage. Adding `split: true` to the flag definition makes both `--allow-stage sign-off,deploy` and `--allow-stage sign-off --allow-stage deploy` produce the same result.

- **Fix: build retry recipes now clear `stage-04a` (pre-review lint gate).** `buildGatePaths()` — the shared helper used by six fix recipes to generate clear_gates lists — was omitting `pipeline/gates/stage-04a.json`. When any recipe re-dispatched the build workstream, the driver saw stage-04a still PASS and skipped the pre-review lint check entirely. Lint errors introduced by the build agent during the re-dispatch reached deploy time undetected. Adding `stage-04a.json` to `buildGatePaths()` closes the gap for all callers at once. Resolves #108.

- **Fix: DAG-derived gate invalidation — Phase 5.1 (closes the #109 class generalized).** Fix recipes hand-listed `clear_gates` per stage; when stage-06d cleared stage-04 build gates, stage-05 (peer-review) and stage-06 (QA) PASS gates were left standing — rewritten code re-entered verification without passing peer-review or QA again. New `core/pipeline/invalidation.js` helper `derivedClearGates()` computes which downstream attestation gates to clear by walking the ordered stage list between the cleared root and the failing stage, returning only paths for gate files that actually exist on disk. Recipes now declare only root stage(s); derived clearing adds every intermediate gate automatically. The hand-listed `stage-04a.json` entry removed from `buildGatePaths`. Updated recipes: stage-04a, stage-04c, stage-05 (code-changes path), stage-06, stage-06b (path 2), stage-06d. `_nextImpl` now passes `stageList` and `changeId` to every `diagnose()` call; the helper is changeId-aware for bounded-mode runs (Phase 5.4). 17 new tests in `tests/invalidation.test.js`, including the regression test that failed on `main` before this fix.

- **Fix: `devteam log` no longer shows artifacts from prior pipeline runs.** When a second feature was started with `devteam restart design --cascade`, gate files were cleared but artifact files (ADRs, design-spec, build-plan, etc.) remained on disk with their original timestamps. The journal sorted all pipeline artifacts by mtime, so first-feature artifacts appeared at the head of `devteam log --follow` output alongside the new feature's events — making the timeline confusing and hard to follow. Fixed by anchoring the artifact timeline to `pipeline/brief.md` mtime: artifacts written before the current brief are treated as prior-run artifacts and suppressed. Gates are unaffected (they are always cleared and re-created). When no brief exists yet, no filtering is applied.

- **fix(write-audit):** `snapshotWritables` now uses `git status --porcelain -z` (NUL-delimited) instead of the newline-based format. Git quotes paths that contain spaces or high-byte characters in the newline format; the old `slice(3).trim()` captured the quote characters verbatim, causing false-positive `allowedWrites` violations that flipped PASS gates to FAIL. The `-z` format never quotes paths. Rename/copy entries (two NUL-separated records) are handled with a `skipNext` flag that discards the origin path, consistent with the previous `" -> "` handling. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.1)
- **fix(orchestrator):** `mergeWorkstreamGates` now falls back to the locally-resolved `track` variable (derived from `opts.track` / `custom_stages` / `default_track`) when `wsGates[0].gate.track` is `undefined`. Previously a workstream gate that omitted the `track` field caused the merged gate to ship `track: undefined`, which the validator flagged as an unrecognised track — a gate the orchestrator itself wrote was self-flagged. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.2)
- **fix(tracks):** `core/gates/validator.js` and the `devteam doctor` track check in `bin/devteam` now derive their valid-track sets from the canonical `TRACKS` export in `core/pipeline/stages.js` rather than maintaining independent inline literals. Any track added to `stages.js` automatically propagates to both without a separate manual edit. `VALID_TRACKS` is now exported from `validator.js` for testability. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.5)

- **fix(config):** `assess --apply` now calls `clearConfigCache()` after writing `.devteam/config.yml`. Previously, any `loadConfig()` call in the same process (e.g. a chained command) would return the stale pre-write value from the in-process cache. Also adds a comment in `core/driver.js` documenting the intentional per-run config pinning: the driver loads config once at run start because track, isolation, and changeId are derived from it and baked into `run-state.json`; re-reading mid-loop could silently corrupt an in-progress run. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.3)
- **fix(orchestrator):** The `--workstream` filter is now applied inside `runStage` before prompt rendering, rather than in separate post-render implementations in `runStageHeadless` and `cmdStage`. Both headless and non-headless modes now share a single filter path. The matching rule is documented as "role-prefix match": filter values are bare role names; for fanout stages, all fanout instances of a role (e.g. `stage-05.backend.claude-code` and `stage-05.backend.codex`) are selected together by a single `--workstream backend` value. `roles[]` in the returned object now reflects the filtered set. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.6)
- **fix(pricing):** `mergeWorkstreamGates` now adds a `warnings[]` entry (`"unpriced model X — budget enforcement incomplete"`) when a workstream gate reports token usage for a model not in the pricing table. Previously, budget sums silently treated unpriced models as zero cost. The pricing table remains hand-maintained; this change makes staleness visible without auto-updating it. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.7)

- **6.5.1 — Unpriced-model WARN on single-role stamp path.** `patchGateForUnpricedModel()` added to `core/orchestrator.js` and called in `runStageHeadless` after a single-role gate is written. Single-role stages (e.g. requirements, executable-spec, qa) now emit the same D7 budget-enforcement warning that `mergeWorkstreamGates` has emitted for multi-role stages since v0.6.0. Function exported for direct unit testing; four cases in `tests/orchestrator.test.js`.
- **6.5.2 — `hosts/generic/capabilities.json` declares `goalLoop: false` explicitly.** Closes the v0.6.0 "explicit false, never ambiguous" gap (same principle applied to gemini-cli in Phase 4). `tests/goal-loop.test.js` strengthened: generic assertion is now `strictEqual(false)` instead of the weaker `ok(!caps.goalLoop)`, so a missing key is caught. `docs/reference/hosts.md` regenerated.
- **6.5.3 — codex/gemini-cli adapter dedup.** The two adapters were ~95% identical (165/166 LOC). Shared `installRoles`, `install`, `uninstall`, `status`, and `renderStagePrompt` logic extracted into `core/adapters/markdown-host.js` as `makeMarkdownHostAdapter(capabilities)`. Both adapters delegate to the base; each retains only its own `invoke()`. Deletes the in-file NOTE comments that flagged this dedup as pending since Phase 3. `tests/audit.test.js` updated: the "uses `core/roles.listRoles()`" invariant now checks the shared base. Adapter-contract and install-roundtrip tests confirm byte-identical rendered output.

- **Fix: `devteam init` hook commands are now portable across machines.** The claude-code adapter previously baked absolute paths to Stagecraft core scripts into `.claude/settings.local.json` at init time (e.g. `node "/abs/path/core/gates/validator.js"`), making the file machine-specific and requiring `devteam init` to be re-run after every fresh clone to a different machine or path. Hook commands now use `devteam hook validate`, `devteam hook secret-scan`, and `devteam hook approval-derivation` — resolved at execution time via the installed `devteam` binary. A new `devteam hook <name>` subcommand dispatches to the same underlying scripts with full stdio and exit-code fidelity. `settings.local.json` is now safe to commit or omit from `.gitignore` without consequence; existing projects can regenerate with `devteam init --host claude-code --force`.

- **Fix: `devteam restart <stage>` now also clears archive files for the restarted stage.** Previously, only the live gate JSON files were deleted; archive files under `pipeline/gates/archive/<stage>.attempt-N.json` were left on disk. This caused `detectNoProgress` to compare stale archives from a prior run against a new attempt, firing a false `convergence-exhausted` halt before the driver could apply the fix recipe and clear gates. The restart command now deletes all archive files for every stage it restarts (including cascade targets), making re-runs after a manual restart start with a clean convergence slate.

- **Fix: stage-06b (accessibility-audit) fix recipe now routes correctly.** The previous recipe scanned `noted_for_followup` items across all gate files — including `stage-06b.json` itself — and, when cosmetic advisory IDs (disclosure triangle, `aria-hidden`) matched the A11Y regex, generated a `devteam advise --apply` command with `clear_gates: []`. Applying cosmetic advisories did not resolve the actual color-contrast blockers, so the stage failed again and `detectNoProgress` fired on the second attempt. The updated recipe (1) excludes stage-06b's own gate from the `noted_for_followup` search, (2) adds an explicit path for stages where A11Y blockers name specific source files — clearing and re-dispatching the backend build + audit gates so the agent can actually edit the CSS.

- **Fix: stage-06d fix steps now include `--patch` on targeted workstream build dispatch.** When a build workstream was identifiable from blocker text (e.g. `Fix: src/backend/server.js:10`), the recipe generated `devteam stage build --workstream backend --headless` — without `--patch`. Without patch mode the build agent verifies existing code rather than implementing the fix, so an operator following the fix steps verbatim would see the same verification blockers again. Added `--patch --from verification-beyond-tests` to match the existing else-branch (unidentifiable workstream) behaviour. Note: the autonomous driver still dispatches build without patch context (`runStageHeadless` does not yet accept `patchFrom`); this fixes the human-visible fix steps only.

- **Fix: stage-06d auto-fix now dispatches build before re-running verification.** When `_wsFromText` could not map a verification blocker to a known workstream (e.g. the blocker contained `"Fix: if (value instanceof Date)…"` — a code snippet, not a file path — and files like `hasher.js` didn't match `src/backend/` patterns), `computeFixSteps` fell back to a no-op descriptive step with empty `commands`. `clearGatesFromFixSteps` found only `rm pipeline/gates/stage-06d.json`, so the driver cleared the gate and re-ran the verifier on unchanged code — burning both retry slots without any patch attempt. Fix: replace the no-op else branch with a global build dispatch (`devteam stage build --patch --from verification-beyond-tests`) plus `rm` commands for all four per-area gates and `stage-04.json`, so `next()` routes through build before re-running verification. Tests added for the unidentifiable-workstream and code-snippet Fix: clause cases.

- **refactor(cli):** `bin/devteam` is now a 91-line pure registry dispatcher. All 31 commands (read-only, pipeline, tooling) are extracted into `core/cli/commands/<command>.js` modules, each exporting `{ name, flags, run(positional, flags) }`. The monolith that was ~2,900 lines is gone; every command is independently loadable, testable, and auditable. `core/cli/get-orchestrator.js` provides a shared lazy-loader so commands that need the orchestrator don't incur its ~60 ms cold-load unless invoked. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.1 PR 2)

- **refactor(cli):** Introduce `core/cli/flags.js` — a schema-driven flag parser (`parseFlags(argv, schema)`) that replaces the flat shared `parseFlags` in `bin/devteam`. Each command now declares its own `FLAGS` schema (`{ flagName: { type, key?, split?, description } }`), and unknown flags are rejected per-command rather than via a global catch-all. `generateHelp(commandLine, schema)` produces per-command usage text from the same schema, replacing hand-maintained `Usage:` strings. The Phase-1.4 `--apply` peek-ahead hack dissolves: `assess` declares `apply` as `boolean`, `advise` declares it as `string` — the parser enforces the correct contract for each. `bin/devteam` stays monolithic in this PR; commands move in PR 2. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.1 PR 1)

- **refactor(gates):** extracted gate snapshot/restore logic from the replay command module into `core/gates/replay-backup.js`. The new module provides `snapshotGate`, `restoreFromBackup`, `deleteBackup`, `findLeftoverBackups`, `archiveReplayGate`, and `clearOriginalGate`. Command modules now contain argument handling and output formatting only — all gate file operations are delegated to core. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.1 PR 3)
- **fix(replay):** eliminated the clobber-and-restore race in `devteam replay`. Previously, the headless run overwrote the original workstream gate with only an in-memory restore; a crash between dispatch and restore left the original silently replaced. Fix: the original gate is now snapshotted to `pipeline/gates/.replay-backup/<name>.json` *before* dispatch; restored from disk afterwards; backup deleted on clean success. On next `devteam replay` invocation, leftover backups from a prior crash are detected and reported before any new dispatch begins. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.7.4)

- **refactor(recipes):** replaced the 440-line `computeFixSteps` if-ladder in `core/orchestrator.js` with a per-stage recipe registry in `core/pipeline/fix-recipes.js`. Each stage registers a `diagnose(gate, ctx)` function that returns `{ clear_gates, steps }` directly as data; a single `formatGateClear` formatter derives the human-readable `rm` command strings — no other code generates them. `clearGatesFromFixSteps` (the reverse parse of rm strings back into paths) is deleted; `_nextImpl` calls `getRecipe().diagnose()` directly and the structured `clear_gates` array is attached to every `fix-and-retry` action without post-hoc parsing. `driver.js` updated to consume `clear_gates` exclusively (the `fix_steps` fallback path is removed). orchestrator.js shrank by 521 lines (1481 → 960). (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.2)

- **refactor(markers):** Extracted `upsertSection` and `stripSection` into a new `core/markers.js` module, eliminating three copies of begin/end-marker section logic. The two strip implementations (core/gates/validator.js and bin/devteam) were byte-for-byte identical; no behavioral divergence. The upsert implementation in core/driver.js had a verified bug: inverted markers (end before begin) or a missing end marker caused a duplicate section to be appended. The new `upsertSection` treats corrupt input explicitly — replaces from the begin-marker to EOF and emits a warning — instead of appending. Both functions operate on text strings; callers own file I/O. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.3)

- **refactor(deps):** Moved `@huggingface/transformers` from `dependencies` to `optionalDependencies`. The package is only needed for `devteam memory` with the default local embedding provider; it is never loaded by other commands. `core/memory/embed.js` already lazily `require`d the package — the change adds MODULE_NOT_FOUND detection and a clear actionable error message naming the install command. `devteam doctor` now reports embedding availability as informational (`ℹ`) instead of omitting it or treating absence as a failure. Honest scope note: `npm install` (with no flags) still installs optional dependencies by default, so most users see no change; only `npm install --omit=optional` skips the ~447 MB download. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.4)

- **test(coverage):** Added behavioral test suites for the two previously-untested core modules: `tests/preflight.test.js` (19 tests covering `runGitHygieneCheck`, `runImportPathCheck`, `runDeferredItemsRisk`, and `runPreflight` — gate I/O, track propagation, FAIL/PASS status, skipWrite) and `tests/a11y-fixer.test.js` (21 tests covering `parseBlocker` including malformed-input cases, `buildA11yFixPrompt` across empty/single/multiple/null blockers, and `fixA11yBlockers` error paths — no blockers, missing headless capability, missing headlessCommand, non-zero agent exit). Added a non-blocking `Coverage report (informational)` step to `.github/workflows/test.yml` using `node --test --experimental-test-coverage`; baseline at 3.6 merge: 85.43% lines / 76.02% branches / 82.42% functions. No threshold set. Honest scope note: `runGitHygieneCheck`'s blocker path (committed-but-ignored files) is unreachable on git 2.27+ because `git ls-files --ignored --exclude-standard` now requires `-c` or `-o`; the function always produces a warning instead of a blocker on modern git. (plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md §3.6)

---

## [0.6.0] — 2026-06-11

### Added

- **Split `rules/gates.md` per stage to cut per-invocation token cost** (Phase 2 item 2.3). `rules/gates.md` (~21.5 KB, in every stage's `readFirst`) was the single largest driver of prompt overhead: every agent invocation loaded the full gate reference including 11 stage-specific schemas the agent would never write. The file is now split three ways: (1) `rules/gates-core.md` (~5.5 KB) — the universal contract every agent needs: required fields JSON, status lattice (PASS/WARN/FAIL/ESCALATE), workstream vs. stage gates, `affected_workstreams[]` and `noted_for_followup[]` schemas, retry protocol, tamper-evident chain, track field, validator summary, and failure classification; (2) per-stage `## Gate` sections added to all 14 existing `rules/stage-NN.md` files plus 3 new thin stage files created (`rules/stage-01.md`, `rules/stage-02.md`, `rules/stage-09.md`) — each gate section contains a complete JSON example with all fields matching the `gate:` skeleton in `stages.js`; (3) `rules/gates.md` converted to a one-paragraph tombstone pointing at `gates-core.md` and each per-stage file (retained for one release; external docs may link to it). All 16 `readFirst` arrays in `core/pipeline/stages.js` updated: `.devteam/rules/gates.md` → `.devteam/rules/gates-core.md`. **Token reduction for stage-04:** framework readFirst bytes 32 633 B → 13 039 B (−19 594 B = −19.1 KB). Reference audit: `core/gates/validator.js`, `core/gates/schemas/gate.schema.json`, `rules/pipeline.md`, `rules/pipeline-build.md`, `rules/orchestrator.md`, all `roles/*.md`, and `docs/*.md` updated to cite `gates-core.md` or the per-stage file; CHANGELOG history entries left unchanged. Tests: `tests/contract.test.js` extended with two new suites — (a) every stage `readFirst` contains `gates-core.md` and not the old `gates.md`; (b) every `rules/stage-NN.md` that exists contains a `## Gate` section whose field names match the `gate:` skeleton in `stages.js`. `scripts/consistency.js` `checkRequiredRulesPresent` extended to require both `gates.md` (tombstone) and `gates-core.md`. Suite: 1236 pass (was 1234), lint clean, `npm run consistency` 237 checks pass. *Honest scope note:* The per-stage gate docs are now in stage files that are not yet in any stage's `readFirst`; they serve as human reference and hold the gate examples that tests pin. A follow-on item could add the specific `rules/stage-NN.md` to that stage's `readFirst` if agents need the field-level prose, at the cost of ~1–3 KB per stage.

- **Consistency drift sweep (phase 2.2).** Eliminated all 88 prose-vs-code violations identified by the 2.1 checker, burned the baseline to empty, and deleted `scripts/consistency-baseline.json` so `npm run consistency` runs fully un-baselined. Changes: (1) gate-filename fixes — corrected every dash-form workstream gate reference (`stage-04-backend.json` → `stage-04.backend.json`, `stage-05-{area}.json` → `stage-05.{area}.json`) in roles/backend.md, roles/frontend.md, roles/platform.md, roles/reviewer.md, roles/qa.md, roles/security.md, rules/stage-04.md, rules/stage-04a.md, rules/stage-04b.md, rules/stage-05.md, and rules/gates.md; (2) ghost command surface — replaced all slash-command and `bootstrap.sh` / `npm run review:derive` references in rules/ and roles/ with real `devteam` CLI invocations; (3) missing stage rule files — added rules/stage-04c.md (red-team), rules/stage-04d.md (migration-safety), rules/stage-06d.md (verification-beyond-tests), rules/stage-06e.md (performance-budget); updated rules/pipeline-build.md index to list all 14 build-phase stages; (4) stage/track count fixes — updated 17→18 stage count and 4→6 track count across docs/concepts.md, docs/faq.md, docs/ci.md, docs/TESTING.md, docs/comparative-analysis.md, two walkthroughs, three audit docs, skills/audit/SKILL.md, and rules/gates.md; (5) tracks matrix generator — `scripts/generate-tracks-matrix.js` derives the "What each track runs" matrix in docs/tracks.md from `STAGES_BY_TRACK`; a new `checkTracksMatrixSync` check in consistency.js fails if the committed block is stale; 2 meta-tests added; (6) secondary docs refresh — AGENTS.md (300+→1200+ tests, 11→12 locked decisions, stale backlog blurb replaced), CONTRIBUTING.md (remove "until test suite lands" placeholders), README.md (11→12 locked decisions), docs/TESTING.md (remove stale count, add autonomous-execution suites to inventory), docs/faq.md (3→4 adapters, remove stale test count, fix /goal paragraph), docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md (rewrite "Honest limitations"), docs/runbooks/escalation.md (renumber duplicate §4b to §4c), rules/compaction.md (renumber duplicate items 8/8/9 to 8/9/10), docs/BACKLOG.md (strike E1 devteam summary, E5 devteam doctor as landed); (7) template referers — rules/gates.md, rules/stage-08.md, core/deploy/README.md, core/deploy/docker-compose.md, and core/gates/schemas/stage-01.schema.json updated to point at `templates/` canonical blank forms instead of `docs/` annotation guides; remaining 27 stage-id and 5 ref-existence violations fixed (un-padded stage IDs in runbook headings/anchors, stale `core/skills/` path references, target-project-path clarifications). Suite: 1231 pass, 3 skipped, lint clean.

- **Prose-vs-code consistency checker — six new check classes** (Phase 2 item 2.1, closes plan `phase-2-consistency-and-docs.md`). `scripts/consistency.js` now detects six classes of drift between the prose artifacts (rules/, roles/, docs/, skills/) and the live code (`STAGES`, `TRACKS`, `ORDERED_STAGE_NAMES`, `approval-derivation.js`): (1) **gate-filename** — dash-separated workstream gate names (`stage-04-backend.json`) where the canonical form is dot-separated (`stage-04.backend.json`, per `approval-derivation.js:217`); also catches dash-form template placeholders (`stage-05-{area}.json`); (2) **stage-id** — non-canonical stage IDs (un-padded: `stage-4`, `stage-5`) and IDs not present in `STAGES`; (3) **stage-count** — "N-stage" prose claims that disagree with `ORDERED_STAGE_NAMES.length` (18); (4) **track-list** — "N tracks" count claims that disagree with `TRACKS.length` (6), and enumerated "Valid values:" lists that name some tracks but omit others; (5) **ref-existence** — backtick-path and markdown-link references to `rules/`, `roles/`, `core/`, `skills/`, `docs/`, `.devteam/rules/` that point to files not present in the repo; (6) **stage-rule-file** — stages in STAGES build range (04–08) or indexed in `rules/pipeline-build.md` with no corresponding `rules/stage-NN[x].md`. **Baseline mode** (`scripts/consistency-baseline.json`, 86 entries): pre-existing violations are suppressed (reported but not failing) so CI can enforce the checker immediately; item 2.2 burns the baseline down. Baseline keys are stable (`checkClass:file:identifier`, no line numbers), so unrelated edits don't churn the file. Flags: `--no-baseline` (show raw violations), `--root <path>` (fixture-tree mode, prose checks only), `--json` (machine output). Supports `CONSISTENCY_BASELINE_FILE` env var to inject a fixture baseline in tests. `module.exports = { main }` for programmatic invocation. `tests/consistency-meta.test.js` extended with 23 fixture-tree tests — one violation-detected + clean fixture per check class, plus baseline suppression (exit 0 + "baselined" note) and non-baselined failure (exit 1). Suite: 1227 pass, lint clean, `npm run consistency` exits 0.

- **`next()` is now a pure read — Stage 7 auto-fold write extracted** (closes phase-1-trust-consolidation item 1.2). `core/orchestrator.js` documents `next()` as "Pure read; never mutates state" but `tryAutoFoldSignOff` was writing `pipeline/gates/stage-07.json` as a side effect of asking "what's next?". The auto-fold *feature* is preserved — the **write** is moved to callers. `tryAutoFoldSignOff` is now a pure function returning the folded gate object (or `{ok:false}`) with no `fs.writeFileSync`. `_nextImpl` returns a new `"fold-sign-off"` action carrying `gate_path`, `gate_content`, and `acCount` in the payload. **`cmdNext`** handles `fold-sign-off`: writes the gate, emits a stderr notice, re-runs `next()`, and shows the subsequent action — single-command UX preserved. **`devteam run` (driver)** handles `fold-sign-off`: writes the gate and appends `{"event":"auto-fold-sign-off","derived_from":"brief AC mapping"}` to `run-log.jsonl` — making the fold visible in the audit log (previously invisible). `--allow-stage` is not required for the fold (it is orchestrator-derived from verified AC mapping, not model-asserted). `NEXT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped `1.0 → 1.1` (fold-sign-off is a new action a programmatic consumer must handle). Tests: `tests/auto-fold.test.js` updated (3 old tests) — now assert `next()` leaves `pipeline/gates/` byte-identical, that the payload validates against stage-07 schema required fields, and that callers write via `gate_content`. 4 new tests added: (a) byte-identity gate snapshot, (b) schema field validation, (c) driver writes gate + `run-log.jsonl` event + `onEvent` fires, (d) `cmdNext` writes stage-07.json on fold-sign-off. `tests/pipeline-e2e.test.js` `walkPipeline` updated to handle `fold-sign-off`. `tests/next.test.js` schema_version assertion updated. Total: 1172 pass, lint clean. *Honest scope note:* `cmdNext --json` returns the `fold-sign-off` action shape to programmatic callers; callers that previously received the subsequent action in JSON mode must handle the new action value.

- **Per-attempt gate archiving in the autonomous driver.** When `devteam run` auto-fixes a `code-defect` and is about to clear/overwrite the failed stage gate, it first snapshots it to `pipeline/gates/archive/<stage>.attempt-N.json`. The progression of failed attempts now survives — diff `attempt-1` vs `attempt-2` to see whether blockers were shrinking or stuck. New `core/gates/archive.js` (`archiveGate`, `listArchives`); the driver archives before clearing and records the path on the `fix-retry` run-log event. This is the audit/debug record on its own, and the data a future *progress-based* convergence check would read — but convergence stays **count-based** for now (this layer only archives; no behavior change to retry/escalate). Best-effort (never breaks a run). Tests: `tests/archive.test.js` (snapshot + content preservation + sorted `listArchives`) and a driver test that a retry archives `attempt-1` before clearing; full suite 1153 pass, lint clean. Docs: `docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md`.

- **Structured `clear_gates` on the `fix-and-retry` action.** `devteam next` now attaches `clear_gates` — the repo-relative gate files (e.g. `pipeline/gates/stage-04.backend.json`) a fix recipe wants cleared before re-running, derived once via a shared `clearGatesFromFixSteps` helper in `core/orchestrator.js`. The autonomous driver consumes this structured field directly instead of regex-parsing the recipe's `rm …` command strings (the parse is gone from `core/driver.js` — `extractGateClears` now delegates to the shared helper, kept as a fallback for older action shapes). `clear_gates` also appears in `devteam next --json`, so any programmatic consumer sees the gate-clear intent as data. No behavior change to the recipes themselves; purely makes the intent first-class. Tests: `clearGatesFromFixSteps` unit + `next()` attaches it on a recipe-bearing FAIL (`tests/next.test.js`); driver honors the structured field with no `fix_steps` present (`tests/run.test.js`). Docs: `rules/gates.md`, `README.md`.

- **CI enforces the tamper-evident gate chain** (BACKLOG **F4** + **C6**). The GitHub Actions PR-checks template (`templates/ci/github-actions/stagecraft-pr-checks.yml`, dropped by `devteam ci install`) gains a `devteam verify-chain` step between gate validation and check-run posting. It recomputes the C6 chain on the PR's gates and **fails the build on a break** (a stamped gate whose predecessor changed after chaining — tampering, or an un-re-stamped earlier-stage re-run). Blocking by design — a tamper-evident audit trail is only meaningful if a break fails CI; the step documents how to soften it to advisory (`continue-on-error: true`) for projects that haven't adopted gate chaining. Unstamped/absent chains are reported, not fatal, so adoption is incremental. Tests: `tests/ci.test.js` asserts the step is present; full suite green, lint clean. Docs: `docs/ci.md`.

- **Autonomous-decision authority bound into the tamper-evident chain** (BACKLOG **C6**, PR-D2 — completes C6). When `devteam run --auto-rule` autonomously resolves an escalation, the driver now writes the authority record **onto the escalating gate** as `resolved_by: { authority: "auto-rule:<class>", grant_class, ruling, ts }` — not only to `run-log.jsonl`. Because the gate is hashed by the next downstream stamp, the provenance inherits C6's tamper-evidence: altering "who/what authorized this autonomous decision" after the fact breaks the chain. `devteam verify-chain` (and `--json`) now surfaces a `resolved` list of `{stage, authority, ruling, …}` so an auditor sees, per stage, which autonomous grant resolved it. Optional `resolved_by` object added to `gate.schema.json` (additive). Best-effort: if the applicator cleared the gate to re-run rather than fixing it in place, `run-log.jsonl` still carries the record. Tests: driver writes `resolved_by` onto a real escalating gate (`tests/run.test.js`); `verifyChain` surfaces it (`tests/chain.test.js`); full suite 1148 pass, lint clean. Docs: `rules/gates.md`, design doc (§3.2 — authority-on-gate now landed), `docs/BACKLOG.md` (C6 follow-up closed).

- **Tamper-evident gate chain** (closes BACKLOG **C6**, PR-D1). Recording *what* decided a stage (C4 reproducibility) is only half the audit story — the record must also be tamper-evident. Each **stage-level** gate now carries a `chain` field: `{ prev_stage, prev_hash, algo }`, where `prev_hash` is a **canonical-JSON SHA-256** of the predecessor stage gate's *full* content (including its own `chain` field, so the chain is transitive). Mutating any earlier gate changes its hash, so every gate downstream no longer matches what it recorded — **`devteam verify-chain`** recomputes the chain, locates the break, and exits non-zero (CI-usable); it also flags unstamped stage gates rather than silently passing. New pure module `core/gates/chain.js` (`stampChain`, `verifyChain`, `stampAll`, `canonicalGateHash`). Stamped automatically by the orchestrator: `mergeWorkstreamGates` for multi-role stages, and `runStageHeadless` for single-role stages — **only when the dispatch actually wrote the gate** (pre/post mtime guard, so `devteam replay` against a no-write host stays distinguishable). New **`devteam stamp-chain`** (re)stamps every stage gate in order — used after a deliberate earlier-stage re-run, which legitimately invalidates the chain until re-stamped (documented, not a false alarm). Canonical hashing means key order / whitespace never false-positive; any content change does. Schema: optional `chain` object added to `gate.schema.json` (additive). Makes C4 provenance and the autonomous-run authority records (`--auto-rule`) tamper-evident — the EU AI Act / SOC 2 "who decided this, and was the record altered?" guarantee. Tests: new `tests/chain.test.js` (6 — stamp/genesis, canonical hashing, intact verify, tamper-detect-and-locate, unstamped flagging, re-stamp restore); full suite 1146 pass, lint clean. **PR-D2 follow-up:** bind the `--auto-rule` authority record (`auto-rule:<class>`) onto the chained gate (`resolved_by`) so it inherits the tamper-evidence. Docs alongside: `rules/gates.md`, `README.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`, design doc, `docs/BACKLOG.md` (C6 ✅).

- **`devteam run --auto-rule` — bounded autonomous escalation resolution** (BACKLOG **H2** / Phase 2 **PR-C2** of [ADR-003](docs/adr/003-bounded-autonomous-execution.md) / [autonomous-execution-design.md](docs/autonomous-execution-design.md)). Closes the autonomous loop for *pre-authorized* decision types — with the safety model from the original design intact. **Default behavior is unchanged**: the driver halts on every escalation and doesn't even dispatch the Principal. Opt in per-run with an allowlist of bounded ruling classes — `devteam run --auto-rule formatting-only,doc-only` (**CLI-only**, no config persistence; **allowlist-only**, no wildcard). On an escalation the driver dispatches the Principal (invoking the existing, tested `devteam ruling` / `devteam fix-escalation` commands as subprocesses — injectable for tests), reads the Principal's *newest* output via a new ordered `loadPrincipalOutputs()` in `core/escalation.js`, and: applies a `PRINCIPAL-RULING` whose `[class:]` ∈ the grant (`fix-escalation`) then resumes; **halts** on a `PRINCIPAL-CANNOT-DECIDE` (surfacing `cannot_decide.{reason_class, question}` in the run summary), an ungranted/`unclassified` class, or no output. **Hard stops `--auto-rule` can never cross** (halt even with a matching grant): the consequence ceiling (`sign-off`/`deploy`) and `convergence-exhausted`; and a given escalation is auto-ruled **at most once** (re-escalation → halt). Authority provenance — `grant_class`, the `ruling`, and `authority: auto-rule:<class>` — is recorded to `pipeline/run-log.jsonl` (C6 tamper-evident chaining is a follow-up). New `--auto-rule` flag + `auto-ruled` / `auto-rule-dispatch` / `cannot-decide` progress events. Tests: auto-rule paths in `tests/run.test.js` (granted-apply / cannot-decide / ungranted / ceiling-never / once-only / no-grant-no-dispatch) with injected runners; full suite 1138 pass, lint clean. Honest scope note: the driver invokes the CLI commands rather than calling extracted in-process functions — a clean `core/escalation.js` extraction is a noted follow-up. Docs alongside: `docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` #12, design doc (Phase 2 PR-C2 landed).

- **Typed escalation contract** (BACKLOG **H2** / Phase 2 **PR-C1** of [ADR-003](docs/adr/003-bounded-autonomous-execution.md) / [autonomous-execution-design.md](docs/autonomous-execution-design.md)). Makes the Principal's escalation output *typed*, the prerequisite for any bounded autonomous escalation resolution — and an immediate improvement to the human-driven flow. The Principal now writes ONE of two lines into `pipeline/context.md`: `PRINCIPAL-RULING: <topic> → <decision> [class: <slug>]`, where the optional `[class:]` tags the *kind* of decision (e.g. `formatting-only`, `doc-only`, `known-safe-dependency-bump`) — the category an operator will pre-authorize via `devteam run --auto-rule` (PR-C2); or `PRINCIPAL-CANNOT-DECIDE: <authority|information|value> → <question>` when the decision is underdetermined (commits an ungranted resource, needs a fact outside every artifact, or an unranked value tradeoff — ADR-003 §3.1). A precise cannot-decide is a *correct* outcome that routes the decision to the human who holds the missing authority/information/ranking; it is never auto-resolved. New pure parser module `core/escalation.js` (`parseRulingLine`, `parseCannotDecideLine`, `loadRulings`, `loadCannotDecide`); the ruling prompt (`renderPrincipalRulingPrompt`), `roles/principal.md`, and `rules/escalation.md` carry the contract and the "cannot decide" boundary (rule only what's *derivable*; never inflate a class to fit a grant). `devteam next` now surfaces a `PRINCIPAL-CANNOT-DECIDE` question directly so the operator sees exactly what's needed. **Backward-compatible**: legacy untyped rulings parse as `class: unclassified` and are never auto-applied; no schema migration. **No autonomy change** — escalations still halt for a human (the driver acting on grants is PR-C2). Tests: new `tests/escalation.test.js` (9). Docs alongside: `roles/principal.md`, `rules/escalation.md`, `docs/runbooks/escalation.md`, design doc (Phase 2 PR-C1 landed; PR-C2 scoped).

- **`devteam run` — autonomous fix-and-retry** (BACKLOG **H2**, Phase 1 **PR-B** of [ADR-003](docs/adr/003-bounded-autonomous-execution.md) / [autonomous-execution-design.md](docs/autonomous-execution-design.md)). The driver now **acts** on the machine-fixable cases instead of halting. On a `code-defect` FAIL it clears the failing `pipeline/gates/*` paths the recipe names — extracted in-process from `computeFixSteps`' `rm` steps; the recipe's `devteam stage`/`merge` strings are ignored since the driver's own loop re-dispatches and re-merges — writes the blockers into `pipeline/context.md` under an upserted `<!-- devteam:run-blockers -->` section (cross-stage context so the re-dispatched agent sees *why*), and loops. Bounded by a **driver-side** retry ceiling (`autonomy.max_retries`, default 2) — the authoritative backstop, since `next()`'s `convergence-exhausted` relies on the agent bumping `retry_number`, which the driver doesn't control; on exhaustion it escalates (`resolve-escalation` / `convergence-exhausted`). Adds **`classifyDispatch`** (the H1-deferred dispatch-time classifier, in `core/gates/classify.js`), replacing PR-A's no-progress guard: a dispatch that writes no gate is `transient` (backoff `--retry-delay-ms`, default 30s, then re-dispatch) up to `maxTransientRetries` (default 1), then `structural-input` → halt; a clean exit (code 0) with no gate is structural immediately. Escalations still halt for a human (Phase 2 adds the Principal). New progress events `fix-retry` / `transient-retry` in the log + stderr. Tests: `classifyDispatch` units + fix-retry / transient-recovery / convergence-ceiling / structural / `extractGateClears` cases in `tests/run.test.js`; full suite 1123 pass, lint clean. Honest follow-ups documented: a structured `clear_gates` field on the fix recipe (retire `rm` string-parsing) and gate archiving for progress-based convergence. Docs updated alongside: `docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md`, `README.md`, design doc (Phase 1 PR-B landed), `ARCHITECTURE.md` #12.

- **`devteam run` — bounded autonomous driver, skeleton + happy path** (BACKLOG **H2**, Phase 1 PR-A of [ADR-003](docs/adr/003-bounded-autonomous-execution.md) / [autonomous-execution-design.md](docs/autonomous-execution-design.md)). A deterministic code loop (`core/driver.js`) that advances the pipeline unattended — `next → dispatch → merge` until `pipeline-complete` — and **halts cleanly the moment a human is needed**. The only LLMs in the loop are the workstream agents it dispatches (in-process via `runStageHeadless`, so it gets the structured `{exitCode, gatePath, timedOut}` a later transient/structural classifier needs). Halts on any `fix-and-retry`/`resolve-escalation` (surfacing the H1 `failure_class`), at the **consequence ceiling** — never advances into `sign-off` (stage-07) or `deploy` (stage-08) without an explicit `--allow-stage` grant — on a `--budget-usd` cap (pre-dispatch, summed from merged stage gates to avoid double-counting), at a `--until` boundary, or on a no-gate dispatch (the interim no-progress guard that closes the infinite-loop hole until PR-B's `classifyDispatch` lands). Introduces run-scoped state the otherwise-stateless-within-a-run model avoids: `pipeline/run.lock` (exclusive, with a stale-pid check + `--force`), `pipeline/run-state.json` (resumable via `--resume`), and `pipeline/run-log.jsonl` (one line per transition — the audit + debug trail). Flags: `--track`, `--until`, `--max-iterations` (default 100), `--budget-usd`, `--timeout-ms`, `--allow-stage` (repeatable), `--resume`, `--force`, `--json`. Exit codes: 0 = complete or a clean configured stop (`--until` / ceiling), 1 = needs attention (FAIL / escalation / no-progress / merge-failed / budget / max-iterations), 2 = lock held. **Scoped — does NOT auto-fix or auto-rule yet** (that is PR-B for `code-defect` and Phase 2 for escalations). `run()` accepts injectable `next`/`runStageHeadless`/`mergeWorkstreamGates` so the loop is unit-testable without spawning hosts. Tests: new `tests/run.test.js` (13 — real-`next()` halt paths + injected-dep dispatch loop); full suite 1113 pass, lint clean. Docs alongside: new [docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md](docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md), `README.md` CLI table, design doc (Phase 1 PR-A marked landed; PR-B scoped), and `ARCHITECTURE.md` locked-decision #12 (dispatch-time vs gate-time class split clarified).

- **Typed failure classification on `devteam next`** (BACKLOG **H1**, Phase 0 of [ADR-003](docs/adr/003-bounded-autonomous-execution.md) / [autonomous-execution-design.md](docs/autonomous-execution-design.md)). `next()` historically collapsed every non-pass, non-escalate gate into one `fix-and-retry` action — a corrupt gate file and a failing test got identical handling, and a structural failure that writes no gate could be re-dispatched forever (no `next()`-level circuit breaker). Each non-pass action now carries a **`failure_class`** so the reader (human stage manager today; the autonomous driver later) responds correctly: `code-defect` (change code, re-run), `state-corruption` (gate unreadable — repair it, **don't** re-run), `external-blocked` (a human/external action is required), `judgment-gate` (`status: ESCALATE` — make a ruling), and `convergence-exhausted` (retry budget spent → `next` returns `resolve-escalation` instead of another fix-and-retry). New pure-function module `core/gates/classify.js` (`classifyGate`); the convergence ceiling is a count-based check on the gate's `retry_number` against `autonomy.max_retries` in `.devteam/config.yml` (default 2) — the orchestrator-side backstop for the agent-side self-escalation rule in `rules/gates.md` § Retry Protocol. **Additive and backward-compatible**: the action vocabulary is unchanged (the `halt`/`block` actions and the dispatch-time `transient`/`structural-input` classifier are deferred to the driver in H2, where they have a consumer), and there is **no gate-schema migration** — `failure_class` is derived from existing gate state. `devteam next --json` gains a `schema_version` field (`"1.0"`) for programmatic callers; the human output shows the class as a `[tag]`. Honest limitation documented: true *progress-based* convergence detection (escalate when blocker counts stop decreasing) needs gate archiving and remains a follow-up; today's ceiling is count-based. Tests: new `tests/classify.test.js` (10) + failure-classification / convergence / `--json` cases in `tests/next.test.js`; full suite 1100+ pass, lint clean. Docs updated alongside the code: `rules/gates.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`, `docs/runbooks/fix-and-retry.md`, `docs/runbooks/escalation.md`, `README.md`, and the design doc (§2.5/§2.6 corrected against the as-grounded code; Phase 0 PR-1 marked landed).

- **`devteam consistency analyze` — cross-artifact drift check across the full pipeline chain** (closes BACKLOG **B8** / `cmp-E-1`). Generalizes `devteam spec verify` (which checks brief.md ↔ spec.feature ↔ test-report.md drift) to **every** intermediate artifact + the gate-vs-reality dimension. Five drift classes covered: (1) AC ↔ scenario in spec [delegates to existing G2 logic]; (2) AC ↔ test-report rows [delegates to G2]; (3) **NEW** — AC in brief without a `## Verify` bullet in any `pr-{area}.md`, OR a Verify bullet for an AC not in the brief; (4) **NEW** — red-team `must_address_before_peer_review[]` items that haven't been resolved by stage-05 reaching PASS; (5) **NEW** — gate field claims that drift from the artifact they describe (e.g. `stage-01.acceptance_criteria_count` ≠ actual AC count in brief.md; `stage-06.tests_total` ≠ actual row count in test-report.md). The gate-vs-reality dimension is uniquely possible because Stagecraft has machine-readable gate JSON — no other framework in the 2026 comparative landscape (Spec Kit, BMAD, AI-DLC, OpenSpec, Agent OS, Kiro) can do this check because none has Stagecraft's gate-as-executable-state-seam. New module `core/spec/analyze.js` (~250 lines) with pure-function and file-path wrappers. New `bin/devteam consistency analyze [--strict] [--json]` subcommand renders the report as readable markdown or structured JSON; exits non-zero on any drift. 25 new tests in `tests/cross-artifact-analyze.test.js` (810/810 suite total). README CLI table updated. From the 2026-06-03 comparative analysis (Spec Kit's `/speckit.analyze` was the inspiration).

- **`eslint-plugin-security` enabled** (closes BACKLOG **C7** / `hist-a`). Targeted subset of rules focused on the shell-injection-shape class CodeQL has caught post-merge three times since v0.5.0 (PRs #31, #34, #38). Pre-push catch is the goal. Rules enabled: `detect-child-process`, `detect-eval-with-expression`, `detect-pseudoRandomBytes`, `detect-new-buffer`, `detect-bidi-characters`. Rules deliberately disabled with reasoning preserved in `eslint.config.js`: `detect-unsafe-regex` (flagged 7 bounded line-by-line parsers under operator control — DDL keywords, Gherkin grammar, AC identifiers, YAML config); `detect-non-literal-fs-filename` (Stagecraft legitimately reads dynamic paths); `detect-non-literal-regexp`, `detect-non-literal-require`, `detect-object-injection`, `detect-possible-timing-attacks` (all high false-positive rates in this codebase, none catching a real bug class). The empirical case for adding the plugin was strong (3 CodeQL alerts / week); the empirical case for the disabled rules was weak. Lint suite still 0 violations; test suite still 785/785.

- **`docs/BACKLOG.md` re-prioritized post comparative analysis.** Six new items folded into existing buckets: **B8** cross-artifact consistency analyze (from Spec Kit's `/speckit.analyze`), **B9** bounded workspace deltas (from OpenSpec), **B10** Discover Standards preprocessing (from Agent OS), **C7** `eslint-plugin-security` (from between-cycle observations after 3 CodeQL alerts in one week), **C8** CHANGELOG-per-PR fragments (from recurring `[Unreleased]` merge conflicts), **C9** verify-before-promoting structural enforcement (from the C-1 retraction failure mode), **E9** conversational stage mode (from BMAD's Party Mode). Cross-reference added: G6 (stage shopping) confirmed as the same direction as `cmp-E-3` (adaptive pathways from AI-DLC + BMAD). The stale Top-10 priority queue (all 10 items landed) was replaced with a fresh three-tier queue — top-tier (~30 days, 5 items), mid-tier (~quarter, 5 items), strategic bets (~year, 3 items) — plus 5 consciously-deprioritized items (VS Code extension, more host adapters, documentation gate, ticket/chat integrations, self-modifying pipeline) with reasoning. Item-source tags (`[cmp-E-N]`, `[hist-N]`) make the provenance auditable.

- **`docs/comparative-analysis.md` — Stagecraft vs the AI-dev tooling landscape (2026).** A 300-line positioning document comparing Stagecraft against six adjacent public frameworks: BMAD-METHOD, GitHub Spec Kit, Agent OS, OpenSpec, AWS Kiro, AI-DLC. Synthesized from three independently-written analyses (one generated in Claude Code, two contributed by the operator from external sessions) and verified against current web sources. Includes: (a) a four-school taxonomy of the 2026 AI-dev-framework space, with a complementary 5-layer functional-stack lens; (b) a 10-dimension comparison matrix; (c) per-framework deep-dives; (d) three claims Stagecraft can defend (gate JSON as executable state seam, heterogeneous multi-model dispatch, downstream-of-build coverage); (e) three honest cases where Stagecraft is *not* the best fit; (f) seven evolution opportunities ranked by leverage with effort estimates and a "what Stagecraft should NOT absorb" section; (g) a four-layer composability stack diagram. Linked from README under Reference / extension. Doc-only; no code changes.

- **Between-cycle observations channel in `docs/audit-archive/HISTORY.md`.** Three observations surfaced between audit #2 (2026-06-03) and the next audit, but had nowhere durable to live — they'd be lost from the conversation that produced them. New `## Between-cycle observations` section in HISTORY.md captures them; `skills/audit/SKILL.md` § Phase 3.1 updated to read the section and fold each observation into the new backlog (promoted to a finding with full ratings, or closed-out with a citation). Convention is "observations live with the audit they informed" — at archive time, processed observations move from HISTORY.md into the archived audit's `09-backlog.md` § Project-Specific. First three captured: (a) `eslint-plugin-security` as a P3 candidate — three CodeQL alerts in one week since v0.5.0 suggests the PR 2.1 defer should be revisited; (b) CHANGELOG-per-PR fragments as a P3 — `[Unreleased]` merge conflicts hit 4+ times across Batch 1/2; (c) "verify before promoting" discipline didn't hold even after codification — the C-1 retraction in PR #32 was the same failure mode as the 2026-05-28 audit's S5, suggesting an enforceable mechanism is needed, not just textual guidance.

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **B2 — Performance budget stage (stage-06e).** New `stage-06e`, role `qa`, between observability-gate and sign-off. Checks Lighthouse Web Vitals (LCP/CLS/FID against the brief's targets), bundle size delta (vs. merge-base), and k6/autocannon load-test throughput against configured budgets (`.devteam/config.yml` `performance.budget.*` or fallback `performance.budget.json`). `budget_exceeded: true` → gate FAIL; `skipped_reason` for non-performance-relevant changes (backend-only, config-only, dep-update). `requiredCapabilities: { shell: true }`. Included in `full`, `quick`, `hotfix` tracks; excluded from `nano`, `config-only`, `dep-update`. `skills/performance-budget/SKILL.md` walks seven steps from tool selection through gate writing. `templates/performance-report-template.md`. Stage journal entries embed budget status, checks run, and Lighthouse score. 25 tests in `tests/performance-budget.test.js`. *Honest scope note:* the stage depends on the project having a Lighthouse-compatible frontend and a load-testing entrypoint; non-applicable projects use `skipped_reason` rather than failing. (closes BACKLOG B2)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **B9 — Bounded workspace deltas.** Opt-in isolation mode: when `.devteam/config.yml` sets `pipeline.isolation: bounded`, every in-flight feature's artifacts (prompts, gates, logs) land under `pipeline/changes/<changeId>/` instead of the global `pipeline/`. `changeId` is derived by slugifying `opts.feature`. New `core/paths.js` exports `pipelineRoot`, `gatesDir`, `logsDir`, `prefixPipelineRelative`. `changeIdFromFeature` added to `core/config.js`. `orchestrator.js` computes `ctx.changeId`, threads it into `buildDescriptor` (prefixes `readFirst`/`allowedWrites`/`artifact`), and uses path helpers in `runStageHeadless`/`mergeWorkstreamGates`. `headless.js` routes gate + log files to the bounded directory. `render-helpers.js` writes the bounded gate path into the agent prompt. `validator.js` reads `DEVTEAM_CHANGE_ID` env var to validate gates in the bounded directory. Default `in-place` mode is unchanged — zero impact for existing setups. 35 new tests in `tests/bounded-workspace.test.js`. (closes BACKLOG B9 / cmp-E-2)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **B10 — Discover Standards preprocessing.** New `devteam standards discover [--cwd <dir>] [--json] [--dry-run] [--force]` — pure static analysis that scans a project codebase and writes `docs/project-conventions.md`. Detects: tech stack (JS/TS/Python/Go/Rust via manifest files), module system (ESM/CJS/mixed from `package.json type` + source sampling), file layout (top-level dirs + source subdirs), naming style (kebab/PascalCase/camelCase/snake_case plurality), tooling (TypeScript/ESLint/Prettier/Biome/Husky/EditorConfig), test config (framework, co-location, pattern), and most-used imports (regex-based tally, skips builtins). `--dry-run` prints without writing; `--json` emits the structured discovery result; `--force` overwrites an existing file. `core/standards/discover.js` exports `discover()`, `formatReport()`, and individual detectors for unit testing. 67 tests in `tests/standards-discover.test.js`. Pair with `readFirst` lists and AGENTS.md to inject discovered conventions into agent prompts. (closes BACKLOG B10 / cmp-E-5)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **C1 — Filesystem-level `allowedWrites` enforcement.** Post-hoc write-audit via git-status diff for adapters that lack hook infrastructure (codex, gemini-cli). New `core/guards/write-audit.js` exports `snapshotWritables` (git status before spawn), `auditWrites` (diff before/after, checked against `allowedWrites` patterns), `isAllowed` (exact-file + directory-prefix matching). `runHeadless` snapshots dirty state before the host process starts and diffs after close; unauthorized writes are logged immediately and returned as `writeViolations[]`. Orchestrator patches the gate to `FAIL` and appends violation messages to `blockers[]` when violations are found. `codex` and `gemini-cli` capabilities updated from `prompt-only` to `post-hoc-audit`; `claude-code` keeps `tool-call-time` (hooks already block writes at dispatch time). `render-helpers.js` caption updated to reflect actual enforcement level. 33 tests in `tests/write-audit.test.js`. (closes BACKLOG C1)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **C3 — License compatibility gate.** `stage-04a.schema.json` now requires `license_check_passed` (boolean) and `license_findings[]` (per-package `{ package, license, policy: allowed|warned|denied, note? }`). Default policy table: MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD-*/ISC/CC0-1.0/Unlicense → `allowed`; UNLICENSED/SSPL/BUSL → `warned`; GPL-*/AGPL-*/LGPL-* → `denied`. `roles/platform.md` step 4 replaced with a concrete procedure: license-checker (Node), pip-licenses (Python), cargo-license (Rust) + SPDX classification; fallback advice for missing tools; `.devteam/config.yml license.extra_allowed[]` override for intentional exceptions. `rules/gates-core.md` and `templates/pre-review-template.md` updated with the per-license policy table. Gate skeleton in `stages.js` carries the new fields. (closes BACKLOG C3)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **C5 — Capability-required permissions.** All four adapter `capabilities.json` files now declare `enforces.shell` and `enforces.network` (true for claude-code/codex/gemini-cli; false for generic). Four stages that need shell access to do their work declare `requiredCapabilities: { shell: true }` in `stages.js` (pre-review, qa, verification-beyond-tests, deploy). `assertCapabilities()` in `orchestrator.js` is called at dispatch time for every workstream — headless or interactive — and throws a named `CapabilityError` if the routed host lacks a required capability; the error surfaces as an `ESCALATE` gate with a "host does not enforce shell" message. 31 new tests in `tests/capabilities.test.js`. (closes BACKLOG C5)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **E7 — `/goal` integration for convergence-shaped stages.** `claude-code` and `codex` now declare `capabilities.goalLoop: true`. `build` (stage-04) and `qa` (stage-06) carry a `goalCondition` template in `stages.js` with a `{workstreamId}` placeholder that `buildDescriptor` resolves at dispatch time. `runStageHeadless` prepends `/goal "<condition>"` to the prompt for any workstream whose adapter has `goalLoop: true` and whose stage has a `goalCondition` — instructing the host to loop until the gate passes rather than exiting after one pass. Non-goal stages and non-goal-loop hosts (gemini-cli, generic) are unaffected. `hosts/gemini-cli/capabilities.json` explicitly declares `"goalLoop": false` so absence is never ambiguous. 30 new tests in `tests/goal-loop.test.js`. (closes BACKLOG E7)

- *(entry backfilled post-release)* **G6 — Stage shopping (AI-inferred tracks).** `devteam assess [--description "..."] [--json] [--apply] [files...]` runs rule-based track analysis: description keyword patterns + path/content heuristics (via `security-heuristic.js` and `migration-heuristic.js`) recommend a track with rationale. Priority order: hotfix keywords → dep-update → config-only → nano → quick → full (default). Heuristic overrides: migration-safety-required bumps lighter tracks to full; security-required bumps nano to quick. `--apply` writes `pipeline.custom_stages` to `.devteam/config.yml`; `custom_stages` is consumed by `next()`, `summary()`, and `runStage()` transparently, overriding `default_track`. `orderedStageNamesForTrack` and `isStageInTrack` now accept custom stage arrays. `trackLabel()` serializes array tracks for OTel span attributes. New `core/stage-shopping/assess.js`. 38 new tests in `tests/stage-shopping.test.js`. *Cross-confirmed by comparative analysis:* AI-DLC dynamic pathway selection and BMAD scale-adaptive depth both validate this direction (cmp-E-3). (closes BACKLOG G6)

### Changed

- **Escalation rendering + dispatch consolidated into `core/escalation.js` (in-process).** Pure refactor closing the PR-C2 follow-up. The Principal-ruling and escalation-applicator prompt renderers, the headless dispatch, the raw-ruling-line loader, and topic derivation moved from `bin/devteam` into `core/escalation.js` (`renderPrincipalRulingPrompt`, `renderEscalationApplicatorPrompt`, `dispatchToPrincipal`, `runRuling`, `runFixEscalation`, `loadPrincipalRulingLines`, `deriveTopicFromGate`). `bin/devteam`'s `cmdRuling` / `cmdFixEscalation` now delegate to these (CLI behavior, messages, and exit codes unchanged — `tests/ruling.test.js` stays green). The autonomous driver (`devteam run --auto-rule`) now renders + dispatches the Principal **in-process** instead of spawning `devteam ruling` / `devteam fix-escalation` as subprocesses — one fewer Node process per escalation, and the driver shares the exact code path the CLI exercises. No behavior change, no new flags; full suite 1148 pass, lint clean.

### Fixed

- **PATCH MODE rendering on all four host adapters** (Phase 1 item 1.5). `--patch --from <stage>` is the centerpiece of the fix-and-retry runbook, but the scoping constraint was silently dropped for workstreams dispatched to `codex` or `gemini-cli`: `hosts/codex/adapter.js` and `hosts/gemini-cli/adapter.js` never read `ctx.patchItems`, so a patched workstream routed to either host received no "fix ONLY these items" instruction. **Fix:** extracted the PATCH MODE block rendering from `hosts/claude-code/adapter.js` and `hosts/generic/adapter.js` into `core/adapters/render-helpers.js` as `renderPatchBlock(ctx, lines)` (claude-code wording canonical), and wired all four adapters to call it. Output for `claude-code` and `generic` is byte-identical to before the refactor (verified by SHA-256 hash of the no-patch path). A comment in `codex` and `gemini-cli` notes the ~95% adapter duplication and points to `plans/phase-3-structural-debt.md` for the deferred base-class refactor. **`[verify-first]` goalLoop on gemini-cli:** `hosts/gemini-cli/capabilities.json` was missing the `goalLoop` key. Investigation confirmed the `/goal` directive is a Claude Code session-level feature with no Gemini CLI equivalent; the absence was intentional. Added `"goalLoop": false` explicitly so absence is never ambiguous (the existing goal-loop test already asserted `!caps.goalLoop`; updated the test description and assertion to `strictEqual(caps.goalLoop, false)`). Tests: 9 new cases in `tests/adapter-contract.test.js` — for every host, descriptor WITH `patchItems` renders the PATCH MODE block; descriptor WITHOUT does not; `absent vs empty patchItems` produces identical output for `claude-code` and `generic`. Full suite 1188 pass (was 1179), lint clean.

- **Stoplist enforced on the autonomous path** (Phase 1 item 1.1 — highest severity). The safety stoplist (auth / credentials / PII / payments / migrations / …) was previously checked only in the interactive path (`devteam stage`), leaving `devteam run` able to silently dispatch lighter tracks on sensitive changes unattended — exactly the path where the guard matters most. Two check-points added to `core/driver.js`: (1) **run-start** — before the first loop iteration, after track resolution; (2) **pre-build** — immediately before dispatching `stage-04 (build)`, catching briefs written by the requirements agent after run-start. On a match the driver halts with `halt_action: "stoplist"`, writes a `stoplist-halt` event to `run-log.jsonl`, and surfaces `explainMatches()` output. `full` and `hotfix` tracks bypass by design (unchanged). `--force` opts out (consistent with the interactive path). `STOPLIST_TRACKS` moved from a local constant in `bin/devteam` into `core/guards/stoplist.js` (exported), so both paths share a single source of truth; `gatherCandidates` now also reads `pipeline/brief.md` so the pre-build check can catch content written mid-run. Tests: 3 new cases in `tests/run.test.js` (quick+match→halt, full+match→no-halt, mid-run brief→pre-build halt); full suite 1171 pass, lint clean. Docs: `docs/runbooks/autonomous-run.md` halt-action table. Honest scope note: `--force` bypasses the autonomous stoplist check as it does the interactive one — it is an explicit, auditable operator override.
- **Gate validator: fail closed where it matters** (Phase 1 item 1.3). Three related hardening changes to `core/gates/validator.js`, which underpins the tamper-evident gate chain and CI blocking:
  1. **`--strict` mode** (also activated by `CI=true` env var): the unknown-internal-error path in `runMain()` — previously "treating as PASS" and exiting 0 — now **exits 1** in strict mode, so a validator bug cannot silently green-light the gate chain in CI. Hook-mode (default) **keeps** warn-and-pass (rationale: don't kill an interactive session on a validator defect — that remains correct and intentional), but now **appends a timestamped entry to `pipeline/validator-errors.log`** so unknown errors are discoverable instead of vanishing. The existing ENOENT (exit 0) and HALT_FS_CODES (exit 1) branches are unchanged.
  2. **CI template and `devteam ci install`** now pass `--strict` to the validator invocation in `templates/ci/github-actions/stagecraft-pr-checks.yml`. Deployed CI pipelines re-generated with `devteam ci install --force` pick up this change.
  3. **Escalation sweep ordering replaced**: `listGates()` previously sorted gate files by **filesystem mtime**, which `git checkout`, `touch`, or any CI workspace copy can change without altering gate content — allowing mtime manipulation to flip the "bypassed escalation" verdict. Ordering is now content-derived: **gate `timestamp` field** (written by the orchestrator at gate-write time) is the primary key, with **stage order from `core/pipeline/stages.js`** as a deterministic fallback for equal or absent timestamps. A comment explains why mtime was wrong. *Honest scope note: hook-mode fail-open is preserved by design — this item only makes the CI/strict path fail closed and the hook-mode failures discoverable.*
  Tests: 7 new cases in `tests/gate-validator.test.js` covering hook-mode exit 0 + log write, `--strict` exit 1, `CI=true` exit 1, and an `fs.utimesSync` mtime-manipulation test asserting the bypass verdict no longer changes. Full suite 1175 pass, lint clean, consistency 235 checks passed.

- **`parseFlags` flag-parsing bugs** (Phase 1 item 1.4). Three documented flags were absent from the shared `parseFlags` vocabulary and exited 2 "Unknown flag" whenever used: `--skip-write` (referenced in `cmdPreflight`), `--skip-preflight` (referenced in `cmdStage`), and `--skip-advise` (referenced in `cmdNext`). All three are now registered as booleans with keys exactly matching the existing checks. Additionally, `--apply` was unconditionally value-consuming — `devteam assess --apply --json` swallowed `--json` as the apply-value (losing JSON output) and `--apply` as the final argument set `flags.apply = undefined` (silently skipping the apply). `--apply` is now peek-ahead boolean-or-value: when the next token is absent or starts with `--`, `flags.apply = true` (boolean, no token consumed); otherwise the value string is consumed as before (preserving `devteam advise --apply AC-11=A`). `cmdAdvise` now errors with a clear message when `flags.apply === true` since it requires a selection string. Orphan audit found no further missing flags beyond these four. Tests: 7 new cases in `tests/cli.test.js`; full suite 1168 pass, lint clean.

- **Bounded isolation (B9) read-side wiring complete** (Phase 1 item 1.6). `core/paths.js` cleanly mapped `changeId → pipeline/changes/<id>/…` and dispatch honored it, but the read side hardcoded `path.join(cwd, "pipeline", …)`: `next()` in `core/orchestrator.js` always looked in `pipeline/gates/`; `summary()` did the same; `tryAutoFoldSignOff` resolved `brief.md`/`test-report.md`/`runbook.md` from the global `pipeline/`; and the driver's lock/run-state/run-log/gates/context paths in `core/driver.js` were always global. In bounded mode, dispatch wrote gates under `pipeline/changes/<id>/gates/` while `next()` checked `pipeline/gates/` — so the pipeline appeared permanently "not started". Changes: (1) `next()` now accepts `changeId` (or derives it from `opts.feature` + config isolation), calls `getGatesDir(cwd, changeId)`, and threads `cwd`/`changeId` through to `_nextImpl` and `tryAutoFoldSignOff`; (2) `summary()` same; (3) `tryAutoFoldSignOff` uses `pipelineRoot(cwd, changeId)` for artifact paths; (4) driver path helpers (`lockPath`, `runStatePath`, `runLogPath`, `gatesDir`, `writeRunBlockers`) all accept `changeId`; (5) `run()` derives `changeId` from `opts.feature` + config isolation (same mechanism as `runStageHeadless`), threads it through every helper, and passes `feature` to `_runStageHeadless` so dispatch and read are in the same bounded subtree; (6) `cmdRun` passes `flags.feature` to `run()`; (7) `_merge` call in the driver now passes `changeId`. All other `path.join(cwd, "pipeline"` hits in `core/` carry a `// B9 exemption:` comment explaining why they are changeId-exempt. Tests: 6 new end-to-end tests in `tests/bounded-workspace.test.js` — `next()` advances past a bounded-path gate; `summary()` reports PASS from bounded gates; in-place mode is unaffected by bounded-path gate files; driver writes run-log/run-state under the change root and not under global `pipeline/`; driver with real `next()` completes when all bounded gates are seeded PASS. Full suite 1185 pass (1188 total, 3 skipped for DEVTEAM_HEADLESS_COMMAND), lint clean. *Honest scope note:* The stoplist brief scan in `runStoplistCheck` still reads `pipeline/brief.md` (global path); in bounded mode the brief lives at `pipeline/changes/<id>/brief.md`. This is noted with a `// B9 exemption:` comment; wiring changeId into `checkStoplist` is a small follow-up.

- **Small hardening basket** (Phase 1 item 1.7 — three fixes in one PR). See `plans/phase-1-trust-consolidation.md` item 1.7.
  1. **`summary()` crash on status-less gate** (`core/orchestrator.js`). A valid-JSON gate file missing the `status` field caused `gate.status.toLowerCase()` to throw `TypeError`, crashing `devteam summary` and the UI `/api/state` endpoint. Applied the `(x || "unknown").toLowerCase()` pattern to both the merged-gate path and the `workstreams[]` mapping so status-less entries render as `"unknown"` instead of exploding. Regression tests: gate `{}` and a workstream entry without `status` both yield `state: "unknown"` without throwing.
  2. **Secret-scan bypass scope narrowed from whole-file to per-line** (`core/hooks/secret-scan.js`). The `devteam-allow-secret:` magic comment previously disabled the *entire* scan for any file containing it — including LLM-written content where one comment could silence all findings across hundreds of lines. New behavior: the comment suppresses findings only on its own line and the immediately following line (comment-above-code style). Every suppressed finding is appended as a JSON record `{file, line, reason, ts}` to `pipeline/secret-allowlist.log` for audit. The stderr help text is updated to describe the narrower scope. Regression tests: comment on line 1 + secret on line 30 → fail; comment adjacent to secret → pass + log entry written.
  3. **Budget-cap blind spot closed** (`core/driver.js`). `totalCostUsd()` previously summed only merged stage gates (`stage-NN.json`), leaving per-workstream gate costs (`stage-NN.<role>.json`) invisible to the budget cap until `mergeWorkstreamGates` ran. The fix includes workstream gate costs when no merged gate exists yet for that stage, and uses only the merged gate once it exists — avoiding double-counting (`mergeWorkstreamGates` already rolls workstream costs up into the merged gate's `cost_usd`). Regression tests: workstream gates totalling > cap with no merged gate → budget halt fires; merged gate + workstream gates → no double-count, no spurious halt.
  Full suite 1195 pass, lint clean. *Honest scope note: `appendAllowlistLog` in the secret-scan hook writes relative to `process.cwd()`. In practice the hook is invoked from the project root (standard Claude Code hook contract), so `pipeline/secret-allowlist.log` lands in the right place; a hook invoked from a non-root directory would write to a different location.*

- **Two GitHub code-scanning alerts** that surfaced after the v0.5.0 push.
  - **HIGH (alert #2): `js/incomplete-sanitization` in `tests/audit.test.js:152`.** The test built a regex via `new RegExp(f.replace(/\./g, "\\."))` to confirm the audit skill references each of the 11 phase output files. The escape only handled dots — not backslashes or other regex metacharacters. The filenames are hardcoded literals so the risk was structural rather than active, but the cleanest fix is to drop the regex entirely: `text.includes(f)` does the same check without any escape concerns. Removes the alert class for that call site.
  - **MEDIUM (alert #1): `actions/missing-workflow-permissions` in `.github/workflows/test.yml`.** Added a top-level `permissions: { contents: read }` block. The default `GITHUB_TOKEN` has overly-broad write scope; the test workflow only reads source (lint, tests, consistency check, devteam-doctor smoke test — no issue / PR / package / release writes), so minimal `contents: read` is correct.

- **CHANGELOG fragments — per-PR fragment files replace [Unreleased] direct edits** (closes BACKLOG C8). The `[Unreleased]` section caused 4+ merge conflicts across earlier PRs; per-PR fragment files under `changelog.d/` end the conflict class — each PR owns one file, nothing collides. `scripts/release.js` gains an `assemble <version>` step that reads all `changelog.d/*.md` files (stable alphabetical order) plus any existing `[Unreleased]` content, folds both into the new version section, and deletes the fragment files.
